Imports `S`/`current`, `$`/`el`, `sleep`, `clamp`, `REDUCED` (`util/motion.js`), `albumKey` (`record-art/tiers.js`), `albumTracks` (`library/model.js`), `albumLinerNotes`/`albumCreditRoll`/`openPersonOrArtist` (`library/credits.js`), `thumbURL` (`library/thumbs.js`), `getCrateArtURL` (`crate/tiers.js`), `fmt` (`player/clock.js`), `setQueue` (`player/queue.js`), `play` (`player/transport.js`, a real circular pair — `transport.js` imports `updateGatefoldNowPlaying` back from here, the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`), `runStartSequence` (`shell/start-sequence.js`), `openPlayerViaSheet` (`shell/player-overlay.js`), and `runGatefoldHandoff` (`shell/handoff.js`, a second real circular pair — `handoff.js` imports `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant` back from here) — no `main.js` import at all. `gatefoldSocialControlsHTML`'s `MizikiSocial` calls reach it as the real global (see `src/main.js`'s own entry), so this file is in `eslint.config.js`'s `MizikiSocial`-global block too.

Module-private: `sealedHandoffPlay`, `closeSealedSheetInstant`. Deliberately not folded into `library/sealed.js`: that module stays about the sealed-records *data* (`S.sealed`, `isAlbumSealed`, the persist calls), independent of listening history by design (SEALED spec §2) — this file is the sheet's own UI, which needs `buildGatefoldTracklist`/`gatefoldTrackIndices`/`GF` from `view.js` (`GF` read-only, per rule 7) the same way the gatefold itself does, so it lives alongside it instead. Imports `S`, `el`/`$`, `timeAgoShort` from `storefront.js`, `renderProfile` from `screens.js` (part of the same 3-way cluster), and, since step 06, `wirePrivacyLink` from `./privacy.js` and `getBackupStatus`/`listBackupDevices` from `../sync/backup.js`; since step 08, `legalLinksRow` from `./legal.js` (real, one-directional — `legal.js` doesn't import anything back).

The screen's own backup-status line and copy carry the spec's required exact sentence verbatim: *"Your listening history is backed up privately to your account. Your music and your location never leave this device."* **`wirePrivacyLink` takes `renderProfile` as a parameter rather than importing it from `./screens.js`** — the one deliberate design choice in this file: `screens.js` already has a real circular pair with `storefront.js`/`settings.js` (see above), and routing a fourth edge through `privacy.js` as well, on top of this file's own real import of `sync/backup.js`, was enough additional surface to transitively reach `library/view.js`'s whole web during this step's own test runs (see the step 06 changelog's account of the chain-explosion bugs) — passing the already-imported function in from each of the two call sites (`screens.js`'s `renderProfileHome`, `settings.js`'s `renderSocialSettingsScreen`) avoids the new edge entirely. Imports `el` (`util/dom.js`), `storeSwitch`/`timeAgoShort` (`./storefront.js`), `getBackupStatus`/`collectDownloadData` (`../sync/backup.js`) — no `./screens.js` import, no `main.js` import.
  - `legal.js` (step 08, P3, part A) — the **Privacy Policy** and **Terms of use** screens, linked from the sign-in wizard's first step (`settings.js`), the Account screen (`settings.js`) and the Privacy screen (`privacy.js`): `renderPrivacyPolicyScreen(body)`, `renderTermsScreen(body)`, and `legalLinksRow(renderProfile, before)` — a small "Privacy Policy · Terms of use" footer row, reused by all three call sites. `before`, when given, runs immediately before navigating; only the sign-in wizard's own call site passes one (`closeSocialAuth`, from `settings.js`), since tapping either link there has to close the sign-in modal first — the Account/Privacy call sites just redraw `#profileBody` in place and don't need it. The actual wording lives in `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`/`docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md`, the reviewable source for either page's text — this file is the in-app rendering of the exact same words, kept in sync by hand (there's no markdown renderer in this app, and a build step to generate one from the other would be more moving parts than the two-file-review discipline this project already uses for CLAUDE.md itself), via the same `el()`/`textContent` DOM-building primitives every other screen in the app uses — not because this static, developer-authored text could carry a tag-injection payload (unlike a file's own embedded tags, it can't), but because there's no reason for this one screen to be the sole exception to how the rest of the app builds its DOM. A `LAST_UPDATED` constant drives the "Last updated: …" note both screens open with. Imports `S` (`../state.js`), `el` (`../util/dom.js`) — no import of `screens.js` (kept one-directional, the same `wirePrivacyLink`-style parameter-passing shape `privacy.js` already established, for the same reason: avoiding a fourth edge into `screens.js`'s already-dense circular-import cluster), no `main.js` import.
  - `screens.js` — `persistTrackLastPlayed` (the one `meta` setter `history/played.js`/`history/clear.js` call), `persistProfile`, `formatDisplayName`, `fmtCount` (not a 999/1.2k-style abbreviator — just `n + ' ' + (singular or plural word)`, picking the word by `n === 1`; tested as "the code's actual rules," per the step 6e prompt's own escape valve, not the prompt's own 999/1.2k example), `topTile`, `renderProfile`, `renderProfileHome`, `renderProfileExtras`, `renderTopAlbumsScreen`, `renderTopSongsScreen`, `renderCollectionScreen`. `labels` gained two entries in step 06: `privacy:'Privacy'` and the `socialSettings` tab was relabeled `'Account'` (from `'Settings'`); step 08 (P3, part A) added two more, `privacyPolicy:'Privacy Policy'` and `termsOfUse:'Terms of use'`. `renderProfile()`'s dispatch gained `else if(screen === 'privacy') renderPrivacyScreen(body);` in step 06; `renderProfileHome()` calls `wirePrivacyLink(body, renderProfile)` right after `renderStorefrontSection(body)`, passing itself in for the reason given in `privacy.js`'s own entry above. Since step 08, the dispatch also appends `legalLinksRow(renderProfile)` to the `socialSettings` and `privacy` branches (after their own render call, so the footer sits below each screen's content) and gained two new terminal branches, `else if(screen === 'privacyPolicy') renderPrivacyPolicyScreen(body);` and `else if(screen === 'termsOfUse') renderTermsScreen(body);` — both leaves, nothing dispatches past them. Imports `S`, `el`/`$`, `meta`/`profile` from `storage/repo.js`, `topAlbums`/`topTracks`/`achievementsList`/`crateFilled`/`collectionGroups` from `history/achievements.js`, `metalBadge`/`collectionBadge` from `badges.js`, `renderStorefrontSection` from `storefront.js`, `renderSocialSettingsScreen` from `settings.js`, `openShareSheet` from `share/sheet.js`, `renderPrivacyScreen`/`wirePrivacyLink` from `./privacy.js` (since step 06), and, since step 08, `renderPrivacyPolicyScreen`/`renderTermsScreen`/`legalLinksRow` from `./legal.js` (all real), plus `refreshLibraryTools` back from `main.js` (circular, temporary — see rule 8; `screens.js`'s only `main.js` dependency). Since step 07 (B4), `persistProfile()` also imports `queueAvatarUpload` from `sync/backup.js` (real, one-directional) — called fire-and-forget whenever `S.profile.artBlob` exists, on every save (including a name-only change), since the re-encode/hash in `queueAvatarUpload`'s own flush cycle is what actually dedupes an unchanged picture, not this call site.

Imports `$`/`el`, `storeAwning`/`storeStat`/`storePickTile`/`localAlbumArt`/`timeAgoShort`/`renderStoreError`/`STORE_TIER_LABEL` from `profile/storefront.js`, plus `feedEventText` from `home.js` — a real circular pair (`home.js` imports `openFriendStore` back), documented in each file's own header comment, the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`.

Imports `$`/`el`, `renderOpenStoreCard`/`storeRequestsCard` from `profile/storefront.js`, plus `openFriendStore` from `friend-store.js` (the circular pair above).

  No file under `src/social/screens/` or `src/profile/` other than `profile/screens.js` has any `main.js` import of its own.
  - `demo.js` — `installSocialDemo` only: the fixture data and `MizikiSocial.*` method monkey-patching behind `?social=demo` (dev-only, per CLAUDE.md's own social-config note — must never run in production). Loaded via a **dynamic `import()`** in `main.js`'s `restoreLibrary()`, at the exact point `installSocialDemo()` has always run from, only when the URL has `?social=demo` — this, not a static `import`, is what makes Vite put it in its own chunk (`dist/assets/demo-*.js`, confirmed by build-output inspection) so real users never download it. Imports `S`, `$`, `DB`, `albumKey`/`trackTier`, `albumMetalFor`, `groupBy` — no `main.js` import.
- `src/share/` (step 6d) — the "share image" section, split along its own existing sub-headers. Composed off-screen, not a screenshot of the tab (PROFILE spec §6, §8): each card composer is a pure function of its options, returning a canvas, touching nothing else.
  - `styles.js` — the "style x format system" (Part 2 §2): `SHARE_BG`, `SHARE_FORMATS` (the three formats' pixel dimensions and safe-zone bands), `shareFitText`/`shareFitLines` (auto-shrink/wrap/truncate), `shareSwatchGradient`, `loadImageEl`, `drawCover`, `roundRectPath`, `drawShareWordmark`, `shareGrainTile`, `shareDominantColor`/`hslStr`, `resolveShareStyle` (Night/Cover/Paper → a kit of ink/sub/accent colours plus a `paintBg` painter), `shareChip`. Also `hexMix` and `drawDiagonalWordmark` (the black-square fallback jacket for art-less tracks) — a deliberate judgment call: both sat physically between the two card composers in the pre-step-6d file with no sub-header of their own, and both are used by *both* composers (`hexMix` for the metal-tier disc gradient, `drawDiagonalWordmark` for the fallback jacket), so homing either in just one of `crate-card.js`/`now-playing-card.js` would have made the other import across a card-to-card boundary for no reason — they belong with the rest of the shared style primitives instead. No `S`, no DOM at module top level (only inside functions, once actually called) — no `main.js` import, not even a circular one.
  - `crate-card.js` — the My Crate card (`composeShareImage`): avatar/name/counts, hero = top album, a grid of the next covers, top songs, metals + collection, brand. `opts`: `{style, format, sections}`. Imports `topAlbums`/`topTracks`/`achievementsList` from `history/achievements.js`, `albumKey`/`VARIANT_DEFS` from `record-art/tiers.js`, `METAL_COLORS` from `record-art/metals.js`, the shared primitives above from `styles.js`, and, since step 6e, `fmtCount` from `profile/screens.js` instead of circularly from `main.js` (all real) — no `main.js` import at all, since step 6e.
  - `now-playing-card.js` — the Now Playing card (`composePlayerShareImage`): the record peeking from behind its sleeve (`fillShareDisc`, module-private — same look precedence `applyDiscVariant()` uses on the real platter), kicker + title (auto-shrink to 2 lines) + artist + album·year, a live progress line, spec chips, brand footer. `opts`: `{style, format, data}`, `data` gathered by the caller (`renderPlayerShareCanvas`, in `sheet.js`) from `current()`/`S.pos`/`resolveLook()` — the composer itself touches nothing but its arguments. Imports `albumKey`/`trackIdentityKey`/`VARIANT_DEFS`/`selectVariant` from `record-art/tiers.js`, `albumMetalFor`/`trackMetalFor`/`METAL_COLORS` from `record-art/metals.js`, `fmt` from `player/clock.js`, and the shared primitives from `styles.js` (all real) — no `main.js` import.
  - `sheet.js` — the share sheets' wiring: style/format read from the DOM (the sheet's own chips), everything else (the pixels) comes from the pure composers above. Live preview re-renders are debounced ~100ms so rapid taps don't each trigger a full recompose. `shareSelectedStyle`/`shareSelectedFormat`, `updateShareActionButtons`, `shareDoSave`/`shareDoShare`/`shareDoCopy`, `queueShareRender`/`openShareSheet`/`closeShareSheet`, `queuePlayerShareRender`/`openPlayerShareSheet`/`closePlayerShareSheet`. Imports `composeShareImage` from `crate-card.js`, `composePlayerShareImage` from `now-playing-card.js`, `resolveLook` from `record-art/looks.js` (all real) — no `main.js` import.

  The navigation-shell functions that used to sit under the old "share image" header purely by physical accident (`isLibraryRouteVisible`/`isProfileRouteVisible`, `updateHeaderActionsAvailability`, `updateLibTabsForLayout`, `refreshLibraryTools`, the header search field, the header + menu, and `showRoute`) stayed in `main.js`, now under their own correctly-named header, for step 7's navigation-shell module.
- `src/sync/` (step 03, B2 — pure merge logic; step 06, B3 — the engine that actually wires it in; step 07, B4 — custom-cover/avatar images) — the account-backup merge logic plus the client that calls it. `merge.js` and `backup-doc.js` are still pure functions only, no DOM, no network, no `S` import; `images.js` (new, step 07) is a leaf too. Only `backup.js` touches the network/DOM/IndexedDB. Together they implement the two invariants the B2 doc called out by name: no counter is ever lost or double-counted across devices, and "Clear listening history" wins everywhere once synced — plus, since step 07, "upload once, cache forever" for images (same content, same name, never re-uploaded).
  - `images.js` (step 07, B4) — the one leaf this step adds: `sha256Hex(bytes)` (pure, async — a Blob/ArrayBuffer's SHA-256 as lowercase hex, works identically in a browser and in Node 22+ since the Web Crypto API is global in both, which is what makes it the one unit-testable piece here — `tests/unit/images.test.js`), `prepareCoverUpload(blob)`/`prepareAvatarUpload(blob)` (re-encode through a module-private `cropSquareStepped` — a miniature, duplicated-not-imported copy of `crate/tiers.js`'s own stepped-downscale square crop, kept separate specifically so this file stays a leaf with zero imports of its own — covers: max 1200px @ ~0.85 quality; avatars: 512px square @ ~0.85 — then hash the *re-encoded* bytes, not the source file's, so the upload is named by what actually leaves the device). Re-encoding through canvas strips whatever EXIF/GPS metadata the source photo carried — the file header comment calls this out explicitly, since it matters most for the avatar (the one image in this pipeline that's publicly readable). No `main.js` import, no DOM-free module import either.
  - `backup.js` (step 06, B3; extended step 07, B4 for image upload/download/cleanup) — the sync engine: `scheduleBackupSync(delay)` (debounced ~30s, piggy-backing the same timing `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave()` already uses — called from every place a backed-up field actually changes, not just from `saveMeta()`), `flushBackupSync()` (pushes immediately, no debounce — called on backgrounding/`pagehide` by `src/wiring/lifecycle.js`), `pullBackupOnForeground()` (pulls and merges on launch/foreground, never pushes), `initBackupSync()` (the boot-time hookup — does an initial pull-if-signed-in, then wires a `MizikiSocial.on('status', ...)` listener so sign-in later in the session also triggers the first-sync/restore-summary path), `getBackupStatus()` (`{backedUp, lastSyncAt, lastError}`, for the Account screen's "Backed up · X ago"/"Waiting for connection" line), `listBackupDevices()` (every device's own row, for the Account screen's device list), `reconcileImportedTrack(t)` (called from `library/import.js` on every newly-added track — resolves any pending cross-device playlist/albumOrder/overlay/**cover** entry that was waiting on this exact identity key), `collectDownloadData()`/`collectLocalDownloadData()` (the Download-my-data export, B3 point 5), and a small `onBackup(evt, fn)` event emitter (just one event, `'applied'`, fired after a pull merges new data in). Since step D2, `buildLocalState()`'s own `prefs` field is `serializePrefs(S)` with `theme` deleted from the copy before upload — the one deliberate exception to "prefs syncs as a whole, last-write-wins": `S.theme` is device-only by `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`'s own default, so a pinned appearance choice must never follow the account to a different device the way the rest of `prefs` does.
    - **Step 07's own additions**: `queueCoverUpload(trackId, blob)` (called fire-and-forget from `library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit()` whenever a custom cover is set — re-encodes/hashes via `images.js`, writes `{hash, updatedAt}` onto that track's overlay row, schedules a sync), `queueAvatarUpload(blob)` (same shape, called from `profile/screens.js`'s `persistProfile()` whenever a profile picture exists — re-encodes/hashes, stamps `S.profile.pictureHash`/`touchBackupField('picture')`, persists the profile record), `uploadOne`/`flushPendingImages` (the actual network upload, re-deriving the canonical re-encoded blob+hash fresh from the locally-stored source blob *every* sync cycle rather than persisting a second copy of pre-encoded bytes — deduped only by a lightweight persisted `Set` of already-confirmed `'bucket:hash'` tags, `loadUploadedHashes()`/`persistUploadedHashes()`, so an unchanged image is never re-uploaded), `orphanedFiles(files, referencedHashes, keepNewest)` (pure, exported for its own unit test — covers keep zero unreferenced files, avatars keep the single newest one, per B4 point 5's cleanup rule), `cleanupOrphanedImages`/`listBucketFiles`/`parseHashFromFilename` (the Storage-side half of cleanup, best-effort, wrapped in try/catch), `queueCoverDownload`/`queueAvatarDownload` (download-on-restore — only when a matching local track exists, fetched lazily, written into the local `artwork` store, letting the existing crate-tier/thumbnail code rebuild from there), and `applyCovers` (the per-track-identity-key half of `applyMerged`, mirroring how playlists/overlays already resolve a cross-device claim against whatever's in the library right now).
    - **Never imports `library/view.js`, `record-art/looks.js` or `storage/meta.js`**, even though a successful pull logically wants to re-render the library/refresh the current disc's look, and a profile-picture upload logically wants to flush `S.backupUpdatedAt` the normal way — all three would drag in the single most circularly-connected part of the codebase (`library/view.js`'s seven circular pairs; `record-art/looks.js`'s own circular pair with `history/sessions.js`, which itself reaches `library/thumbs.js` → `library/edit.js` → `library/view.js`'s whole web; `storage/meta.js`'s own chain into `player/background.js` → `player/transport.js` → `library/view.js` again), which is both a real risk for any future module that imports `backup.js` and, concretely, broke several existing unit tests' mocking during steps 06 and 07 until removed (see each step's own changelog entry for its chain-explosion bugs). Instead: `applyMerged()` calls `emit('applied')`, and `main.js` — which already safely imports `renderTracks`/`applyDiscVariant`/`isProfileRouteVisible`/`renderProfile` — is the one listener; and `queueAvatarUpload()` writes `S.backupUpdatedAt` straight to IndexedDB with `meta.put('backupUpdatedAt', S.backupUpdatedAt)` (the already-safely-imported, true-leaf `meta` from `storage/repo.js`) instead of going through `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave()`/`saveMeta()`.
    - **Never awaited from anywhere in the playback path** (refactor rule 2/9's own territory, even though this is a feature PR, not a refactor): `scheduleBackupSync`/`flushBackupSync`/`pullBackupOnForeground` are all synchronous entry points that kick off their own internal `async` work and return immediately — nothing in `player/transport.js`'s `play()`, or anywhere else on the tap-to-play path, calls into this file at all. Same for `queueCoverUpload`/`queueAvatarUpload` — both fire-and-forget, never awaited by their own callers (`library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit()`, `profile/screens.js`'s `persistProfile()`), matching the existing codebase style for background work that must never block a save. The debounce/backoff timers (`BACKOFF = [15000, 30000, 60000, 120000, 300000]`, the same shape and first two values as `miziki-social.js`'s own sync engine) run entirely in the background.
    - **Pending cross-device references** (a playlist/albumOrder item, an overlay, or (step 07) a **cover** that names a track this device doesn't have yet) are kept in a small module-private `B.pending` structure (now `{overlays, playlistItems, albumOrderItems, covers}`), persisted to `meta` — never injected as a bare identity-key string into `S.playlists[].items`/`S.albumOrder[album]`, which the rest of the app assumes only ever hold real local file ids. `reconcileImportedTrack(t)` is what resolves a pending entry (of any of the four kinds) once a matching file actually shows up.
    - Imports: `S`/`touchBackupField` (`../state.js`), `meta`/`overlays`/`artwork`/`profile` (`../storage/repo.js` — `artwork`/`profile` are step 07's own additions, for writing a downloaded cover/avatar into local storage and reading a locally-staged picture back out), `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` (`../storage/prefs.js`), `trackIdentityKey`/`albumKey` (`../record-art/tiers.js`), `idxOf` (`../library/model.js`), `toDoc`/`fromDocs`/`resolveIdentityKey`/`SCHEMA_VERSION` (`./backup-doc.js`), `deleteCrateArtTiers`/`buildCrateTier` (`../crate/tiers.js` — step 07, a safe leaf confirmed DOM-free the same way `images.js` is, for rebuilding crate art once a downloaded cover lands locally), `prepareCoverUpload`/`prepareAvatarUpload` (`./images.js`). Reaches `MizikiSocial` as the real global, never imported (see `src/miziki-social.js`'s own entry), through two tiny defensive accessors (`getSocial()`/`getClient()`) so a test or a signed-out/social-config-empty session never throws trying to call through a missing global — every call site goes through them rather than touching `MizikiSocial` directly.
  - `merge.js` — the generic, doc-shape-agnostic primitives, one per row in the key-inventory table: `sumCounters` (per-device counters, summed), `maxField` (`trackLastPlayed`/`albumLastPlayed`, max), `unionFlags` (`rareUnlocked`, union), `unionEarliest` (`achievements`/`collection`, union by key, earliest earned date wins on overlap), `mergeSealed` (broken anywhere stays broken everywhere), `lastWriteWins` (one winner per *field*, not per whole doc — `playlists`/`albumOrder`/`albumSort`/`albumDisplay`/`trackDisplay`/`albumEdge`/`albumLookPref`/`prefs`/`profileName`/**`picture`** (step 07) each carry their own `{at, value}`, so a later unrelated field never clobbers an earlier one), `mergeOverlays` (per-track-identity-key, last write — edit or revert-tombstone — wins), `mergeCovers` (step 07, B4 — the same per-track-identity-key last-write-wins shape as `mergeOverlays`, kept as its own primitive rather than folded in, since a cover change and a tag change are independent edits with their own timestamps; a `null` hash is the tombstone, winning exactly like any other newer value), `computeHistoryClearedAt`/`freshRows` (the clear-wins-everywhere gate), and `mergeRows(rows)`, the one entry point that composes all of the above over every row for a user — its return now also includes `covers: mergeCovers(rows)` and `lastWrite.picture: lastWriteWins(rows, 'picture')`. No imports at all.
  - `backup-doc.js` — the doc format and the local-state ↔ doc conversion: `SCHEMA_VERSION` (**2** as of step 07 — was 1), `toDoc(localState)`, `fromDocs(ownLocal, remoteRows)`, `resolveIdentityKey(key, tracks)`. `localState` is a plain snapshot shaped like the relevant slice of `S` (not `S` itself), documented in full at the top of the file — this is what keeps the module importable and testable without a browser or a database. The only real import is `trackIdentityKey`/`albumKey` from `record-art/tiers.js`, the same portable keys duplicate detection and the play counts already use — `toDoc` converts `playlists`/`albumOrder` items and `overlays` from local file ids (`name|size|lastModified`, meaningless on another device) to these keys on the way out; an id that doesn't currently resolve to a track (forgotten but kept, per F3) is kept as the bare local id rather than dropped, since there's nothing left to derive an identity key from — not portable to another device, but still reconnects on the *same* device after a reinstall, the same way F3's own id match already does. `fromDocs` builds this device's own fresh doc internally (never trusting a possibly-stale copy of itself inside `remoteRows` — any row sharing `ownLocal`'s own `deviceId` is dropped, and remaining rows are de-duplicated by `deviceId` too, so even a duplicate row from the server can never double-count), then calls `mergeRows`. Its result's `totals.*` are the cross-device grand totals for *display only* — they must never be written back into this device's own per-device counters, or the next upload would double-count what's already synced; its `ownReset` flag is true exactly when another device's clear is newer than this one's, meaning the caller must zero its own local history counters and adopt the newer `historyClearedAt` before the next upload. `resolveIdentityKey` isn't called by `fromDocs` itself — resolving a pending identity-key entry to a real local track has to happen at *apply* time, against whatever's in the library right now, since a resolution computed once at merge time would go stale the moment the next file is imported. **Step 07's own additions**: a new `convertCovers(overlaysRaw, idToKey)` (parallel to, but kept separate from, `convertOverlays` — a cover claim and a tag claim are independent, each with its own timestamp; a whole-overlay revert tombstones the cover too, `{hash: null}`, at the same `revertedAt`; a plain tag-only edit that never touched the cover emits no cover claim at all), feeding a new top-level `covers` array in the doc; and `lastWrite.picture`, using the exact same `lw()` helper every other last-write-wins field already uses. A v1 doc (no `covers`/`picture` at all) still loads without throwing — every reader already treats a missing field as "no claim" by construction (`row.covers || []`, etc.) — see `tests/unit/backup-merge.test.js`'s own schema-v1-compatibility tests.
- `tests/unit/` holds `node:test` unit tests for pure functions extracted out of `main.js` (`tiers.test.js`, `solar.test.js`, `engine.test.js`, `clock.test.js`, `scratch-processor.test.js`, `idb.test.js`, `prefs.test.js`, `repo.test.js`, `library-model.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `tags.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `edit.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `credits.test.js`, `search.test.js`, `metals.test.js`, `sealed.test.js`, `controls.test.js`, `share.test.js`, `profile.test.js`, `meta.test.js`, `shell.test.js`, `crate-geometry.test.js`, `crate-model.test.js`, `launch.test.js`, `library-view.test.js`, `gatefold.test.js`, `backup-merge.test.js`, `sidebar.test.js`), run via `npm run test:unit`. Separate from `tests/client.test.js`, which needs Postgres. `engine.test.js` and `clock.test.js` use Node's module-mocking (`node --experimental-test-module-mocks`, see Commands) to replace whichever real modules in their import chain would otherwise need a browser (`main.js`'s DOM-dependent top-level code) or hang the test process (`clock.js`'s real `setInterval` — see the step 3c changelog entry) — each mocks the *whole* module it needs to avoid pulling in, not just its own direct imports, since `mock.module`/`mock.timers` replace by resolved specifier/global, not per-importer. `scratch-processor.test.js` instead loads that file's source with Node's `vm` module (it has no `export`, so nothing to `import`) into a sandbox stubbing the bare `AudioWorkletProcessor`/`registerProcessor`/`sampleRate` globals its top level needs — see F2. `idb.test.js` tests the real `DB` against `fake-indexeddb` (the second dependency, after Vite — see Commands), giving each test a fresh `IDBFactory` instance assigned to the bare `indexedDB` global so tests can't see each other's databases — see step 4a and "Persistence rules" below for what it protects. `prefs.test.js` needs none of that (`serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` touch no DOM, no database, no `S`-the-global — `serializePrefs` takes a plain object shaped like `S`) — it round-trips a fake prefs object, loads old/partial saved objects missing newer fields, and feeds back wrong types, unlisted enum values and `null`/`undefined` to confirm bad or missing values are left out rather than thrown on — see step 4b. `repo.test.js` uses the same `fake-indexeddb`/fresh-`IDBFactory` approach as `idb.test.js` to round-trip every store through the real interface, confirm `meta.get`'s unwrapping and `meta.thumbs()`'s filtering, and confirm the storage-unavailable case returns the same `false`/`null`/`[]` `DB` itself does — see step 4c. `library-model.test.js` needs none of the above — it imports `model.js` directly, no mocking, and checks `byName`'s case/accent-insensitive natural-number ordering, `trackSort`'s disc/track/title order, `groupBy`'s grouping, and `albumTracks`'s three sort modes (auto/title/manual, including the manual-order append/drop-stale-entries behavior) — see step 5a. `queue.test.js` mocks `main.js`, `transport.js`, `clock.js`, `sleep-timer.js`, `audio/engine.js` and `ui/path-note.js` the same way `engine.test.js` does, to load `queue.js`/`buffers.js` without a browser, then checks `buildOrder`'s shuffle (every index exactly once, the given "first" kept at the front), `reorderQueue`'s shuffle-on/off behavior, `advance`'s repeat off/all/one branches at the end of the queue (using `mock.fn()` on the mocked `play`/`pause`/`seek` to assert which ran), and `ensureBuffer`/`touchLRU`'s LRU eviction (stubbed decoding; the 3-track cap; the currently-playing track is never evicted even when it's the least recently used) — see step 5a. Since step 5e, it also mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a capable stub (`appendChild`, `addEventListener`, cached by selector so, e.g., `openQueueSheet` and a later `$('#queueOverlay')` read the same object) and checks `playNext`/`addToQueue` (insert position, moving rather than duplicating an already-queued track, a no-op on the currently-playing track) and `openQueueSheet`/`closeQueueSheet`'s overlay toggle, run through the same `renderQueueSheet` tree-building the real UI uses. `tags.test.js` builds small synthetic byte fixtures in the test itself (no real music files committed) to check `readTags`/`readHeader`/`readDetails`/`findArtworkBlob` against FLAC and ID3v2 (title/artist/album/track, a UTF-16 text frame, FLAC STREAMINFO's sample rate/channels/bit depth, a FLAC PICTURE block and an ID3 APIC frame, and that a file with neither returns no artwork), that truncated or garbage bytes never throw and fall back to the empty/default result, and `findDuplicateTrack`'s duration tolerance — it needs the same `main.js`/`transport.js`/`clock.js`/`sleep-timer.js`/`audio/engine.js`/`ui/path-note.js` mocking as `queue.test.js` to load `library/import.js` (where `findDuplicateTrack` lives) without a browser, even though that one function only reads `S.tracks` — see step 5b. `history.test.js` mocks `main.js` with the union of what `played.js`/`sessions.js`/`clear.js` need from it (since `played.js` and `clear.js` are loaded for real, and `played.js` itself pulls in the real `sessions.js`), plus `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` (so `clear.js`'s one `$('#route-profile')` call doesn't need a real `document`) — then checks `addPlayedRange`/`playedCoverage` (contiguous merges, a scrub-skip leaving a real gap, overlapping replays not double-counted, the 0.05s joining tolerance), `checkTrackCompletion` (counts once at ≥95% coverage, never twice for the same play-through, never below 95% even at the track's end, never for a zero-duration track), and `clearListeningHistory` against `fake-indexeddb` (full scope wipes every counter and leaves the library/other settings alone; orphaned-only scope drops just the identities no longer in `S.tracks`) — see step 5c. `edit.test.js` mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a tiny stub element (settable `textContent`/`style`, a real `classList`) rather than pulling in a real `document`, plus `main.js` with what `edit.js` needs from it directly, then checks `applyEdit` (writes fields onto the track and into the overlay store; a second edit merges onto the existing overlay rather than replacing it; retagging a sealed album carries the seal to the new album id and persists it, an unsealed one never touches `S.sealed`), `revertTrack` (clears the overlay, restores the embedded tags) and `applyEditWithUndo`/`undoLastEdit` (undo restores the exact prior overlay when one existed, or clears it entirely when there was none) against `fake-indexeddb` — see step 5d. `delete.test.js` needs the same `main.js`/`transport.js`/`clock.js`/`sleep-timer.js`/`audio/engine.js`/`ui/path-note.js` mocking as `queue.test.js`/`tags.test.js`, since `delete.js`'s real `setQueue` import pulls in the same `player/queue.js` → `buffers.js` → `audio/engine.js` chain — it checks the pure helpers (`countForAlbum`/`countForArtist`, `formatBytes`, `totalLibraryBytes`), `deleteTracks` against `fake-indexeddb` (removes only the given tracks; un-seals an album that just lost its last track and persists that, but leaves a still-populated album's seal alone; clears `S.index` when the playing track is deleted; an empty id list is a no-op; playlist/queue entries referencing a deleted track are dropped while others survive by identity, not by stale index), `forgetLibrary` (clears the whole library and every session/metal counter, but keeps achievement/collection thumbnails in `meta`), and `findAllDuplicateGroups` (`src/library/duplicate-scan.js` — pure and import-free beyond `S`/`tiers.js`, tested here alongside `deleteTracks` rather than in a fourth mock-heavy file, since it hands `deleteTracks` its "keep newest only" choice) — see step 5d. `favorites.test.js` mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a tiny stub (for the "pop" animation's `classList`/`offsetWidth`) and `main.js`'s `queueSave`/`renderTracks`/`REDUCED`, then checks `isFavorite`/`toggleFavorite` (creates the system `favorites` playlist on first use; adopts a pre-existing user playlist literally named "Favorites" rather than duplicating it; toggles on and back off; `ensureFavoritesPlaylist()` still runs, but no item is added, for an id no longer in `S.tracks`; reads `S.playlists` by the *current* index, not a remembered id — surviving a reorder is `deleteTracks`' job, not this module's) and `updateFavoriteButtons` — see step 5e. `credits.test.js` mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to the same capable stub `queue.test.js` added (for `renderPersonView`'s/`creditRow`'s tree-building), stubs the bare `document.createTextNode` global directly (`creditNamesCell` calls it without going through `util/dom.js`), and needs the same `main.js`/`transport.js`/`clock.js`/`sleep-timer.js`/`audio/engine.js`/`ui/path-note.js` mocking as `queue.test.js`/`tags.test.js` since `credits.js`'s real `setQueue` import pulls in the same chain — it checks `trackHasCreditsContent` (true for any of: a credit-role bucket, performers, genre/year/label/catalog/isrc, or *any* non-empty comment, even a rip-tool signature — that junk filtering is `renderCreditsSheet`'s job, not this gate's), `openPersonOrArtist` (routes to the artist view for an exact library-artist match, the person view otherwise, and remembers `personReturn` when leaving a non-person view), `creditRow`, `openCreditsSheet`/`closeCreditsSheet`, `updateCreditsButtonForCurrent`, `albumLinerNotes` (longest qualifying comment across the album), `albumCreditRoll` (de-duplicated by role + name across every track), and `rebuildPeopleIndex`/`renderPersonView` (every track a person is credited on, routed through the mocked `songRow`; the "no credits found" fallback for an unknown name) — see step 5e. `search.test.js` needs the same DOM/chain mocking as `credits.test.js` (plus a `document.createElement`/`createDocumentFragment` stub for never a filter), `librarySortComparator`/`sortAlbumGroups` (`added` and `sessions` ordering), `highlightMatches`, and `renderSearchResults`/`renderSearchChips`/`updateSearchChipsVisibility` (the "no matches" fallback, grouped results calling the mocked `songRow`, one chip per field/smart-filter, and chips hiding once the search box has focus and text) — see step 5e. Since step 6b, `clock.test.js`/`queue.test.js`/`tags.test.js`/`history.test.js`/`edit.test.js`/`delete.test.js`/`credits.test.js`/`search.test.js` all needed their `main.js` mocks adjusted the same way: `applyDiscVariant`/`restoreThumbs`/`persistSealedAlbums`/`checkAlbumAchievementAndCollection` stopped being `main.js` exports, so each test now mocks `record-art/looks.js`/`library/thumbs.js`/`library/sealed.js`/`history/achievements.js` directly for whichever of those names its own real chain still needs (loading any of the four for real would reach `library/edit.js` and, through that, `library/sealed.js`/`library/credits.js` again, each still circularly importing a few names from `main.js` — the same "stop the chain at the door" reasoning as the existing `audio/engine.js` mocks, just one layer further out now). `metals.test.js` mocks `library/thumbs.js`'s `restoreThumbs` (so loading `record-art/looks.js` — needed for its `resolveLook` tests — doesn't reach `library/thumbs.js`'s real chain via `history/sessions.js`) and checks `albumMetalFor`/`trackMetalFor` at, just below and just above each threshold in `ALBUM_METAL_THRESHOLDS`/`TRACK_METAL_THRESHOLDS` (read from the exported arrays, not hardcoded numbers, so the test tracks the code's own thresholds), and `resolveLook` (rareUnlocked overrides an earned metal and the plain variant; with no rare unlock an earned metal wins over the variant; a saved look preference wins while still available and falls back cleanly once it isn't) — see step 6b. `sealed.test.js` mocks `main.js`'s `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`/`renderTracks` (sealed.js's own circular import) and `library/thumbs.js`'s `restoreThumbs`/`thumbURL`/`persistable` (reached via `history/achievements.js` → `history/sessions.js`) and checks `isAlbumSealed` (true right after import, false once the sealed-records setting is off), `breakSeal` (clears the seal once, calls both of its side effects; breaking an already-broken seal is a no-op), `restoreSealedAlbums` (grandfathers to "none sealed" with no saved row — no `fake-indexeddb` needed, since `DB.ok` stays false and `meta.get` resolves `null` by the same "storage unavailable" design every other module relies on), and `topAlbums`/`topTracks` from `history/achievements.js` (count order, ties broken by most-recently-played, an orphaned id still getting a display fallback) — see step 6b. Since step 6c, `clock.test.js`'s and `engine.test.js`'s `main.js`/`player/background.js` mocks needed the same kind of adjustment step 6b's mock-chasing did: `bgPosition` and `applyOutputRoute` stopped being `main.js` exports (real imports from `player/background.js` instead), so each test's `main.js` mock dropped that key and a new `player/background.js` mock (or, in `clock.test.js`'s case, letting the real `background.js`/`controls.js` load, since their own dependencies were already satisfied by existing mocks) took its place. `controls.test.js` (step 6c) mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a small stub (`dataset`, `title`, `classList`, matching `queue.test.js`'s shape), `history/sessions.js`'s `invalidateActiveSessionIfAny`, and the same `main.js`/`player/transport.js`/`player/clock.js`/`player/sleep-timer.js`/`ui/path-note.js`/`player/background.js` mocking `queue.test.js` needs (since `controls.js`'s real `advance`/`reorderQueue` import pulls in the real `player/queue.js`) — then checks `cycleRepeat`'s exact cycle order (off → all → one → off, read off the running code rather than assumed), `toggleShuffle` (flips `S.shuffle` both ways while leaving `S.playing`/`S.pos`/`S.index` untouched and never calling `play`/`pause`/`seek`), `setMode` (a fake `S.source`/`S.nodes` confirms Pure routes to `master` and Vinyl to `wobble`), and that `setMode` leaves F1's Pure-rate rule intact (`computeRate()` from `sundown/solar.js` stays exactly `1` in Pure regardless of `S.auto`, and Vinyl still follows the manual rate afterward) — see step 6c. `share.test.js` (step 6d) needs no mocking at all — `src/share/styles.js` has no imports of its own, DOM-free at module top level — and sticks to the two genuinely pure parts of the share-image code, since canvas drawing itself can't run in Node: `SHARE_FORMATS`' per-format pixel dimensions and safe-zone bands, and `resolveShareStyle` returning a valid kit (ink/sub/accent/chip colours plus a `paintBg` function) for every one of the nine style × format pairs — `resolveShareStyle('cover', ...)` without a real DOM `Image` constructor rejects internally and falls through to the Night kit by design, so even the Cover branch is exercised without a browser. There is no separately-testable "pick the hero record / top items" function — `composeShareImage`/`composePlayerShareImage` read `topAlbums()`/`topTracks()` directly inline, woven into the same function that does the canvas drawing — so per the step 6d prompt's own escape valve this isn't tested here; the selection algorithm those two calls use is already covered by `sealed.test.js`'s `topAlbums`/`topTracks` tests. Since step 6e, `clock.test.js`/`queue.test.js`/`history.test.js` each needed one more mock-chain fix of the same shape step 6b's and 6c's did: `persistTrackLastPlayed` (and, for `history.test.js`, `renderProfile` too) stopped being `main.js` exports once `history/played.js`/`history/clear.js` started importing them for real from `src/profile/screens.js` — each test dropped those two keys from its existing `main.js` mock and added a new `mock.module` targeting `src/profile/screens.js` directly instead, so loading the real `played.js`/`clear.js` doesn't pull in the real profile/social chain just to satisfy those two names. `profile.test.js` (step 6e) mocks `util/dom.js`'s `el`/`$` to a tiny stub element (`appendChild`/`childNodes`, matching `favorites.test.js`'s shape) and stubs the bare `document.createElement` global directly (`metalBadge`/`collectionBadge` call it without going through `util/dom.js`), then mocks `profile/storefront.js`, `profile/settings.js`, `share/sheet.js`, `history/achievements.js` and `main.js` (for `refreshLibraryTools`) directly — the same "stop the chain at the door" reasoning as every other mock-heavy file in this suite, since loading any of the first four for real would reach the rest of the profile/social chain (and, through `settings.js`, `social/screens/home.js`) just to read `fmtCount` or the two badge functions. It checks `fmtCount` (singular vs. plural word selection — explicitly not a 999/1.2k-style abbreviator, testing "the code's actual rules" per the step 6e prompt's own escape valve) and `metalBadge`/`collectionBadge` (a rare tier with art uses the art as a background image; a rare tier without art falls back to a plain gradient; a metal tier like gold/bronze uses `metalBackground()`/`variantBackground()` and a capitalized or record-named label; art appends a real `<img>` to the badge) against the real `METAL_COLORS`/`VARIANT_DEFS` tables, so the fixture data used stays in sync with what the app actually ships. `meta.test.js` (step 7a) mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a tiny stub element, `audio/mode.js`'s `setMode`, `audio/engine.js`'s `applyCharacter`, `sundown/location.js`'s `applyVolume`, `player/controls.js`'s `toggleShuffle`/`cycleRepeat`, `player/background.js`'s `bgUpdateUI`/`applyBgAudioSetting` and `main.js`'s `attachLaunchSettingRow` directly, plus the bare `document.querySelectorAll` global — the same "stop the chain at the door" reasoning as every other mock-heavy file in this suite, since `applyPrefs()` (reached via `restoreMeta()` whenever a saved prefs row exists, which `saveMeta()` always writes) calls all of these for real, and `setMode` alone would otherwise need `document.documentElement.style.setProperty`. Against `fake-indexeddb`, it checks the full `saveMeta()`/`restoreMeta()` round trip (playlists, manual album order and album sort survive, read back by id rather than position), a playlist entry whose track no longer exists is dropped rather than thrown on, `queueSave()`'s 600ms debounce (several calls collapse into exactly one `saveMeta()` run, confirmed by spying on `meta.put`'s `'playlists'` calls), and that with storage unavailable all three functions quietly no-op — see step 7a. Since step 7a, `clock.test.js`, `controls.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `engine.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `search.test.js` and `tags.test.js` each needed a mock-chain fix of the same shape step 6b's/6c's/6e's did: `queueSave` stopped being a `main.js` export once `player/background.js`/`sundown/location.js`/`library/search.js`/`library/delete.js`/`library/favorites.js`/`library/import.js` started importing it for real from `src/storage/meta.js` instead — and `storage/meta.js` itself real-chains into `audio/mode.js`/`audio/engine.js`/`sundown/location.js`/`player/controls.js`/`player/background.js` for its own `applyPrefs()` helpers, each of which could in turn reach wide chains (`player/queue.js`, `history/sessions.js`, `library/sealed.js`'s own circular `main.js` import) this suite already stops at the door elsewhere. Each affected test added a direct `mock.module` for `src/storage/meta.js` (providing just `queueSave`) rather than trying to patch every one of those five downstream modules' own mocks. `shell.test.js` (step 7b) stubs `globalThis.window = { matchMedia(){ return {matches:false}; } }` before importing anything — the only file in this suite that needs to, since it's the only one loading the real `src/util/motion.js`, whose `REDUCED` export reads `window.matchMedia` at module load — then checks `easeOutCubic` (the curve's fixed points at 0 and 1, the documented formula at 0.5, monotonic across the range) and `shouldPutAway` (true only when `auto`, `S.sleevePullEnabled` and not reduced-motion all hold; false for a manual skip even with the setting on; false when the setting itself is off), mocking `shell/mini-player.js` and `shell/start-sequence.js` directly so `shell/put-away.js`'s own real imports (reached regardless of which export is actually used, since ES modules evaluate their whole graph) don't pull in `player/background.js`'s wider chain. Since step 7b, eleven *existing* test files (`clock.test.js`, `controls.test.js`, `credits.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `edit.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `profile.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `search.test.js`, `tags.test.js`) needed the same kind of mock-chain fix once more: `updateShellAlbum`/`renderMiniPlayer`/`showRoute`/`closePlayer`/`clearSpinDown`/`runStartSequence`/`openPlayerViaSheet`/`shouldPutAway`/`putAwayInstant`/`runPutAwaySequence`/`REDUCED`/`refreshLibraryTools` stopped being `main.js` exports once the modules each test loads for real started importing them from `src/shell/*.js`/`src/util/motion.js` instead — each test moved the affected names (whichever subset its own chain actually reached) into new `mock.module` calls targeting the specific new module, the same pattern as every prior step's mock-chasing. `crate-geometry.test.js` (step 7c) needs no mocking at all — `src/crate/geometry.js` has no imports of its own — and checks `flowPose`'s centered/undeflected/opaque base case, direction sign following `d`'s sign not its magnitude, the opacity falloff past `|a|=3`, the speed-stretch, `opts.originY`/`opts.spread`, and `flowZIndex`'s floor past `|a|=9`. `crate-model.test.js` (step 7c) stubs `globalThis.window = {devicePixelRatio: 1}` for `crate/constants.js`'s top-level `CRATE_DPR` read and mocks `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave`, then checks `libraryHasGenres`, `ensureCrateModel`'s one-record-per-album grouping and its dirty-flag/group-change rebuild gate, `isNowPlayingAlbum`, `crateRailJumpTarget`'s divider-to-first-record landing, and `persistCrateAnchor`. `launch.test.js` (step 7c) stubs `globalThis.document` for `src/launch/intro.js`'s top-level `--launch-*` CSS custom-property writes and mocks `crate/tiers.js`'s `crateArtCache`/`storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave` directly, then checks `resolveLastPlayedAlbum`'s latest-play-wins-over-most-recently-added fallback and empty-library case, `resolveLastPlayedTrackIndex`'s within-album latest-play/first-track fallback and unknown-album case, `computeFlickPosition`'s exact start/end landing and monotonic increase, `flickSpeed`'s accel/cruise/decel ramp, and `sampleStackAlbums`'s exact-cap sampling with its must-include id always kept. Since step 7c, `clock.test.js`/`delete.test.js`/`history.test.js`/`queue.test.js`/`tags.test.js` each needed a `crate/tiers.js`/`crate/model.js` mock in place of a stale `main.js` one (`invalidateCrateModel`/`deleteCrateArtTiers`/`buildCrateTier`/`healCrateArt` stopped being `main.js` exports), and `meta.test.js` needed a `launch/intro.js` mock in place of its stale `main.js` one (for `attachLaunchSettingRow`) — same "stop the chain at the door" reasoning throughout, since the real `crate/model.js` reads `window.devicePixelRatio` via `crate/constants.js` at module load. `library-view.test.js` (step 7d) mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a capable stub (classList, appendChild, and a real `addEventListener` that records the handler so a test can fire it) and every one of `view.js`'s real-circular-pair siblings (`library/credits.js`, `library/favorites.js`, `library/search.js`, `library/sealed.js`, `library/edit.js`, `library/delete.js`, `crate/view.js`, plus `player/clock.js`/`player/queue.js`/`storage/meta.js`/`storage/repo.js`/`ui/path-note.js`/`shell/header.js`) directly, the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning since none of them are this file's own subject — then checks `enterSelectMode`/`exitSelectMode` (toggle `S.view.editSelecting`, clear the selection), `closeRowMenu`/`openConfirmDelete`/`closeConfirmDelete` (the module-private `rowMenuTrackId`/`confirmDeleteAction` `let`s, exported read-only, set and clear correctly), `renderTracks` (empty library centers the empty state; songs mode renders one row per track; crate mode delegates to the mocked `renderCrate` and skips the songs list), `songRow` (clicking a row calls `setQueue` with that row's own queue/position), and `groupRow` (tapping the row calls the provided `onTap`). Since step 7d, `clock.test.js`, `credits.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `edit.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `sealed.test.js`, `search.test.js` and `tags.test.js` each needed a mock-chain fix of the same shape as every prior step's: `renderTracks` (and, for several of them, `exitSelectMode`/`songRow`/`groupRow` too) stopped being `main.js` exports once `library/edit.js`/`library/credits.js`/`library/favorites.js`/`library/search.js`/`library/sealed.js`/`library/delete.js`/`library/import.js`/`crate/view.js`/`player/transport.js` started importing them for real from `src/library/view.js` instead — and `view.js` itself real-chains widely (reaching, for instance, `shell/put-away.js`'s own `shell/mini-player.js` needs, which some of these tests' existing `mini-player.js` mocks didn't provide), so each affected test added a direct `mock.module` for `src/library/view.js` (providing just the names its own chain needed) rather than trying to patch every downstream module's own mock. `gatefold.test.js` (step 7e) stubs `globalThis.window = { matchMedia(){ return {matches:false}; } }` (for `util/motion.js`'s `REDUCED`, the same stub `shell.test.js` already uses) and `globalThis.document = { documentElement: { style: { setProperty(){} } } }` (for `GATEFOLD_ANIM`'s own CSS-custom-property side effects at module load), then mocks `library/credits.js`, `library/thumbs.js`, `crate/tiers.js`, `player/clock.js`, `player/queue.js`, `player/transport.js`, `shell/start-sequence.js`, `shell/player-overlay.js`, `shell/handoff.js` and `library/sealed.js` directly, the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning — `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js`'s own `buildSealOverlay` import would otherwise reach the real `library/sealed.js`, which reaches the real `library/view.js` (the step 7d hub, seven real circular pairs) just to satisfy one name neither of this file's two tested functions (`gatefoldSideSplits`, `isSealedSheetOpen`) ever touches. It checks `gatefoldSideSplits` (exported for this test, same precedent as `angleDelta` in `scrub.js` — fewer than 4 tracks never splits; `S.albumSides` off never splits regardless of count; a single disc of 4+ splits at the midpoint; multiple discs split by disc, not by midpoint) and `isGatefoldOpen`/`isSealedSheetOpen` (both read `false` with nothing open — the module-private `GF`/`SEALSHEET` state isn't otherwise exercised here, since opening either is a DOM- and `requestAnimationFrame`-driven choreography with no meaningful pure core beyond `gatefoldSideSplits`, the same "not much here is pure" shape `shell.test.js` found for `shell/put-away.js`; the Playwright script covers the open/close/hand-off sequences end to end instead). No *existing* test file needed a mock-chain fix for this step: `GF`/`openGatefold`/`openSealedSheet`/`sealedQuietPlay`/`isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant`/`updateGatefoldNowPlaying`/`updateSealedSheetAfterBreak` were never mocked as plain `main.js` exports by any test in this suite before this step (the gatefold had no test coverage of its own, and the handful of tests that did mock `main.js`'s `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak` — `clock.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `sealed.test.js` — moved that one key to a new `src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` mock, the same "stop the chain at the door" move, since `library/sealed.js`'s import of it is real now instead of circular). `forget-library.test.js` (step 02, F3 spec) is a new file, using the same mocking scaffolding `delete.test.js` already established (plus a `profile/screens.js` mock, for its one `clearListeningHistory` smoke test) — against `fake-indexeddb` and the real `storage/repo.js`, it checks `forgetLibrary()` empties the `tracks` store while `meta`/`sessions`/`achievements`/`collection` and the `overlays`/`artwork` records for an edited track and its custom cover are all untouched; that a stats field forgetLibrary leaves alone in memory persists its *current*, not a stale, value on a later `persistSessionCounts()` call (the write-back bug step 5d flagged, now provably fixed rather than just not-regressed); and, resolving ids through `idxOf` by hand (no real DOM, so no re-render to inspect), that a playlist's ids survive Forget untouched and resolve back to indices once matching tracks reappear in `S.tracks` — fully, and partially with the rest staying recorded rather than dropped. Since step 02, `library-model.test.js`'s two manual-album-order tests, `favorites.test.js`'s two id-vs-index tests, `meta.test.js`'s playlist/album-order round-trip and "entry no longer exists" tests, and `delete.test.js`'s playlist-survives-deletion test were all rewritten for the same id-not-position representation (`S.playlists[].items`/`S.albumOrder[album]`) — `delete.test.js`'s own two old `forgetLibrary` tests moved to the new file outright rather than being patched in place, since the behavior they checked no longer exists. `delete.test.js` and `tags.test.js` also each added `pruneThumbs` to their existing `library/thumbs.js` mock, since `delete.js`'s `forgetLibrary()` (and, transitively, `library/import.js`'s real `deleteTracks` import) needs that export to resolve now. `search.test.js`'s one `is:favorite` test was updated to seed the Favorites playlist with a track id instead of an index, for the same reason. `backup-merge.test.js` (step 03, B2) needs none of the mock-chasing above — `src/sync/merge.js` has no imports at all, and `src/sync/backup-doc.js`'s only import (`record-art/tiers.js`, for `trackIdentityKey`/`albumKey`) is already DOM- and database-free — so every row and local-state fixture in this file is a plain object literal. It checks the counter invariants (sum across devices; three devices merged in every order of four permutations give the same total; repeated/duplicate syncs never inflate it; this device's own `localState` is never mutated and never ends up holding another device's counts), overlay merge (a later revert beats an earlier edit and vice versa; an overlay for a track not currently present is skipped, not thrown on), playlist portability (an item for a track that only exists on another device survives the merge as its identity key; `resolveIdentityKey` finds it once a matching track is "imported"), the history-clear gate (a newer clear on one device zeroes another's merged totals and sets `ownReset`; once that device adopts the new `historyClearedAt`, its next play counts from zero again; achievements/Collection/rareUnlocked from a stale row are excluded the same way; sealed records are untouched by any of this), sealed-record merging (broken anywhere stays broken after merging with a device that still has it sealed), last-write-wins field isolation (a later change to one field never clobbers an earlier change to an unrelated field), robustness (`toDoc`/`fromDocs`/`mergeRows` on empty or old-format input never throw), a round trip with no other devices, and that Forget Library on one device (F3) leaves its stats counting in full once merged — see step 03's own changelog entry for the key-inventory table this file's test names track. Since step 07 (B4), 9 more tests cover the image-backup additions, using the same plain-object-literal fixtures (no new mocking needed, since `mergeCovers`/`convertCovers`/`lastWrite.picture` are all as DOM- and database-free as everything else in this file): `covers` merging by last-write-wins per track identity key (a later cover change beats an earlier one regardless of row order; a revert tombstone, `hash: null`, wins over an earlier cover set the same way an overlay tombstone does; a tag-only edit with no `cover` field makes no cover claim at all; an unresolvable local id is skipped, not crashed on); `picture` as a plain last-write-wins field (a later change on another device wins; an unrelated, later change to a different field never clobbers an earlier picture change — the same per-field isolation test already covers for `prefs`/`albumSort`, just for the new field); and two schema-v1-compatibility tests confirming a doc with neither `covers` nor `picture` at all — the exact shape every pre-step-07 device's last upload would have had — still merges without throwing, with `merged.covers` empty and `merged.lastWrite.picture` reading `null` (this device's own fresh, current-schema doc always contributes a claim, even an unset one, so a v1 remote row simply has nothing to compete with — it never leaves `lastWrite.picture` literally `undefined`) rather than any value the v1 row couldn't have supplied. `sidebar.test.js` (step D3, `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`, new, 4 tests) needs none of the mock-chasing above either — `src/shell/sidebar.js` only touches `S`/`$`/`el`, so this mocks `util/dom.js`'s `$`/`el` to a tiny, DOM-free `fakeHost`/`fakeEl` pair (an object literal collecting whatever `appendChild` is called with, so a test can assert on the resulting children directly) rather than pulling in a real `document`. It checks the empty-library fallback row, that a system playlist (Favorites) is pinned first, the doc's own required test case (a playlist name containing `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` renders as a single `textContent` string, never parsed as markup — the fake host has no `innerHTML` parser to misuse in the first place, so a regression here would show up as the payload going missing, not as a real `<img>` appearing), and that a missing `#sidebarPlaylists` host (not yet in the DOM) is a quiet no-op.
- `src/styles/` holds the CSS, split out of what used to be one inline `<style>` block, one file per section (`00-base.css`, `01-header.css`, …), referenced from `miziki.html` as separate `<link rel="stylesheet">` tags in that same original order. Cascade order matters — that numeric prefix is load order, not importance, and the files must stay in it. `fonts.css` (step 01, no numeric prefix — it's not part of that original cascade, just linked first) holds the self-hosted `@font-face` rules that replaced the Google Fonts CDN link; see `src/fonts/`'s own entry and "Security" below. `21-legal.css` (step 08, P3, part A) is the latest numbered file, appended after `20-friend-store.css` — a small, scoped sheet for the Privacy Policy/Terms of use screens: `#profileBody h2` heading styling (scoped there rather than a generic `h2` rule, since `00-base.css` has no generic element resets anywhere in this app), `.legal-list`/`.legal-list li` for their bullet lists, and `.legal-links`/`.link-btn` for the inline "Privacy Policy · Terms of use" footer row `legal.js`'s `legalLinksRow()` builds. Since step D1 (desktop layout prerequisite — see `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`), `00-base.css`'s `:root` also carries nine new tokens (`--sky3`, `--cream`, `--panel`, `--chrome`, `--raised`, `--field`, `--sel`, `--cool`, `--disc-a`/`--disc-b`/`--disc-c`), each given the exact dark-mode value every literal it replaces already had — this PR added no light values and changed no pixel; see that step's own changelog entry below for the full token list and the two literals it consolidated onto an existing token (`--paper`→`--cream` on five sticker/note backgrounds whose color was always the cream swatch, never the page's own paper tone; `.mini-player`/`.tabbar`'s two slightly different chrome alphas onto the one `--chrome` value). Since step D2, `00-base.css`'s `:root` also sets `color-scheme:dark` (so native controls follow the theme too), and `22-theme.css` is the latest numbered file, appended after `21-legal.css` — the light token set and the Auto/pinned-Dark selector logic; see that step's own changelog entry for the exact three selector blocks and why only the thirteen tokens that actually differ between themes are redeclared there (everything else — `--accent`, `--on-accent`, `--cool`, the disc gradient, the fonts — is left to inherit from bare `:root` on purpose, per the doc's own "only chrome changes" rule). `23-desktop.css` (step D3; extended step D4, D5, D6) is the latest numbered file, appended after `22-theme.css` — the desktop shell itself: the `@media (min-width:1024px)`/`(min-width:1280px)` breakpoint rules that turn the shared tab-bar markup into the sidebar, drop `.wrap`'s 520px cap, and show the sidebar's new playlists-list/Sundown-card content; everything in it sits behind one of those two media queries except `.sidebar{display:contents}`/`.sidebar-extra{display:none}`, which apply unconditionally so the sidebar wrapper is invisible scaffolding below the breakpoint rather than a visible box — see that step's own changelog entry for the full design (including the `order:-1` fix a real flex-ordering bug needed, and why the header's search field/`+` menu needed no new markup at all). Since step D4, the D3-era `.mini-player{display:none !important}` placeholder is gone, replaced by the real 88px player-bar layout (`position:fixed;bottom:0`, full viewport width, `order` on each child for the "now playing / transport+seek / mode+queue" zones) — see that step's own changelog entry for the full design, including why volume is the one mockup zone it leaves out. Since step D5, also carries the crate's own desktop layout: `.crate-layout{display:contents}`/`.crate-detail-panel{display:none}` unconditionally (the same invisible-scaffolding-below-the-breakpoint shape `.sidebar` already established), then, inside the `1024px` block, `--crate-card-size:340px` (overriding `10-crate.css`'s own `278px` default) and the `.crate-layout`/`.crate-detail-panel`/`.crate-detail-art`/`.crate-detail-play` rules for the inline record-detail panel beside the bigger crate — see that step's own changelog entry for why the card's own size lives in a CSS variable rather than a second hardcoded number, and why the detail panel never overlaps the sidebar or the crate stage. Since step D6, also carries Now Playing's own desktop layout (`.player-layout{display:contents}`/`.stage-info{display:contents}`/`.player-side-panel{display:none}` unconditionally, the same scaffolding shape; then, inside the `1024px` block, `--sidebar-width` — a new token read-only outside this file, set to the sidebar's own current width at each of its two breakpoints so `.player-overlay`'s own `left` offset never repeats those numbers a third time — `--platter-size:min(32vw,440px)` overriding `02-stage-platter.css`'s own `278px`-baseline default, the `.player-overlay`/`.player-dismiss`/`.player-overlay .stage`/`.platter-stage`/`.stage-info` rules that confine the overlay to the main area and lay the platter/info/side-panel out in three columns, and the `.player-side-panel`/`.player-side-tabs`/`.player-side-tab`/`.player-side-body` rules for the tabbed panel itself) — see that step's own changelog entry for the full design, including the specificity fight one rule had to be written to actually win, and why `#queueBtn` is hidden there.

  `10-crate.css` (step D5) gained its own new token, `--crate-card-size` (default `278px`, the literal every `.crate-card` rule already used, just named — zero visible change at the phone width this was always implicitly fixed at), read live by both this file's own `.crate-card` sizing rules (`width`/`height`/`margin-left`, the last now `calc(var(--crate-card-size) / -2)` in place of the old fixed `-139px`) and by `crate/view.js`'s own `currentCardSize()` — see that module's updated entry below for how the two meet in the middle.

  `02-stage-platter.css` (step D6) gained the same treatment for the full player's own platter: a new `:root{--platter-size:min(72vw,278px)}` (the exact literal `.platter-stage{width:...}` already used, just named — zero visible change at the phone width this was always implicitly fixed at), with `.platter-stage`'s own `width` now reading it. Nothing else needed to change for the var alone to work — every place in `src/shell/` that measures the platter already does so live via `getBoundingClientRect()` (the morph geometry, the hand-off/put-away/start-sequence ceremonies), never by reading this value directly.
- `src/fonts/` (step 01) holds the self-hosted font files `src/styles/fonts.css`'s `@font-face` rules point at: `antonio-variable.woff2`, `archivo-variable.woff2` (each a single Google-served variable-weight file that already covers every weight this app asks for — 400/600/700 and 400/500/600 respectively — confirmed by diffing Google's own CSS2 API response, which points every static weight at the identical URL per family), and `ibm-plex-mono-400.woff2`/`ibm-plex-mono-500.woff2` (not variable, so two separate files). Only the `latin` Unicode-range subset of each is shipped, not the `latin-ext`/`cyrillic`/`cyrillic-ext`/`vietnamese` subsets Google's own CSS2 API also serves as separate files — a deliberate scope call for this app's own UI text; a song tag in one of those scripts still displays, just in the body font's fallback rather than the display face, the same graceful fallback a missing glyph in a real font always gets. `OFL-antonio.txt`/`OFL-archivo.txt`/`OFL-ibmplexmono.txt` are each family's required license file, fetched from the `google/fonts` GitHub repo unchanged.
- `src/miziki-social.js` is the social client (Supabase). It exposes the `MizikiSocial` global. **This is the source of truth and the only copy** — `miziki.html` loads it directly (as a plain classic `<script src="src/miziki-social.js">`, not `type="module"`, so it keeps executing synchronously in place the same as the inline copy it used to be; see the comment above that tag for why a relative path, not an absolute one — it's also what makes this reference work unchanged under both bases below). Since step 06 (B3), its public API also exposes `getClient()` — a small accessor returning the real supabase-js client instance (or `null` if the social layer is inert), added specifically so `src/sync/backup.js` could reach the one client the social layer already creates instead of constructing a second one of its own, per the step 06 doc's own instruction. Also added: `signOutEverywhere` (`auth.signOut({scope:'global'})`, as opposed to the existing `signOut`'s now-explicit `{scope:'local'}` — previously implicit/default, made explicit so the Account screen's two buttons are provably different, not just differently labeled), `deleteAccount` (calls the `delete_my_account()` RPC from migration 005, then resets local social state and signs out), and `listBlocked` (the Privacy screen's blocked-accounts list). Since step 07 (B4), `deleteAccount` also removes the caller's own files from both Storage buckets *before* calling the RPC, via a new module-private `removeStorageFolder(bucket, uid)` (lists `bucket/<uid>/`, removes every file found, best-effort — wrapped in its own try/catch so a Storage hiccup can never block account deletion itself) called once for `'covers'` and once for `'avatars'`. This has to happen client-side, and before the RPC, because Storage objects have no foreign key to `auth.users` — `delete_my_account()`'s existing cascades (migration 005) can't reach either bucket, and this device's own owner-only RLS policies (migration 006) are what let it list/remove its own files right up until the RPC actually deletes the account out from under it.
- `supabase/migrations/` holds the schema, RPCs, digging list, follow counts, (step 05, B1) the account-backup table, reserved handles, account deletion and a security hardening pass, and (step 07, B4) the two image Storage buckets. Apply them in numeric order. Never edit a migration that has already been applied. Add a new numbered file instead. `005_backup.sql` added `public.library_backup` (one row per device, owner-only RLS — see "Backup: what gets synced" and the step 05 changelog entry below), a `profiles_handle_reserved` trigger, `public.delete_my_account()`, and moved four RLS-helper functions (`is_blocked_between`, `item_visibility`, `effective_visibility`, `can_view`) into a new `private` schema that Supabase's Data API never exposes — every function body that called one of those four by name was re-declared to call the moved name; the RLS policies that reference them didn't need touching, since a policy binds to a function's resolved OID at creation time, not its schema-qualified name. `006_images.sql` added two Storage buckets, `covers` (private, 1 MB limit, path `{user_id}/{sha256}.jpg`, owner-only read/write/delete) and `avatars` (public, 300 KB limit, path `{user_id}/avatar-{sha256}.jpg`, publicly readable but owner-only write) on Supabase's own `storage.buckets`/`storage.objects` tables, plus the four owner-folder RLS policies per bucket (`storage.foldername(name)` splits the path on `/` and drops the last segment, so `(storage.foldername(name))[1]` is exactly the `{user_id}` prefix both paths are keyed by) — no table of its own, since `storage.buckets`/`storage.objects` already exist in every Supabase project. Its own closing comment notes that account-deletion's Storage cleanup is handled client-side (`src/miziki-social.js`'s `deleteAccount()`), not here, since Storage objects have no FK to `auth.users` for `delete_my_account()`'s cascades to reach.
- `tests/client.test.js` holds Node tests for the social client against a real local Postgres (see `tests/supabase_stub.sql`, extended in step 07, B4 with a minimal `storage.buckets`/`storage.objects`/`storage.foldername()` stand-in so `006_images.sql`'s Storage RLS policies can be exercised with real `auth.uid()` semantics the same way every other table's policies already are). The player itself has no automated tests yet.
- `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`/`docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md` (step 08, P3, part A) are the canonical, reviewable source for the in-app Privacy Policy and Terms of use screens (`src/profile/legal.js`'s own entry above has the full in-app content breakdown) — any PR that changes either document's wording must change both the markdown and `legal.js` together, called out at the top of all three files. Both carry `sckhitokiri@gmail.com` as a temporary contact address, noted inline in all four files as a placeholder to be replaced with an admin@ address on Miziki's own domain once one is purchased (see "Deploy previews"/the Part D roadmap item), and both are written from the facts already established elsewhere in this file (the "Backup: what gets synced" and "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" tables, the reserved-handles/account-deletion rules, SEALED/CREDITS spec behavior where it's user-facing) rather than generic boilerplate. Per the task doc's own framing, this is draft text for Ken (not a lawyer) to have reviewed before the App Store, not a final legal document — the PR that adds them is opened "do not merge" for exactly that reason. The minimum age started at 13 per the task doc's own instruction, then was raised to 16 at Ken's own request, to avoid GDPR's under-16 parental-consent rules for EU users.
- `docs/MODERATION.md` (step 08, P3, part B) — how to review reports against accounts in the Supabase dashboard (weekly is fine at this project's scale, sooner if something looks urgent), and the three actions available once one warrants it: clear an avatar/bio, remove a sleeve note, delete an account. Each has the exact SQL (or the equivalent Table Editor click-path) against `public.reports`/`profiles`/`crate_items`/`auth.users`, plus the matching Storage cleanup, since none of the three run through the app's own client-side RLS-gated paths — a moderator acts as the dashboard's own elevated role, which is also why `delete_my_account()` can't just be called on someone else's behalf and a direct `auth.users` delete is used instead. Docs only — no app code, no migration, nothing to build or preview.
- `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md` (step D, desktop layout + Auto/Light/Dark appearance — Oct 13, 2026 handoff doc, authored by Ken) is the verbatim source for the seven-PR desktop-layout plan (D1-D7): a 1024px+ breakpoint responsive mode (left sidebar from the existing tab bar, main area, persistent player bar, a side panel on Now Playing), an Auto/Light/Dark appearance setting driven by CSS custom-property tokens (Auto follows the OS via `prefers-color-scheme`), and the three mockup screens (Library, Now Playing, Crate Profile). Explicitly out of scope for all seven PRs: the native iOS app, Supabase/schema changes, new social features, and tablet widths 768-1023px (which keep the phone layout). Each PR stops and waits for Ken's go-ahead before the next one starts — this isn't a self-driving sequence like steps 01-08 above. All seven are complete (see "After step D7" at the end of this changelog); DA1-DA6 below is the next plan in the same one-PR-at-a-time, Ken-gated style.
- `docs/DAY_ARC.md` (step DA, the day arc — Oct 13, 2026 handoff doc, authored by Ken, same format and gating as `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`) is the verbatim source for the six-PR day-arc plan (DA1-DA6): Sundown stops being a sunset-only effect and becomes one point on a day-long curve computed from real solar events at the user's location — the platter rises back to full speed across Sunrise (DA1), Vinyl's character drifts warmer at night and brighter in the morning around the user's own settings (DA2, Vinyl only — Pure stays untouched, F1), Sundown and Character merge into one four-tab-bar "Sundown" tab with a Day/Sound switch and the sky follows the whole day in both themes (DA3), a new "Nychthemeron" theme option follows sunrise/sunset directly (DA4), the profile learns and can share a per-phase listening-rhythm stat (DA5, migration `007_rhythm.sql`), and the phase a record was played in travels with "now spinning" and share cards (DA6, migration `008_spin_phase.sql`). Six phases bound the day (Night/Dawn/Morning/Midday/Afternoon/Golden hour/Twilight, by solar altitude — dawn/dusk at −6°, sunrise/sunset at −0.833°), fixing three gaps in today's Sundown: night currently ends at midnight rather than at dawn, sun times go stale if the app stays open past midnight, and there's no morning behavior at all. Each PR stops and waits for Ken's go-ahead before the next one starts, same as the D-series. `docs/design/day-arc/` (added alongside this doc, in the same docs-only PR, per the doc's own first instruction) holds the design handoff: nine `.dc.html` design-canvas source files (`Main`, `MorningLight`, `NightPreview`, `Character`, `ProfileSettings`, `SkyPalette`, `DesktopSidebar`, `Rhythm`, `SpunAt` — text, not renderable by opening directly in a browser, per that folder's own `README.md`) plus the README itself, which also carries the full, already-contrast-checked (≥4.5:1) light/dark sky-token table for all eight day-phase anchors and the phase-colour table DA5's rhythm ring uses — every later DA PR's own prompt points back at this README rather than re-deriving either table.
- `vite.config.js` builds `miziki.html` (not `index.html`) as the one entry point, output to `dist/`. `base` reads from the `MIZIKI_BASE` env var, defaulting to `/Miziki/` when unset — the GitHub Actions workflow never sets it, so the real GitHub Pages deploy is unaffected; see "Deploy previews" below for the other value it takes. It also copies `src/miziki-social.js` into `dist/src/miziki-social.js` verbatim — Vite only bundles `type="module"` scripts referenced from HTML, so the plain classic script above needs that explicit copy step or it would 404 in the built app. Since step D2, a second plugin (`copy-theme-boot`) does the exact same thing for `src/theme-boot.js` — the anti-flash head script, also a classic script Vite would otherwise leave untouched.
- `netlify.toml` configures Netlify's PR deploy previews. Production builds (pushes to `main`) are deliberately skipped there — `miziki.netlify.app` no longer updates. See "Deploy previews" below.

## How the app is put together

- **Global state** lives in one object, `S` (audio context, tracks, queue, mode, character settings, sessions, etc.), plus ~89 other top-level variables and ~536 top-level functions that share scope. Expect hidden coupling through `S` and these globals.
- **Audio:** Web Audio graph (`AudioContext`, `decodeAudioData`). There are two paths: **Pure** (reference — the character chain fully disconnected, not just turned down, **and** the playback rate always exactly 1.0; "Pure means untouched") and **Vinyl** (wow & flutter → saturation → top-end softening → bus compression, in that order, **and** Sundown's rate change). Sundown (auto and manual) only ever reaches the recording in Vinyl — `computeRate()` in `src/sundown/solar.js` returns `1` outright when `S.mode === 'pure'`, the one place that rule lives (see F1). Car Mode volume, the sleep-timer fade and the user's own volume still apply in Pure: those are level changes downstream of the recording, not changes to it, so "untouched" doesn't cover them.
- **Scratch (F2):** in Vinyl, with the Character menu's Scratch toggle on (default), spinning the disc by hand plays the real track forward/backward at the hand's speed via an `AudioWorkletNode` (`src/audio/scratch-processor.js`), joining the Vinyl chain at the same point the normal source does. Scratch off, or Pure mode, keeps spin-to-scrub as an invented, silent fast-navigation gesture (as it always was). Scratch is saved in `S.char.scratch` alongside the sliders; a saved-prefs object from before F2 has no such field, and a missing value means On.
- **Car Mode** raises volume with GPS speed. Speed always comes from GPS and never from the accelerometer.
