- **Background playback** is optional and routes through a media element plus `navigator.mediaSession` for lock-screen controls. It works on iPhone today, so don't regress it.
- **Persistence:** IndexedDB database `miziki`, **version 4**, with stores `tracks`, `meta`, `sessions`, `overlays`, `artwork`, `profile`, `achievements` and `collection`. It holds the original files plus tags, so the library survives restarts. If storage is unavailable, every call no-ops and the app runs session-only.
  - **Never rename the database or drop or rename a store.** Users' libraries live there. Schema changes go through a version bump with a non-destructive `onupgradeneeded`.
  - **Any future version bump must come with a new upgrade test in `tests/unit/idb.test.js`** that starts from the previous version's real store layout, with data in it, and confirms every old record survives the upgrade and only the new stores/fields are added. This is the test in the "upgrade keeps data" case that would fail if anyone ever wrote a destructive upgrade — see step 4a.
  - **Known gap (not yet fixed): no `onversionchange` handler.** When a future version ships, an old copy of the app still open in another tab or window would block the upgrade rather than closing out of the way. Fix this when version 5 actually ships, not preemptively.
  - **Every new saved setting goes into both `serializePrefs` and `parsePrefs`** (`src/storage/prefs.js`, step 4b), **gets a default in `S`** (`src/state.js`), **and gets a test in `tests/unit/prefs.test.js`** that an old saved object without it still loads, with that default in place.
  - **`src/history/` is the only place that writes listening counters** (play counts, session counts, the metal-eligible shadow counters, rare unlocks, last-played timestamps — step 5c). Anything elsewhere that needs to touch one of these should go through `played.js`/`sessions.js`/`clear.js`'s exported functions rather than writing `S.trackPlayCounts`/`S.sessionCounts`/etc. directly.
  - **"Forget library" (`forgetLibrary()`, `src/library/delete.js`) removes your songs and keeps your listening stats** (step 02, F3 spec) — it clears only the `tracks` store, crate-art tiers, and thumbnails nothing else still needs; `meta`/`sessions`/`overlays`/`achievements`/`collection` are never touched, so playlists (incl. Favorites), track edits, custom covers, manual order/sort and sealed state all wait and reconnect automatically once a file with the same id is re-added. "Clear listening history" (above) is the one place that resets stats — the two mean clearly different things now, and should stay that way. **Note for account backup (steps 03+):** because Forget library never touches stats or settings, turning it on needs no account prompt and the same `device_id` carries through — there's nothing backup-relevant for it to disturb.
  - IndexedDB is tied to the origin. Moving the app to a new domain (or a native shell) starts users with an empty library unless there is a migration or export path. The same goes for saved prefs in the `meta` store, including toggles like **Background playback** — a fresh origin starts with `S.bgAudio = false` (the default in `state.js`), even if it's on at the usual URL. This is why background/lock-screen playback can look broken on a Netlify preview the first time: the fix is to flip the toggle on there too, not a code change.
- **Backup: what gets synced** (step 03, B2 — merge logic; step 05, B1 — the server-side table; step 06, B3 — the app actually wires it in; step 07, B4 — custom covers and the profile picture, via Supabase **Storage**, not the `library_backup` doc). The client-side merge logic (`src/sync/merge.js`/`backup-doc.js`), the `public.library_backup` table (`supabase/migrations/005_backup.sql`) and the sync engine that calls both (`src/sync/backup.js`) all exist and are live: signed in, a device debounce-uploads changed fields (~30s, piggybacking `storage/meta.js`'s existing `queueSave` timing), flushes immediately on backgrounding (`src/wiring/lifecycle.js`'s `visibilitychange`/`pagehide` handlers), and pulls-and-merges on launch/foreground. Signed out, nothing is ever uploaded — `scheduleBackupSync`/`flushBackupSync` both check for a real Supabase client first and quietly no-op without one. Audio, embedded art, generated crate tiers/thumbnails, and anything that already syncs through the social layer are never backed up — the music stays on the device, even signed in; **a custom cover and the profile picture are the one exception, backed up since step 07, but as real image bytes in Storage, never inside the `doc` blob itself** — see the table below. **Rule: every new saved key goes into this table** (as backed up, device-only, or excluded) when it's added, the same way every new saved *setting* already goes into `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` above. See the step 06 and step 07 changelog entries for exactly how each field below gets its data into (and out of) `S`, and "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" further below for the privacy-policy-facing version of this same table.

  **`library_backup`'s own privacy rule**: one row per `(user_id, device_id)`, owner-only RLS (select/insert/update/delete all require `user_id = auth.uid()`) — no view, feed, RPC or policy anywhere in the schema ever reads another user's row, and `revoke all ... from anon` means a signed-out request can't even try. The server never looks inside `doc` (a `jsonb` blob, capped at 2MB) — every merge rule in the table below is enforced entirely client-side, in `src/sync/merge.js`/`backup-doc.js`, not by anything in Postgres. This is also why two devices can never corrupt each other's data at the row level: each device owns and writes only its own `(user_id, device_id)` row, and reads every row for that `user_id` (its own and every other device's) to merge locally.

  | Data | Class | Merge rule |
  |---|---|---|
  | `trackPlayCounts`, `sessionCounts`, `metalEligibleSessions`, `trackMetalEligiblePlays` | backed up | per-device counters: the total is the sum over devices |
  | `trackLastPlayed`, `albumLastPlayed` | backed up | max |
  | `rareUnlocked`, `achievements`, `collection` | backed up | union; on overlap, the earliest earned date wins |
  | `S.sealed` (sealed records) | backed up | a seal broken on any device stays broken everywhere, independent of any history clear (SEALED spec §2 holds across devices too) |
  | `sessions` store (per-album in-progress play-through) | **excluded, device-only** | this is resume state for one physical play-through within a 4-hour gap on *one* device — merging two devices' in-progress sessions could corrupt the 95%/in-order completion rule itself. Nothing is lost: a session only ever affects anything durable once it *completes*, and that effect (`sessionCounts`) is already a backed-up, per-device-summed counter |
  | `overlays` (track edits, text only) | backed up | last edit wins by timestamp; a revert is a tombstone that also wins by timestamp |
  | `playlists` (incl. Favorites), `albumOrder`, `albumSort`, `albumDisplay`, `trackDisplay`, `albumEdge`, `albumLookPref` | backed up | last write wins, **per field** (not per whole doc — a later change to one of these never clobbers an earlier change to another) |
  | `prefs`, profile display name | backed up | last write wins |
  | a custom cover's own image bytes (step 07, B4 — Supabase **Storage**, `covers` bucket, path `{user_id}/{sha256}.jpg`, private/owner-only; the *claim* — `{hash, updatedAt}` — travels in the `library_backup` doc as `covers[identityKey]`, but the bytes themselves never do) | backed up | last write wins per track identity key, same shape/timestamp as the overlay it rides alongside — a revert is a tombstone (`hash: null`) that wins the same way |
  | the profile picture's own image bytes, which doubles as the public account avatar once signed in (step 07, B4 — Supabase **Storage**, `avatars` bucket, path `{user_id}/avatar-{sha256}.jpg`, **publicly readable**, owner-only write; the claim, `picture: {hash, updatedAt}`, travels in the doc the same way `profileName` does) | backed up | last write wins, **per field**, same shape as `profileName`/`prefs` above |
  | `tracks` (audio, embedded art), `artwork` store's crate-tier/thumbnail blobs, `thumb:*` blobs | **excluded** | embedded art returns with the file; crate tiers and thumbnails are rebuilt locally from whatever art — embedded, custom, or a freshly-downloaded cover — is on the device |
  | social meta (Supabase-side ratings/notes/pins/follows) | **excluded** | already syncs through the social layer |
  | `deviceId` (random UUID, created once per install) | **device-only** | not uploaded as backed-up data; it's the row key (B1) |
  | `theme` (step D2, `docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md` — Auto/Light/Dark) | **device-only** | part of `prefs` locally (`storage/prefs.js`), but stripped out of the copy `sync/backup.js`'s `buildLocalState()` uploads — an appearance choice follows the device, not the account, per that doc's own default |

  Local state the merge logic above depends on — all four now exist, added in step 06 (B3), each in `src/state.js` unless noted:
  - `S.historyClearedAt`: a timestamp, bumped on a *full*-scope "Clear listening history" only (not the orphaned-only scope, which just prunes entries for identities no longer in the library on *this* device — it isn't "I want my stats reset," and forcing it onto other devices could wipe stats they still have every reason to keep). Stamped in `src/history/clear.js`'s full-scope branch, persisted to `meta`.
  - `S.sealedBroken`, a `Set` of album ids ever sealed-then-broken on this device — the durable "ever broken" record `src/library/sealed.js`'s `breakSeal()` was missing before this step (it only ever removed an id from `S.sealed`, with nothing recording it was *ever* sealed) — which `src/sync/merge.js`'s "broken anywhere stays broken" rule needs to tell "broken" apart from "this device never had the album at all." Persisted via a new `persistBrokenSeals()` alongside the existing `persistSealedAlbums()`.
  - `S.backupUpdatedAt`, one timestamp per backed-up per-field-last-write-wins key (`playlists`, `albumOrder`, `albumSort`, `albumDisplay`, `trackDisplay`, `albumEdge`, `albumLookPref`, `prefs`, `profileName`, and, since step 07, `picture`) — stamped by `touchBackupField(name)` (`src/state.js`) at each field's own real mutation site: `src/library/view.js` (playlist/album-order edits), `src/library/favorites.js` (`toggleFavorite`), `src/library/delete.js` (`deleteTracks`' playlist/order filtering), `src/library/thumbs.js` (`persistAlbumDisplay`/`persistTrackDisplay`/`persistAlbumEdge`), `src/history/sessions.js` (`persistAlbumLookPref`), `src/profile/screens.js` (`persistProfile`), `src/sync/backup.js`'s own `queueAvatarUpload` (step 07, for `picture`), and `src/storage/meta.js`'s own `saveMeta()` for `prefs`.
  - `S.profile.pictureHash` (step 07, B4, `src/state.js`) — the profile picture's own re-encoded SHA-256, `null` until a picture has ever been uploaded or downloaded on this device; set by `src/sync/backup.js`'s `queueAvatarUpload`/`queueAvatarDownload`, persisted alongside the rest of the profile record (`storage/repo.js`'s `profile` store).
  - a timestamp on each overlay write, and on each revert: `src/library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit()` now stores `editedAt: Date.now()` alongside the edited fields, and `revertTrack()`/`undoLastEdit()`'s "no prior overlay" branch now writes a tombstone (`{id, fields: null, reverted: true, revertedAt: Date.now()}`, and since step 07 also `cover: null` in the same tombstone — a revert clears the backed-up cover claim the same way it clears the text fields) instead of deleting the overlay row outright — `loadOverlayFor()`/`applyEdit()` already treat a falsy `fields` as "nothing to apply," so this is a pure addition to the row's persisted shape, not a behavior change for local-only use, and it's exactly the `{id, fields, editedAt, reverted, revertedAt}` shape `backup-doc.js`'s overlay conversion already expected.

  `src/sync/merge.js` and `src/sync/backup-doc.js` are still pure functions over a `localState` snapshot shaped like the above; `src/sync/backup.js` (step 06) is what builds that snapshot from the real `S`/IndexedDB, calls `toDoc`/`fromDocs`, and reads/writes the real `library_backup` row — see that file's own repo-layout entry and the Step 06 changelog entry below for exactly how.
- **Social config:** since step 04, `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL`/`SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` (`src/state.js`) point at the real Supabase project (`https://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co`, the publishable key) — the social layer is live, not inert. `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` holds Supabase's newer *publishable* key (`sb_publishable_…`), not the legacy JWT-shaped anon key Supabase retires at the end of 2026; the **secret key** (`sb_secret_…`) must never go in this repo. supabase-js is bundled (step 01 — see "Security" below), not CDN-loaded; the publishable key is safe in client code because RLS is the real protection.
- **Reserved handles** (step 05, B1): `admin`, `administrator`, `support`, `help`, `miziki`, `official`, `security`, `moderator`, `mod`, `staff`, `team`, `root`, `system`, `null`, `undefined` can never be claimed — enforced in two places that must stay in sync: a client-side pre-check (`RESERVED_HANDLES` in `src/miziki-social.js`, checked by `checkHandle`/`createProfile`/`updateProfile` before any request goes out) and the real enforcement, a `before insert or update of handle` trigger on `public.profiles` in `supabase/migrations/005_backup.sql` (`profiles_handle_reserved`, raising `'That handle is reserved'`). It's a trigger, not a validated `check` constraint, specifically so it only ever fires on a *future* write — no handle already claimed before this migration is retroactively checked or broken.
- **Account deletion** (step 05, B1 — the RPC; step 06, B3 — the app calls it): `public.delete_my_account()` (a `security definer` RPC, fixed `search_path`) deletes the caller's own row from `auth.users` — never anyone else's, since it always deletes `where id = auth.uid()` and raises if the caller isn't signed in. Every downstream row (the `profiles` row, and everything that cascades from it: `follows`, `blocks`, `crate_items`, `verified_stats`, `now_spinning`, `digging_list`, `feed_events`, `reports`, plus `library_backup`) is removed automatically by the existing `on delete cascade` foreign keys — this function doesn't delete from any of those tables itself. Irreversible. Since step 06, the Account screen's "Delete account" row (type-your-handle-to-confirm) calls `MizikiSocial.deleteAccount()`, which calls this RPC, resets local social state, and signs out — see the step 06 changelog entry.
- `?social=demo` turns on a dev-only demo mode for the social screens. It must not run in production.
- Fonts are self-hosted (step 01 — `src/fonts/`, `src/styles/fonts.css`), not loaded from Google Fonts. Antonio, Archivo, IBM Plex Mono.

## What's stored server-side, and who can see it

(Step 06, B3; the two Storage rows below added step 07, B4.) The flip side of "Backup: what gets synced" above — written for the privacy policy and the App Store privacy label, not for a developer extending the sync engine. Every table below lives in the user's own Supabase project; Miziki makes no other third-party requests (see "Security" below). **Audio files, embedded art, and location (GPS speed for Car Mode) never leave the device, signed in or not** — nothing in this repo ever uploads any of the three, in any table or bucket, under any column. **A custom cover and the profile picture are the one exception**: since step 07 (B4), a re-encoded, EXIF/GPS-stripped copy of each is uploaded to Supabase Storage (never the original file bytes, and never as part of the `library_backup.doc` blob) — see the two Storage rows below for exactly who can see each.

| Server-side table/column | What it holds | Who can see it |
|---|---|---|
| `public.library_backup.doc` (one row per device, `jsonb`, ≤2MB) | Listening stats (play/session counts, last-played timestamps), achievements/Collection/rare-unlock state, sealed/broken-seal state, track edits (title/artist/album text, never audio or art), playlists and manual ordering/sort/display prefs, app settings (`prefs`), the profile display name, and (step 07) a *claim* about each custom cover/the profile picture (`{hash, updatedAt}` — a pointer into the Storage buckets below, never the image bytes themselves) — everything in the "Backup: what gets synced" table above marked "backed up." | **Only the account that owns the row.** Owner-only RLS (`user_id = auth.uid()`) on every operation; `anon` has no grant at all; no view, feed, RPC or policy anywhere in the schema reads another user's `library_backup` row. The server itself never parses `doc` — it's an opaque blob to Postgres. |
| Storage bucket `covers` (step 07, B4; one file per unique re-encoded image, path `{user_id}/{sha256}.jpg`) | A re-encoded (EXIF/GPS stripped), capped-at-1200px copy of a custom cover set in the edit sheet. | **Only the account that owns the folder.** Private bucket, owner-only read/upload/delete RLS on `storage.objects` (`(storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text`) — the same shape as `library_backup`'s own row-ownership check, just on a Storage path instead of a column. |
| Storage bucket `avatars` (step 07, B4; path `{user_id}/avatar-{sha256}.jpg`) | A re-encoded (EXIF/GPS stripped), 512px-square copy of the profile picture — the one image in this whole pipeline that's **publicly readable**, since it doubles as the account's public avatar once signed in. | **Anyone** (including signed out) can read it — that's the point, the same way `profiles`' own handle/display name/avatar can be. Only the owning account can upload or delete its own file. |
| `public.profiles` (handle, display name, avatar URL, bio) | The social-layer profile a user chooses to show. | The handle/display name/avatar are visible to anyone the user's visibility setting (private/friends/public, Privacy screen) allows — friends by default. Blocked accounts are excluded via `is_blocked_between`/`can_view` (now in the API-invisible `private` schema, step 05). |
| `public.now_spinning`, `crate_items`, `digging_list`, `verified_stats` | "Now spinning" presence, crate picks, the digging list, verified play-count stats — the social layer's own opt-in sharing surface. | Same visibility rule as `profiles` — gated by the Privacy screen's share-now-spinning/share-milestones toggles (both default **off**) and the overall visibility setting, enforced by RLS using the same `private`-schema helper functions. |
| `public.follows`, `blocks` | Who follows whom; who's blocked whom. | A user's own follow/block lists are visible only to themselves via RLS; follow *counts* are the only thing exposed more broadly, through an existing RPC. |
| `public.feed_events` | The social home feed's own event log (a friend started spinning something, hit a milestone, etc.) | Visible only to accounts that could already see the underlying event per the rules above — generated from, not a bypass of, the tables it summarizes. |
| `public.reports` | User-filed reports against other accounts/content. | Not visible to the reported account or to other users — moderation-only by RLS. |
| `auth.users` (Supabase Auth, not a Miziki-defined table) | Email address, used only for the emailed one-time sign-in code. | Supabase's own auth infrastructure; Miziki never stores a password (there is none — sign-in is a 6-digit emailed code). |

**Nothing above was new as of step 06** except that `library_backup` actually held real data for the first time (migration 005/step 05 created the empty table; step 06 was the first PR that wrote real rows to it) — every other row in that version of this table described tables that predate step 06's own work. **The two Storage rows are new as of step 07** (migration 006 created the empty buckets; step 07 is the first PR that writes real files to them). The one-sentence version, shown verbatim on the Privacy screen, is unchanged by step 07 — it was never a claim about images, only about music and location: *"Your listening history is backed up privately to your account. Your music and your location never leave this device."*

**Download my data** (Privacy screen, step 06): a `miziki-data-YYYY-MM-DD.json` export a user can pull straight from the app — local stats/settings/playlists/track-edit text always (no audio, no images, signed in or not), plus the account/social/backup section's own current snapshot when signed in. This is the same data inventoried above, just handed directly to the user who owns it, in the one format (JSON, not a account support ticket) that actually answers "what do you have on me."

## Security

- **Miziki makes no third-party requests at runtime, other than the user's own Supabase project, when one is configured.** Everything else the app needs — fonts, the social client library, the app's own code — is bundled or self-hosted; nothing is fetched from a CDN. **Rule: a future change that adds a new external host (a CDN, an API, an embed) needs both a Content Security Policy update (below) and a privacy note here saying what that host receives and when.**
- **Content Security Policy** (`miziki.html`'s `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy">` — GitHub Pages serves static files with no way to set a real HTTP response header, so a meta tag is the only option, and it's a second line of defense, not the only one): `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' blob: data: https://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co; media-src 'self' blob:; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' blob: https://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co wss://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co; worker-src 'self' blob:; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; object-src 'none'`. `style-src` needs `'unsafe-inline'` because the app writes many inline `style="..."` attributes directly (crate/gatefold pose math, share-canvas layout, disc backgrounds, …) — there is no inline `<script>` anywhere, so `script-src` has no matching exception. `worker-src 'self' blob:` is what lets the AudioWorklet scratch processor (`src/audio/scratch-processor.js`, loaded same-origin via `new URL(...)`, F2) register at all; `img-src`/`media-src`'s `blob:` covers locally-decoded artwork and audio, never a network fetch. **`connect-src`'s `blob:` was missing from step 01's original policy and added in step 02** once Playwright verification for that step turned up that `src/crate/tiers.js`'s `buildCrateTier()` and `src/library/thumbs.js`'s `makeThumb()` both `fetch()` a track's own blob: object URL to re-read its embedded cover art before resizing it — without `blob:` here, every one of those fetches was silently blocked by the browser and swallowed by its own `catch(_e){return false}`, so crate art and thumbnails never actually built for any file with embedded artwork (or a custom cover) once the CSP shipped. Caught and fixed before step 02 merged, not a separate incident.
  - **Step 04 added the real Supabase project's own origins**, once `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL`/`SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` stopped being empty: `https://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co` in both `connect-src` and `img-src`, and `wss://yeyeprzdlbyacisfftjp.supabase.co` in `connect-src` — the realtime "now spinning" subscription needs the websocket origin, REST/auth calls and any Storage-hosted avatar need the https one. Without these, sign-in fails closed with a CSP violation rather than a clear error.
- **Supabase client is bundled, not CDN-loaded** (`@supabase/supabase-js`, an exact-pinned dependency in `package.json`). `main.js`'s `restoreLibrary()` only `await import()`s it when `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL`/`SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` are both non-empty, passed to `MizikiSocial.init()` as `opts.supabaseLib` — the same dynamic-import-for-code-splitting shape as the `?social=demo` chunk. Before step 04, with both keys empty, Rollup proved the import's branch unreachable and eliminated it entirely — confirmed by grepping the built main chunk for any trace of supabase-js (none) — so it added zero bytes to what every user downloaded. **Since step 04, real keys mean this is now a genuinely separate, lazily-fetched chunk** (confirmed in both built targets: a ~231 kB/~60 kB-gzip chunk, fetched only once sign-in is actually opened, main chunk size unchanged). `src/miziki-social.js`'s `init()` has no CDN fallback — passing `supabaseLib` (or a real global at `root.supabase`, e.g. for a test harness that loads it another way) is the only way to give it a client library; if neither is available, `init()` fails closed (inert, same as missing keys) rather than ever fetching one.
- **Tag text (song title/artist/album — untrusted, since it comes from a file's own embedded metadata) must never reach `innerHTML`/`outerHTML`/`insertAdjacentHTML` with anything but a plain string literal.** Build the DOM with `textContent`/`createElement`/`createTextNode` instead. Enforced by an ESLint `no-restricted-syntax` rule (`eslint.config.js`, step 01) that errors on exactly that shape; the small number of genuinely static markup literals already in the codebase (`'Mi<span>zi</span>ki'`, clearing something with `= ''`) are unaffected, since the rule only objects to a *non-literal* right-hand side/argument. Two real instances of this bug were found and fixed in step 01 — see that step's own changelog entry.
- **Server-side RLS-helper functions never belong in a schema the Data API exposes** (step 05, fixing four findings a Supabase Security Advisor scan surfaced on Oct 12): `is_blocked_between`, `item_visibility`, `effective_visibility` and `can_view` are `security definer` functions that decide what's visible to whom — before step 05 they lived in `public`, reachable by anyone (even signed out) directly via `/rest/v1/rpc/<name>`, which could leak whether two users had blocked each other or whether a private crate item existed, independent of any RLS policy. `supabase/migrations/005_backup.sql` moved all four into a new `private` schema (never added to Supabase's exposed Data API schema list) and revoked `public`/`anon` execute on them, keeping `authenticated` (which the RLS policies that call them still need). A function body that calls one of the four by name has to reference the new `private.`-qualified name — a policy itself doesn't, since it resolves to a function's OID at the moment the policy is created, not by looking up the name again later. The same migration fixed two smaller Advisor findings: `releases_insert`'s policy was `with check (true)` (anyone could pre-register a popular album's `release_key` with a fake title/artist) — fixed by adding a `created_by uuid default auth.uid()` column and changing the check to `created_by = auth.uid()` **(a traceability fix, not a correctness one — `created_by` just lets a bad row be found and cleaned; it doesn't stop a determined user from winning the race on a given `release_key`, since `sync_crate`/`set_now_spinning` are still `security invoker` inserting as whoever got there first. Before a public launch, replace this with real canonical release identity — the planned MusicBrainz pass — or server-verified keys, so `release_key` itself can't be squatted on at all)**; and `touch_updated_at()` had a mutable `search_path` — fixed with `set search_path = ''` (harmless, since `now()` lives in `pg_catalog`, which is always implicitly searched regardless of `search_path`). "Leaked password protection," the Advisor's other standing suggestion, doesn't apply here — Miziki signs in with emailed one-time codes, never passwords.

## Refactor rules (modularization in progress)

1. **No behavior changes in refactor PRs.** A move is a move. Fixes and features go in separate PRs.
2. **One extraction per branch and PR**, small enough to review as a diff.
3. **Deployed URL must not change:** it stays `https://esang-mao.github.io/Miziki/miziki.html`, because the home-screen app is saved to that URL. The build must keep base path `/Miziki/` and output a file named `miziki.html`.
4. Prefer ES modules with explicit imports and exports over new globals. When a module needs `S`, import a shared `state` module rather than reaching for `window`.
5. Keep the section comments and spec references (e.g. "CREDITS spec §2") when moving code. They are the project's design history.
6. After every extraction, run the build, then the device checklist below.
7. **An importing module cannot reassign an exported `let`.** Live bindings are read-only on the import side — only the module that declared the `let` can assign to it. `const` exports are fine to mutate *properties* of (e.g. `S.tracks = …` on the exported `S` object), since that isn't reassigning the binding itself; the rule only bites for a top-level `let` whose value gets replaced wholesale. If code in one module needs to reassign a top-level `let` defined in another, either fold that value into `S` (so it's a property write, not a rebinding) or export a small setter function from the module that owns it. Never work around this with `window`. There are roughly two dozen such top-level `let`s still in `main.js` (`socialAuth`, `GF`, `SEALSHEET`, `saveTimer`, and others) — deal with one only once an extraction actually moves code across the boundary it crosses, not preemptively.
8. **An extracted module may import from `main.js` only as a temporary step**, and only use those imports inside functions, never at module top level — the same shape as the circular imports from step 2c. Each later extraction should move those targets out of `main.js` too, so the number of imports from `main.js` keeps shrinking rather than growing. **As of step 7e, zero modules import from `main.js` anywhere in `src/`** (`grep -rn "from '.*main.js'" src/` outside `main.js` itself returns nothing) — the last five (`player/transport.js`'s `updateGatefoldNowPlaying`, `library/sealed.js`'s `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`, `crate/view.js`'s `openGatefold`/`openSealedSheet`/`sealedQuietPlay`, `shell/routes.js`'s `GF`/`closeGatefoldInstant`, and `shell/handoff.js`'s `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant`) all moved to real imports from the new `src/gatefold/` modules in that step, once the gatefold and sealed-record sheet existed for them to import from directly instead of circularly. This doesn't retire the rule itself — a future extraction could still need to reach back into whatever's left in `main.js` (the persistence/launch-intro-gate glue and the `wiring` section, step 7f's own territory) — but there is currently nothing on this list.

   `src/player/sleep-timer.js`, `src/player/scrub.js`, `src/ui/path-note.js`, `src/library/duplicate-scan.js`, `src/history/sessions.js`, `src/history/played.js`, `src/history/clear.js`, `src/history/achievements.js`, `src/record-art/metals.js`, `src/record-art/looks.js`, `src/audio/engine.js`, `src/player/controls.js`, `src/audio/mode.js`, `src/share/crate-card.js`, `src/profile/badges.js`, `src/profile/storefront.js`, `src/profile/settings.js`, `src/profile/screens.js`, `src/social/screens/friend-store.js`, `src/social/screens/home.js`, `src/social/demo.js`, `src/sundown/location.js`, `src/player/background.js`, `src/player/clock.js`, `src/player/queue.js`, `src/util/motion.js`, `src/shell/player-overlay.js`/`src/shell/start-sequence.js`/`src/shell/put-away.js`, `src/library/thumbs.js`, `src/shell/mini-player.js`, `src/shell/header.js`, `src/storage/meta.js`, `src/crate/geometry.js`/`src/crate/tiers.js`/`src/crate/model.js`/`src/launch/intro.js`, `src/library/import.js`, `src/player/transport.js`, `src/library/sealed.js`, `src/crate/view.js`, `src/shell/routes.js`, `src/shell/handoff.js` and `src/gatefold/view.js`/`src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` import nothing from `main.js` — step 3c moved `sleep-timer.js`/`scrub.js`/`path-note.js`'s last remaining `main.js`-sourced names (`drawTime`/`setPathNote`) into modules of their own, `duplicate-scan.js` never needed any (step 5d) — it reaches `deleteTracks` as a real import from `delete.js` instead — and `sessions.js` lost its last two (`applyDiscVariant`/`restoreThumbs`) in step 6b, once `record-art/looks.js`/`library/thumbs.js` existed for it to import them from directly instead; `achievements.js`, `metals.js` and `looks.js` never needed any (step 6b) — `looks.js` does have a real circular import with `sessions.js` itself (for `persistAlbumLookPref`), the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`, which is a peer import, not a `main.js` one; `engine.js` lost its last one (`applyOutputRoute`) in step 6c, once `player/background.js` existed to import it from directly instead — `engine.js` and `background.js` are now a circular pair of their own (for `ensureContext`), the same shape; `controls.js` and `mode.js` (step 6c) never needed any; since step 6e, `played.js`/`clear.js` lost their last `main.js` name (`persistTrackLastPlayed`, and `clear.js`'s `renderProfile`), now real imports from `profile/screens.js`, and `crate-card.js` lost its last one (`fmtCount`) the same way — `badges.js`/`storefront.js`/`settings.js` (profile) and `friend-store.js`/`home.js`/`demo.js` (social) never needed any; `storefront.js` and `settings.js` do have real circular imports with `profile/screens.js` (a 3-way cluster, the same peer-import shape, just three modules), and `friend-store.js`/`home.js` have a real circular pair of their own (for `feedEventText`/`openFriendStore`) — none of this is a `main.js` import. Since step 7a, `location.js` and `background.js` lost their last `main.js` name (`queueSave`, now a real import from `storage/meta.js`) — both do have real circular pairs with `meta.js` itself (`meta.js` imports `applyVolume` back from `location.js`, and `bgUpdateUI`/`applyBgAudioSetting` back from `background.js`, both for `applyPrefs()`), the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`, not a `main.js` import. Since step 7b, `player/clock.js` lost its last two names (`easeOutCubic`/`REDUCED`, now real imports from `util/motion.js`), `player/queue.js` lost its last five (`runStartSequence`/`openPlayerViaSheet`/`shouldPutAway`/`putAwayInstant`/`runPutAwaySequence`, now real imports from `shell/start-sequence.js`/`shell/player-overlay.js`/`shell/put-away.js`) and `profile/screens.js` lost its last one (`refreshLibraryTools`, now a real import from `shell/header.js`) — `util/motion.js` never needed any; `shell/player-overlay.js` has a real, one-directional import from `shell/start-sequence.js` (not back, so not a circular pair); `shell/start-sequence.js` and `shell/put-away.js` do have a real circular pair of their own (for `waitAnimEnd`/`sleeveArtFor` and `clearSpinDown`), the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js` — none of this is a `main.js` import. Since step 7c, `library/thumbs.js`, `shell/mini-player.js` and `shell/header.js` each lost their last `main.js` name (`invalidateCrateModel`, `applyCrateMiniInset`/`centerEmptyState`, and `libraryHasGenres`, respectively — now real imports from `crate/model.js`/`crate/view.js`/`crate/model.js`), and `storage/meta.js` lost its last one too (`attachLaunchSettingRow`, now a real import from `launch/intro.js`) — `meta.js` and `launch/intro.js` do have a real circular pair of their own as a result (`intro.js` imports `queueSave` back from `meta.js`), the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`. `crate/geometry.js`, `crate/tiers.js`, `crate/model.js` and `launch/intro.js` never needed any `main.js` import in the first place; `crate/view.js` does have a real, one-directional import from `launch/intro.js` (for `resolveLastPlayedAlbum`) — `intro.js` doesn't import back, so not a circular pair. Since step 7d, `library/import.js` lost its last `main.js` name (`renderTracks`, now a real import from `library/view.js`) — a real, one-directional import (`view.js` doesn't import anything back from `import.js`), so not a circular pair; `transport.js`'s own `renderTracks` name moved the same way, shrinking its list to just `updateGatefoldNowPlaying`. `library/view.js` itself never needed any `main.js` import — it does have real circular pairs with six sibling modules (`library/credits.js`, `library/favorites.js`, `library/search.js`, `library/sealed.js`, `library/edit.js`, `library/delete.js`) and `crate/view.js`, the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`, just repeated several times (see `view.js`'s own repo-layout entry above for the full list). Since step 7e, the last five modules on rule 8's list all moved to real imports from the new `src/gatefold/view.js`/`src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js`: `player/transport.js` (`updateGatefoldNowPlaying`) and `library/sealed.js` (`updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`) each pick up a real circular pair with `gatefold/` as a result (`gatefold/view.js` imports `play` back from `transport.js`; `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` imports `buildSealOverlay` back from `library/sealed.js`) — the same peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js`. `crate/view.js` (`openGatefold`/`openSealedSheet`/`sealedQuietPlay`), `shell/routes.js` (`GF`/`closeGatefoldInstant`) and `shell/handoff.js` (`isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant`) each become real, one-directional imports instead — none of the `gatefold/` modules imports anything back from any of these three, so none of them are circular pairs. `gatefold/view.js` and `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` themselves never needed any `main.js` import — `gatefold/view.js` has the two real circular pairs just described plus a third, with `shell/handoff.js` (for `runGatefoldHandoff`; `handoff.js` imports `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant` back, per the above); `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` has the pair with `library/sealed.js` above plus a real, one-directional import from `gatefold/view.js` itself (`GF`/`buildGatefoldTracklist`/`gatefoldTrackIndices` — `view.js` doesn't import anything back from `sealed-sheet.js`, so not a circular pair). **As of this step, zero modules anywhere in `src/` import from `main.js`** — see refactor rule 8 above.

   A future cleanup, noted here rather than done now (step 3b): transport's calls back into `main.js` are direct calls, not events or callbacks, so the order things happen in is exactly what it was before the move. Replacing them with an event/callback layer would change that order and risk breaking the iOS audio-gesture rules (rule 9 below), so it's left as direct, temporary circular imports until the functions on the other end are themselves extracted. The same applies to `clock.js`'s circular import.
9. **The iOS audio-gesture rules are load-bearing and must survive every move untouched.** `play()`'s first call is `bgPrime()`, before any `await` — iOS only allows audio to start inside the original tap, so anything that delays past the first microtask boundary breaks it. When moving or refactoring playback code: never add, remove or reorder an `await`; never make a synchronous function `async` or the reverse; keep the `S.gen` generation counter and `onended` handler logic exactly as they are (they're how a stale source is kept from advancing the queue); keep the encoder delay/padding handling in `startSource` (PLAYBACK spec §1) unchanged.
10. **Every extraction PR must pass `npm run lint` with zero warnings** (step 6a introduced the check at zero *errors*; step 7g tightened it to zero *warnings* too — see that step's own changelog entry for why and how). The rule set is small on purpose (`no-undef` and a handful of other core-ESLint safety checks, no style rules — see the Step 6a changelog entry) — it exists to catch the `idOf` class of bug (step 5d: an import dropped from `main.js` while a function still used it; a missing import inside an ES module doesn't fail the build, it only throws once that line runs) before merge, not to enforce a coding style. `npm run lint` is `eslint . --max-warnings=0`; both CI pipelines run it ahead of `npm run test:unit`, so any lint finding — error or warning — blocks a deploy exactly like a failing test. An unused catch-clause binding, var, or argument that's deliberately unused (the "bad input — leave whatever was already there" pattern, by far the most common shape) is named with a leading underscore (`_e`, `_err`, `_mode`, …) rather than left bare — `no-unused-vars`'s `argsIgnorePattern`/`varsIgnorePattern`/`caughtErrorsIgnorePattern` all match `^_`, so this silences the warning without actually needing the variable. A genuinely dead import or declaration (nothing left that reads it, deliberate or not) gets removed outright instead of prefixed — prefixing would just be hiding dead code, not explaining it.

### Revised extraction order

1. ~~Audio graph (out 1), then render loop and spin-to-scrub.~~ **Done** — steps 3a (`src/audio/engine.js`), 3b (`src/player/transport.js`, `current()`), 3c (`src/player/clock.js`, `src/player/scrub.js`, `src/ui/path-note.js`, `S.scrubbing`). What's left in `main.js`: persistence and library, queue, background playback, the screens, and `wiring` (see "After step 3" below).
2. ~~Persistence behind a storage interface~~, then library, metadata edits, deletion and clear-history. **Persistence part done** — steps 4a (`src/storage/idb.js`, the "never lose the library" tests), 4b (`src/storage/prefs.js`, pure `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs`) and 4c (`src/storage/repo.js`, the named interface replacing ~100 direct `DB.*` calls) are all complete. What's left of this item: the library, metadata edits, deletion and clear-history *screens* themselves still live in `main.js` — only the storage layer underneath them has moved.
3. ~~The library (~3,000 lines), in five PRs.~~ **Done** — 5a (library model + queue + decoded-audio cache), 5b (import and tag reading), 5c (play history), 5d (track edits and deletion), 5e (visible queue, favorites, search, liner notes and credits) — see their own changelog entries below. **Step 5 is now complete.**
4. ~~Step 6: a lint safety net, then metal tiers/looks/sealed records/achievements, background playback and transport controls, share images, and profile/social screens with demo mode split out of the main bundle.~~ **Done** — 6a (ESLint safety net), 6b (`src/record-art/`, `src/library/sealed.js`/`thumbs.js`, `src/history/achievements.js`), 6c (`src/player/background.js`/`controls.js`, `src/audio/mode.js`), 6d (`src/share/`), 6e (`src/profile/`, `src/social/`) — see their own changelog entries below. **Step 6 is now complete.**
5. ~~Step 7, in seven PRs: persistence, navigation shell, launch intro/crate, library list, gatefold/sealed-record sheet, wiring, lint-warnings cleanup.~~ **Done** — 7a (`src/storage/meta.js`), 7b (`src/shell/`, `src/util/motion.js`), 7c (`src/crate/geometry.js`/`tiers.js`/`model.js`/`view.js`, `src/launch/intro.js`), 7d (`src/library/view.js`, `renderTracks`), 7e (the gatefold + sealed-record sheet), 7f (`src/wiring/`, 13 files — `main.js` is now the short boot file the plan always said it should become), 7g (every lint warning fixed, `npm run lint` now blocks on warnings too) — see their own changelog entries below. **Step 7 is now fully complete.**

## Device checklist (run on iPhone, from the home-screen app, after each change)

- [ ] App opens; the launch intro plays (and is skipped when turned off)
- [ ] Existing library is still there after the update (IndexedDB intact)
- [ ] Add songs and add a folder; FLAC, WAV, ALAC and MP3 play
- [ ] Pure and Vinyl switch works; character sliders audibly change Vinyl
- [ ] Sundown auto rate and the preview slider; manual rate
- [ ] Sleeve pull, mini-player to full-player morph, spin-to-scrub, scratching with Scratch on/off
- [ ] Crate view, gatefold, sealed records
- [ ] Queue: play next, add, reorder, shuffle and repeat
- [ ] Edit track info and revert; remove from library
- [ ] Background playback plus lock-screen controls with the screen locked
- [ ] Car Mode reads speed (test while driving as a passenger, or skip)
- [ ] Sleep timer
- [ ] Share image: each style and format saves
- [ ] With social config empty, social screens show the calm "not set up" state

## Commands

- `npm install` — install Vite, `fake-indexeddb` (step 4a) and `eslint`/`globals` (step 6a) — all dev dependencies, test/lint-only, not part of the built app.
- `npm run dev` — Vite dev server at `http://localhost:5173/Miziki/` (note the `/Miziki/` — `base` is mounted in dev too, so `http://localhost:5173/` alone 404s).
- `npm run build` — builds to `dist/miziki.html` plus `dist/assets/` (bundled, hashed CSS and, since step 2a, the bundled `main.js`) and `dist/src/miziki-social.js` (copied verbatim, see `vite.config.js`). `dist/` is gitignored.
- `npm run preview` — serves the built `dist/` at `http://localhost:4173/Miziki/`, closest to what GitHub Pages actually serves.
- `npm run lint` — runs ESLint (`eslint.config.js`, step 6a) over the whole repo. A small, focused rule set — `no-undef` plus a handful of other core-ESLint safety checks (see refactor rule 10 above) — not a style linter; no formatting rules. Zero errors required; `no-unused-vars` warnings don't block. Both CI pipelines run this before `npm run test:unit`, so a lint error blocks a deploy exactly like a failing test.
- `npm test` — runs `tests/client.test.js` (needs a local Postgres; see below).
- `npm run test:unit` — runs `tests/unit/*.test.js` with Node's built-in `node:test`, under `--experimental-test-module-mocks` (needed by `engine.test.js`'s `mock.module()`; requires Node 22, see "Node version" below). No Postgres, no extra dependency. Both CI pipelines (`deploy.yml` and the Netlify build) run this before building, so a failing unit test blocks both deploys.

Social client tests need a local Postgres. See the header of `tests/client.test.js` for the connection defaults (`PGHOST=/var/tmp/pgmz`, `PGPORT=5544`, database `mz`).

## Deploy previews (Netlify)

Every PR gets a Netlify deploy preview, separate from the real GitHub Pages
deploy — **GitHub Pages stays on `/Miziki/` and is untouched by this.**

- `netlify.toml` runs `npm run build` with `MIZIKI_BASE=/` (Netlify previews
  live at their own root domain, not under a `/Miziki/` subpath the way the
  GitHub Pages project site does) and `NODE_VERSION=20`, publishes `dist`,
  and rewrites `/` to `/miziki.html` (status 200, not a redirect — the URL
  bar stays at the preview root) so the preview link opens the app
  directly instead of 404ing on an index page that doesn't exist.
- To test the same build locally: `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, then
  `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run preview` (the preview server also reads `base`
  from the build, so it has to be set the same way or it'll try to serve
  from `/Miziki/` again) — then open `http://localhost:4173/miziki.html`.
- `MIZIKI_BASE` only changes `base` in `vite.config.js`; nothing else
  about the build differs between the two targets.
- A Netlify preview is a different origin from GitHub Pages, so its
  IndexedDB starts empty: an existing library doesn't show up, and prefs
  (including **Background playback**) default off. If lock-screen
  playback stops on a fresh preview, that's this, not a regression —
  toggle Background playback on for that origin too.
- **`miziki.netlify.app` (the production domain) no longer updates, on
  purpose.** Netlify builds both a production deploy (whatever's pushed to
  the site's production branch, `main`) and a deploy preview for every PR,
  as two separate billed builds — but nothing ever links to or uses
  `miziki.netlify.app`; the real app is GitHub Pages. Paying for a
  production rebuild on every push to `main`, on top of the PR previews
  that are the only thing this Netlify site is actually for, is what ran
  the account's build credits out before. `netlify.toml`'s `[build]` table
  now has `ignore = "[ \"$BRANCH\" = \"main\" ]"`: Netlify's `ignore` setting
  runs that command before starting a build and, per Netlify's own docs,
  "skips the build, returning early from the build process" if it exits
  `0` — which happens, per that same bash test, only when the branch being
  built is `main`. Per Netlify's billing FAQ, build-minute counting starts
  when dependencies begin loading, which is after this check, so a skipped
  build shouldn't be billed — this is also the documented, standard way
  (e.g. in monorepo setups) to avoid paying for builds Netlify would
  otherwise run unnecessarily, not a workaround. A PR's deploy preview
  builds its own head branch, never `main`,
  so `$BRANCH` there is the PR branch name, the check is false, and the
  preview builds exactly as before. If `miziki.netlify.app` itself is ever
  needed again (e.g. to compare against a GitHub Pages regression), it's
  one `enabled = false` removal away, or a manual "Trigger deploy" from the
  Netlify dashboard still works regardless of this setting.

### What changed in step 1 (tooling)

No behavior change — this step only adds build tooling and moves code, per the refactor rules above.

- Added Vite (`package.json`, `vite.config.js`) with a GitHub Actions workflow (`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`) that builds and deploys `dist/` to GitHub Pages on every push to `main`.
- The inline `<style>` block became 21 files under `src/styles/`, referenced via `<link rel="stylesheet">` tags in the same order, immediately after the Google Fonts links (unchanged).
- The vendored copy of the social client inside `miziki.html` is gone; it now loads `src/miziki-social.js` directly, as a classic script (not a module) so timing stays identical — the main script right after it still does a synchronous `typeof MizikiSocial` check.
- Verified (see the PR): confirmed the two social-client copies were byte-identical before removing the vendored one; diffed the built `dist/miziki.html` against the pre-step-1 original with the CSS and social-script regions normalized out — the only remaining difference was a stale comment folded into the new one above the script tag. Nothing in the ~10,100-line main script changed.

### What changed in step 2c (lowest-coupling extractions + first unit tests)

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above.

- Extracted the five lowest-coupling sections out of `main.js`, in order: record-art quality tiers + variant selection (`src/record-art/tiers.js`), solar (`src/sundown/solar.js`), the crate-view constants block (`src/crate/constants.js`), location & motion (`src/sundown/location.js`), and the sleep timer (`src/player/sleep-timer.js`). Section comments and spec references (e.g. "SLEEP spec §5", "CRATE spec §3") moved with the code.
- `normKey()`, defined inside the tiers section but used ~40 times app-wide, moved to its own `src/util/text.js` instead of staying in `tiers.js`.
- `clamp()` moved to `src/util/math.js`, and the local `$()` shorthand moved into `src/util/dom.js` alongside `el()` — both were still defined locally in `main.js` and used by the newly-extracted modules, so leaving them in place would have meant copying them instead of moving them.
- `location.js` and `sleep-timer.js` each import a few functions back from `main.js` (`drawSun`/`queueSave`, and `pause`/`drawTime`/`setPathNote` respectively) — a deliberate circular import. Those functions got `export` added in `main.js` with no other change; this is the standard, safe shape for extracting a section that leaf-level code elsewhere in `main.js` still depends on, before that code is extracted too.
- Added the first player unit tests: `tests/unit/tiers.test.js` (`trackTier`) and `tests/unit/solar.test.js` (`solarEvent`, checked against a published sunset time for Columbia, MD plus a polar no-sunset case), run via the new `npm run test:unit` (Node's built-in `node:test`, no new dependency). Wired into both CI pipelines ahead of the build, so a failing unit test blocks both deploys.
- Verified lossless: each new module was diffed against the exact original lines it was extracted from (modulo only `export`/`import` additions), and the full `git diff` of `main.js` for this step was reviewed end to end — every removal matches a verified new-module file, and every other change is either an added import line or a single `export` keyword.

### Node version

Node **22** (bumped from 20 in step 3a — Node 20 reached end-of-life in April 2026). Set in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` (`node-version`), `netlify.toml` (`NODE_VERSION`), and `package.json`'s `engines` field. `npm run test:unit` also needs 22 for `--experimental-test-module-mocks` (see Commands).

### What changed in step 3a (audio graph into `src/audio/engine.js`)

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above.

- Extracted the audio graph (`makeContext`, `buildGraph`, `satCurve`, `applyCharacter`, `routeSource`) and `ensureContext()` (the one function pulled out of the much larger "file loading" section) into `src/audio/engine.js`.
- `ensureContext()` calls `stop()` and `applyOutputRoute()`, which stay in `main.js` for now (rule 8 above) — imported back as a deliberate, temporary circular import, same shape as `location.js`'s and `sleep-timer.js`'s from step 2c. `applyVolume()` was already available from `location.js`.
- Added `tests/unit/engine.test.js`: `satCurve` (identity curve at 0, in-range/monotonic/trimmed-below-1 at 0.35 and 1 — with a small tolerance for a real, intentional overshoot the asymmetry term produces, not float noise) and the Pure/Vinyl chain-order promise (`buildGraph`'s wobble → sat → satTrim → dc → tone → comp → makeup → master wiring, and `routeSource`'s Pure-vs-Vinyl branch), using a fake `AudioContext` that just records `connect()` calls.
- `engine.js`'s circular import back to `main.js` means loading it for real needs a browser (`main.js` has DOM-dependent code at module top level, not just inside functions). The test uses Node 22's `mock.module()` (`node --experimental-test-module-mocks`) to replace `main.js`'s exports with no-ops first — not just the two `engine.js` imports directly, but every export any module in that import chain needs from `main.js` (`location.js`, loaded for `applyVolume`, also needs `drawSun`/`queueSave`), since `mock.module` replaces the whole module by resolved path, not per-importer.
- Upgraded Node 20 → 22 (separate commit) — see "Node version" above.

### What changed in step 3b (transport into `src/player/transport.js`)

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. This is the step where the iOS audio-gesture rules (rule 9 above) mattered most: nothing about *when* anything runs changed, only *where it's defined*.

- Moved `load`, `startSource`, `play`, `stop`, `pause` and `seek` — sitting at the end of the "launch intro" section with no header of their own — into `src/player/transport.js`. Verified lossless: diffed the moved block against the exact original lines (modulo only the `export` keywords it already had, from step 2c/3a, plus the new ones rule 8's list needed) — identical.
- Moved `current()` (`S.tracks[S.index] || null`) into `src/state.js`, next to `S`. It's used ~47 times across the app, so this removes a `main.js` dependency from every module that calls it, present and future.
- `engine.js` imported `stop` from `main.js`; now imports it from `transport.js` instead. `sleep-timer.js` imported `pause` from `main.js`; now imports it from `transport.js` instead. Both still import their other `main.js` names (`applyOutputRoute`, `drawTime`/`setPathNote`) as before.
- `transport.js` itself imports ~20 functions back from `main.js` — see rule 8's list and its future-cleanup note. Confirmed no `await` was added, removed or moved, and no function's sync/async-ness changed, by diffing the six functions' bodies against the pristine originals.
- `tests/unit/engine.test.js` needed a second `mock.module()` call, for `transport.js` (replacing its one export, `stop`, with a no-op) — otherwise loading `engine.js` would transitively pull in `transport.js`'s whole ~20-function `main.js` dependency list too. Mocking `transport.js` directly, rather than also listing everything *it* needs from `main.js`, is simpler and keeps the test from having to track `transport.js`'s dependency list as it grows.

### What changed in step 3c (playback clock and spin-to-scrub)

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. **Step 3 is now complete** — see the "Revised extraction order" note above for what's left in `main.js`.

- Moved the "render loop" section — `tick`, `settleTrackEnd`, `frame`, the hidden-page `setInterval`, the `last`/`lastUI`/`lastSky` variables, `fmt`, `drawTime`, `drawSun` — into `src/player/clock.js`. `sleep-timer.js`, `location.js` and `transport.js` now import `drawTime`/`drawSun` from there instead of `main.js`.
- Moved the "spin-to-scrub" section — `wireSpinToScrub` and its helpers/constants — into `src/player/scrub.js`. `setPathNote`, which happened to sit in that section but is a general on-screen note helper used across the app, got its own module instead: `src/ui/path-note.js`.
- The clock's behavior is unchanged: the `maxDt` caps (0.1s frames, 120s hidden-page timer), the order of work in `tick` (sleep deadline check, then rate, then position, then played ranges and completion), and the 90ms/220ms redraw throttles are all byte-for-byte identical. Verified lossless the same way as step 3b: diffed the moved blocks against the pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions and the one deliberate `scrubbing`→`S.scrubbing` change below).
- `scrubbing` was a top-level `let` declared in the render loop and reassigned by the `#scrub` slider's wiring handlers — the exported-`let` rule (rule 7) meant it had to become `S.scrubbing` (default `false` in `state.js`) once it crossed a module boundary. Every read and write was updated. The gatefold/crate-rail view has its own function-local `scrubbing` that already shadowed the global one; that one is unrelated and was left alone.
- Added `tests/unit/clock.test.js`: `angleDelta` (wraparound across ±180°, both directions, plus a plain in-range case) and `tick` (advances `S.pos` by `dt × S.rate` while playing, doesn't while paused, never passes the track's duration) — `current()` isn't mocked directly; the tests just set `S.tracks`/`S.index` and let the real `current()` read them back, which is simpler than mocking `state.js` for the same effect.
- `clock.js`'s circular import back to `main.js`, and its real (non-circular) import of `sleepCheckDeadline` from `sleep-timer.js`, meant loading it for real in the test would transitively pull in `sleep-timer.js` → `location.js`/`transport.js` → `main.js`'s DOM-dependent top level. `main.js`, `sleep-timer.js` and `transport.js` are each mocked directly in `clock.test.js` to stop that chain at the door, same approach as `engine.test.js`.
- `clock.js`'s top-level `setInterval` (the hidden-page fallback clock) is a real OS timer the moment `clock.js` is imported directly — harmless in the browser (the page has other reasons to stay alive), but it kept `clock.test.js`'s Node process from exiting on its own, hanging the test run. Fixed in the test file only, with Node's `mock.timers.enable({ apis: ['setInterval'] })` before importing `clock.js`, so a real timer is never created; `clock.js` itself is untouched.
- `engine.test.js` needed a third `mock.module()` call, for `clock.js` (replacing its one export that `location.js` needs, `drawSun`, with a no-op) — same reasoning as the `transport.js` mock added in step 3b: otherwise loading `engine.js` would transitively reach `clock.js`'s own dependency chain (`sleep-timer.js`, more of `main.js`) through `location.js`.

### F1: Pure means untouched (feature, not a refactor)

Unlike the steps above, this one **does** change behavior on purpose: Sundown's rate change (auto or manual) now only ever reaches the recording in Vinyl. Pure always plays at exactly 1.0×. See "How the app is put together" above for the one-line rule and why Car Mode volume/the sleep-timer fade/the user's own volume are exempt (level changes, not changes to the recording).

- `computeRate()` in `src/sundown/solar.js` returns `1` outright when `S.mode === 'pure'`, before even checking `S.auto` — the only place this rule lives. `tick()` in `clock.js` already glides `S.rate` into the audio node via `setTargetAtTime` whenever the computed rate changes, and `frame()` already drives the disc's spin off `S.rate` — both needed zero changes for mode switches to glide smoothly and for the disc to visibly follow the real rate; `computeRate()` returning a different number was the whole fix.
- Fixed three places whose wording implied Pure was still affected by Sundown: the Sundown panel's "Now playing at" line (`drawSun()` in `clock.js`) now reads "Pure — always full speed (Sundown applies in Vinyl)" in Pure, rather than reporting a rate that's always 1.000× with no explanation; the Character panel's Pure note gained a line about the rate; and `setMode()`'s mode-switch note no longer claims "the slow-down still works" in Pure. The Sundown panel's other controls (auto/manual, full-night rate, pacing, the preview slider) stay live and usable in Pure — they describe what Vinyl will do.
- `tests/unit/solar.test.js`: `computeRate()` returns `1` in Pure regardless of `S.auto`/`S.target`; Vinyl is unchanged (same values as before this PR).

### F2: true scratching in spin-to-scrub (feature, not a refactor)

Spinning the disc by hand in Vinyl, with the Character menu's new **Scratch** toggle on (default On), now plays the real track forward or backward at the hand's speed, instead of just moving the position silently. Scratch off, or Pure mode, is exactly today's silent fast-scrub, unchanged.

- Safari can't reliably play an `AudioBufferSourceNode` backward (negative `playbackRate`), so this is an `AudioWorkletNode`: `src/audio/scratch-processor.js`, loaded lazily the first time a scratch gesture happens via `ctx.audioWorklet.addModule(new URL('../audio/scratch-processor.js', import.meta.url))` in `src/player/scrub.js`. The `new URL(..., import.meta.url)` form is what makes Vite copy the file as a plain, unbundled asset (confirmed in the built output: `dist/assets/scratch-processor-*.js`, not inlined, not run through the JS bundler) so it resolves correctly and loads under both `/Miziki/` and `/` bases. It is reloaded into a fresh `AudioContext` if `ensureContext()` ever rebuilds one for a different sample rate (tracked by `moduleLoadedCtx` in `scrub.js`).
- **The worklet file has no `import` or `export`, not just no imports.** It originally exported its pure helpers for the unit test, which worked in every desktop browser tested but silently produced no scratch audio on an iPhone: Safari's AudioWorklet module loader has not reliably supported ES module syntax in the processor file itself, so an `addModule()` that resolves fine in Chrome/Firefox can still fail to actually register the processor in Safari — and `scrub.js` already treats that failure as "no scratch audio this session," by design, so there was nothing to see without a device's remote inspector. Fixed by making the file a plain classic script (no `export` at all); `tests/unit/scratch-processor.test.js` now loads its source with Node's `vm` module instead of `import`, since there's nothing left to import — a classic script's top-level `function` declarations become properties of the sandbox object it runs against, which is how the test reaches the pure helpers, and `registerProcessor()`'s own call is still how it reaches the processor class (same as the real worklet; `class` declarations never become global properties, imported or not).
- **The playhead follows position, not velocity.** `scrub.js` sends the worklet a `target` (absolute track seconds) on every `pointermove`; the worklet moves its own `playhead` toward that target with a one-pole follower (`onePole()`, time constant `FOLLOW_TIME_CONSTANT`) and reads the loaded window at the resulting (possibly fractional, possibly decreasing) sample index with linear interpolation (`sampleAt()`). Reading a decreasing index plays the track backward — there's no separate "reverse" code path. **This is also where the pitch comes from**: advancing two samples of source per output sample plays an octave high, half a sample an octave low, exactly like vari-speed playback on a real turntable — no separate pitch-shifting step was needed or added. `FOLLOW_TIME_CONSTANT` was retuned from an initial `0.012s` up to `0.035s` because the tighter value let the playhead snap to each new touch sample almost instantly, which made real motion too brief to read as a held pitch — more a click per touch event than a scratch. `VELOCITY_FLOOR` (`0.05` → `0.03`) and `ENVELOPE_TIME_CONSTANT` (`0.01` → `0.02`) were loosened to match, so slow deliberate scratches stay audible instead of gating to silence between touch events.
- **Holding the disc still is genuine silence, not a frozen sample.** A fixed `target` would otherwise mean reading the same sample forever — a non-zero constant level, not silence. Loudness is tied to how fast the simulated platter is actually turning (`velocityEnvelopeTarget()`, floor `VELOCITY_FLOOR`, smoothed by its own one-pole so it doesn't buzz) the same way a real record is silent with the motor off and the needle resting on one groove point. This is deliberate, not just click-avoidance — see the comment at the top of `scratch-processor.js`.
- **Only a window of audio is ever copied to the worklet**, not the whole decoded track (tens of MB, and GitHub Pages can't send the `SharedArrayBuffer` headers): `loadScratchWindow()` in `scrub.js` copies ±10s (`SCRATCH_WINDOW_SEC`) of channel data around the current position via `Float32Array.slice()` (a real copy, so transferring its buffer to the worklet never detaches the track's own decoded `AudioBuffer`) and re-sends a new window once the position gets within `SCRATCH_REFETCH_MARGIN_SEC` of either edge.
- **Ratio:** `SCRATCH_SEC_PER_ROTATION = 1.8` (the real 33⅓ rpm speed) replaces `SCRUB_SEC_PER_ROTATION = 30` only when claimed as a scratch (Vinyl + Scratch on); otherwise the existing fast 30s/rotation navigation ratio is unchanged. Both constants are exported for their unit test.
- **Gain ramps (5–10ms)** at gesture start/end run on a plain `GainNode` between the worklet and `S.nodes.wobble` (not inside the worklet), scheduled with `linearRampToValueAtTime`. The node and gain are freed (`disconnect()`) once the end-of-gesture ramp finishes, so an idle worklet is never left running between gestures.
- **iOS:** `S.ctx.resume()` is called synchronously at the very top of the `pointerdown` handler, before anything else and before any `await` — the same rule `play()`'s `bgPrime()` follows, since iOS only allows resuming audio from inside the real user gesture. No `await` in the existing `play()`/`seek()` path was touched; release still goes through the unchanged `seek(S.pos)` then `play()` (only if it was already playing).
- **The toggle:** `S.char.scratch` (default `true` in `state.js`), alongside the other Character sliders in the saved prefs' `char` object. `applyPrefs()` special-cases it (the existing `char` merge loop only accepts numbers, since the sliders are percentages) and a saved value's absence leaves the default (On) in place — no IndexedDB version bump, since it's just a new field inside the existing `prefs.char` object. New row in the Character panel (`#scratchBtn`, styled like `#sleevePullBtn`).
- **Falls back safely:** if `addModule()` or the `AudioWorkletNode` constructor ever throws (unsupported browser, bad build), `scrub.js` logs a warning, sets a module-level `scratchSupported = false`, and every future gesture behaves exactly like Scratch-off — scratching is never allowed to break ordinary spin-to-scrub.
- `tests/unit/scratch-processor.test.js`: reads the worklet file's source and runs it with Node's `vm` module in a sandbox stubbing the AudioWorkletGlobalScope globals (`AudioWorkletProcessor`, `registerProcessor`, `sampleRate`), then exercises both the pure math (`clampIndex`, `sampleAt`, `onePole`, `velocityEnvelopeTarget`, reached as properties the sandbox's global object gained from the script's top-level `function` declarations) and the full `process()` loop on fake buffers: a continuously-advancing target produces forward motion, a continuously-retreating one produces reverse, a target held still after one small move settles to near-silence, and the playhead/reads never leave the loaded window or the track. `tests/unit/clock.test.js` gained one more test confirming the 1.8s/30s rotation ratio constants.
- Verified: both builds (`npm run build`, `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`) emit the worklet as its own asset, confirmed free of `export`/`import` in the built output; `npm run test:unit` passes (34/34); a Playwright script dragged the disc in a real Chromium past the 8° claim threshold against both built targets with `AudioWorklet.prototype.addModule`/`AudioParam.prototype.linearRampToValueAtTime` spied from outside the module (nothing is exposed on `window`) — Vinyl + Scratch on produced a matched ramp-up/ramp-down pair per gesture (while paused and while playing), Scratch off and Pure mode produced none, and `?social=demo` stayed clean. No console or page errors beyond the known baseline 404. The ESM-syntax fix and the retuned constants are desktop-Chromium-verified only — Playwright can't stand in for Safari here, so the actual sound and whether scratching now works at all on an iPhone still needs Ken's device.

### Step 4a: database module plus "never lose the library" tests

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. First of three step-4 PRs putting storage behind an interface (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 2); this one is the safety net, added before anything about *how* the app saves actually changes (4b, 4c).

- Moved `DB` (`db`, `ok`, `req`, `open`, `store`, `put`, `get`, `all`, `clear`, `del`) out of `main.js`'s "persistence" section into `src/storage/idb.js`, unchanged — database name, version 4, the eight stores and key paths, the non-destructive `onupgradeneeded`, the 4-second open timeout and the `onblocked` handling are all byte-for-byte identical. Verified lossless: diffed the moved block against the exact original lines (modulo only the `export` keyword). `main.js` now imports `DB` from there; every one of its ~100 existing `DB.put`/`DB.get`/etc. call sites, and the two `MizikiSocial.mizikiAdapter({S, DB, …})` call sites, are untouched (replacing them is step 4c, not this one).
- Added `fake-indexeddb` as a dev dependency (the second dependency, after Vite) and `tests/unit/idb.test.js`: a fresh install creates all eight stores with the right key paths; an upgrade from a v3-shaped database (seeded with only `tracks`/`meta`/`sessions` and real records in each, via the same fake `IDBFactory` instance so it's a genuine upgrade, not a fresh install) keeps every old record and adds exactly the missing stores; with `indexedDB` undefined, every method no-ops safely (`open`/`put`/`del` return `false`, `get` returns `null`, `all` returns `[]`, nothing throws); and `put`/`get`/`all`/`del`/`clear` round-trip correctly, including a `Blob`. Each test gets its own fresh `IDBFactory` instance assigned to the global `indexedDB` `idb.js` reads, so tests can't see each other's databases.
- Added two persistence rules and a known-gap note to "How the app is put together" above: future version bumps need their own upgrade test in `idb.test.js`, and there's no `onversionchange` handler yet (an old tab open during a future upgrade would block it, not get out of the way) — noted for when version 5 actually ships, not fixed now.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (38/38 — 34 before this step + 4 new); both builds succeed; a Playwright script added a track, reloaded the page (closing and reopening is what actually exercises `DB.open()` reopening the same database and `restoreMeta()` reading it back, not just a first load) and confirmed the track was still there, against both built targets. No console/page errors beyond the known baseline 404.

### Step 4b: saved settings — parse, then apply

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Second of the three step-4 PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 2).

- Split `main.js`'s `applyPrefs(p)` (which both validated each saved field *and* applied it — `setMode`, `cycleRepeat`, `toggleShuffle`, `bgUpdateUI` and the rest) into `serializePrefs(S)`/`parsePrefs(raw)` (`src/storage/prefs.js`, pure, no `main.js` import) plus `applyPrefs`, which stays in `main.js` completely unchanged — same body, same order of screen/side-effect calls, just fed `parsePrefs(raw)`'s output instead of the raw saved object. `saveMeta()` now calls `serializePrefs(S)` instead of building the prefs object inline.
- `parsePrefs` mirrors every one of `applyPrefs`'s existing per-field checks exactly: `char`'s four sliders must be numbers and `char.scratch` a boolean (step 4b hardcodes those field names rather than deriving them from `S.char`'s current keys, since `parsePrefs` never receives `S`); `target`/`auto`/`sleevePullEnabled`/`bgAudio`/`launchIntro`/`albumSides`/`sealedRecords`/`playInFullPlayer` must be the right primitive type; `libraryLayout`/`crateGroup`/`shareStyle`/`shareFormat` must be one of their listed values; `pace`/`librarySort`/`crateAnchor`/`repeat`/`mode` keep `applyPrefs`'s original truthy-only checks (no stricter than today). The one deliberate exception: `shuffle` moved from a truthy-only check to a real `typeof === 'boolean'` check, because `applyPrefs`'s own `if(parsed.shuffle && !S.shuffle)` already treats a present-but-`false` value exactly like an absent one — so this doesn't change what `applyPrefs` does with it, it just lets a saved `shuffle: false` round-trip instead of silently vanishing (caught by the round-trip test below).
- `tests/unit/prefs.test.js`: a full round trip through a fake `S`-shaped object; an old saved object missing `char.scratch` (pre-F2) leaves it at its default (On); a saved object missing newer fields (`shareStyle`, `playInFullPlayer`) or holding only a handful of very old fields loads without throwing; wrong types, unlisted enum values, and `null`/`undefined` input are all ignored rather than thrown on; unknown fields are dropped.
- Added a persistence rule to "How the app is put together" above: every new saved setting goes into both `serializePrefs` and `parsePrefs`, gets a default in `S`, and gets a test in `prefs.test.js` that an old saved object without it still loads.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (50/50 — 38 before this step + 12 new); both builds succeed; a Playwright script changed mode (Pure), Scratch (Off), shuffle (on), repeat (cycled), and library layout (list), reloaded the page, and confirmed every one of them was exactly as left, against both built targets. No console/page errors beyond the known baseline 404.

### Step 4c: a named storage interface in place of direct `DB` calls

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Last of the three step-4 PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 2) — **step 4 is now complete.**

- Added `src/storage/repo.js`: one small object per store (`tracks`, `meta`, `sessions`, `achievements`, `collection`, `overlays`, `artwork`, `profile`) on top of `DB`, each with only the methods something in `main.js` actually calls — no method added that nothing uses. `storage.available()`/`storage.open()` replace direct reads of `DB.ok` and the one `DB.open()` call at boot. Every method is a thin forward to `DB`, so today's storage-unavailable behavior (`false`/`null`/`[]`, never throwing) is unchanged by construction.
- `meta` is the one store whose own shape didn't match what callers wanted: `DB.get('meta', key)` returns the whole `{k, v}` row, and every call site in `main.js` was unwrapping `.v` (or checking it was there) by hand. `meta.get(key)` does that once, returning the bare value (or `null` for a missing key or unavailable storage — callers never had a reason to tell those apart). `meta.put(key, value)` takes the key and value separately and builds the row itself. `meta.thumbs()` is the one place `main.js` reads `meta` by scanning all its rows rather than by one key (artwork thumbnails, keyed `thumb:<kind>:<id>`); it returns `{key, blob}` pairs with the `thumb:` prefix already stripped, same as `restoreThumbs()` always did with the slice itself, so it gets its own named method instead of a generic `all()` nothing else would call.
- Replaced every direct `DB.*` call in `main.js`, one commit per section: sealed records + thumbnails/display snapshots; core persistence (`queueSave`/`saveMeta`/`restoreMeta`); session tracking + metal-tier persistence (`restoreSessions` and its dozen `meta.get`s); metadata-edit overlays/artwork (`loadOverlayFor`, `applyEdit`, `revertTrack`, `applyEditWithUndo`, `undoLastEdit`, `storeTrack`); deletion, forget-library and clear-history (`forgetLibrary` needed an actual rewrite, not just a rename — it used to read and reinsert `meta`'s raw `{k, v}` rows directly across a `DB.clear()`, which `meta.get`'s bare-value return and `meta.put`'s key/value signature don't support directly, so it now tracks `{key, blob}` pairs itself across the same clear/reinsert sequence); crate art tiers; profile persistence and the boot sequence's `DB.open()`/`DB.all('tracks')`. The boot sequence's order (open, then load all tracks, then `restoreMeta()`) and every other section's `await` order are byte-for-byte unchanged — confirmed section by section, running the unit tests and both builds after each one.
- `grep -n "DB\." src/main.js` now finds only the two `MizikiSocial.mizikiAdapter({S, DB, …})` call sites (`localStaffPicks()`, `installSocialDemo()`), each with a comment explaining why: the adapter's own signature expects `DB`, and it's social-client code, not `main.js`'s own storage access — the one exception the step's instructions called for. No other direct `DB.*` call remains in `main.js`.
- Added `tests/unit/repo.test.js`: round-trips every store (including a `Blob` through `artwork`/`meta`), confirms `meta.get` returns the bare value (checked against a raw `DB.get` read of the same row) and that `meta.thumbs()` filters correctly, and confirms every method's storage-unavailable return matches `DB`'s own (`false`/`null`/`[]`, nothing throws) — same fresh-`IDBFactory`-per-test isolation as `idb.test.js`.
- Recorded the App Store plan as a note at the bottom of `repo.js` (not built): in a native shell, the audio bytes (`tracks.blob`), artwork blobs and the `thumb:*` blobs currently kept inside `meta` rows should live as files on the device, with only small records (not bytes) left in the database. This module is where that split would happen, since callers already never touch a blob's storage directly.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (59/59 — 50 before this step + 9 new); both builds succeed; `grep -n "DB\." src/main.js` confirms only the two adapter-comment lines remain.

### Step 5a: library model, queue and the decoded-audio cache

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. First of the five step-5 PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 3) — the step 5 prompt's own table maps each part to its new module; this is 5a.

- Three new modules, each one cohesive section of `main.js`:
  - `src/library/model.js`: `byName`/`trackSort` (comparators), `groupBy`, `allIdx`, `albumTracks` (moved from "library", "favorites" and the top of "persistence" respectively), plus `idOf`/`idxOf`. All pure — no DOM, no `main.js` import.
