### Step 7b: navigation shell, player overlay, start/put-away, hand-off

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Second of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — builds on 7a. **This is the most iOS-gesture-sensitive step since 3b/3c** — see the confirmations at the end of this entry.

- Seven new modules, matching the step 7 prompt's own table (see their own repo-layout entries above for exact exports and imports): `src/util/motion.js` (`REDUCED`, `easeOutCubic`) and six files under `src/shell/` — `mini-player.js`, `routes.js`, `header.js`, `player-overlay.js`, `start-sequence.js`, `handoff.js`, `put-away.js`.
- **Two deliberate exceptions to the prompt's own file-name table, both explained where made:**
  1. **`easeOutCubic` and `REDUCED` went to `src/util/motion.js`, not `src/shell/put-away.js`.** The prompt's table listed `easeOutCubic` under put-away.js's target, since that's physically where it sat — but a separate, more specific instruction said to give both it and `REDUCED` a proper home in `util/motion.js` instead, and that's the one followed: `easeOutCubic` is only ever called by `player/clock.js`'s render loop, never by `put-away.js` itself, so filing it under put-away would have been grouping by old physical location, not by what the code actually is. `put-away.js` doesn't import `easeOutCubic` at all as a result.
  2. **`GATEFOLD_ANIM` stayed in `main.js`.** It's physically interleaved with `SLEEVE_ANIM` in the original file (the two animation-timing blocks sit back to back), but it's the gatefold's own open/close timing, not mini-player/overlay code, and the gatefold itself hasn't moved yet (step 7e) — dragging it into `mini-player.js` would have been a move by proximity, not by what the code is, the same judgment the step 6 prompt's own "wrong header" callouts warned against. Its CSS-custom-property side effects (`--gatefold-lift-duration` etc.) stayed right alongside it.
- Three new real circular/peer pairs, the same permitted shape already established between `engine.js`/`transport.js` since step 3a: `mini-player.js` ↔ `routes.js` (`mini-player.js` needs `isLibraryRouteVisible`; `routes.js` needs `updateHeaderActionsAvailability`/`closeSearchField`/`closeAddMenu` from `header.js`, which itself needs `routes.js`'s two visibility checks back — a 3-way cluster the same shape as `profile/storefront.js`/`settings.js`/`screens.js` from step 6e), and `start-sequence.js` ↔ `put-away.js` (for `waitAnimEnd`/`sleeveArtFor` and `clearSpinDown`). `player-overlay.js` → `start-sequence.js` is a real but one-directional import (no import back), since nothing in `start-sequence.js` ever needs to call back into the player-overlay functions.
- The biggest effect is on the five modules already circularly importing these names from `main.js` since earlier steps: `player/transport.js` (`updateShellAlbum`/`renderMiniPlayer`/`showRoute`/`clearSpinDown`), `player/queue.js` (`runStartSequence`/`openPlayerViaSheet`/`shouldPutAway`/`putAwayInstant`/`runPutAwaySequence` — all five, step 5a's entire list), `library/edit.js` (`renderMiniPlayer`), `library/delete.js` (`clearSpinDown`/`closePlayer`/`renderMiniPlayer`), `library/credits.js` (`closePlayer`/`showRoute`), `library/favorites.js` (`REDUCED`) and `player/clock.js` (`easeOutCubic`/`REDUCED`, its *entire* `main.js` list) and `profile/screens.js` (`refreshLibraryTools`, also its entire list). All of these are now real imports from the new `shell/`/`util/motion.js` modules instead — `player/queue.js`, `player/clock.js` and `profile/screens.js` drop off rule 8's list completely as a result; `player/transport.js`, `library/edit.js`, `library/delete.js`, `library/credits.js` and `library/favorites.js` all shrink.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `runStartSequence`/`runPutAwaySequence`/`runGatefoldHandoff`/`runMorphOpen`/`openPlayerViaSheet`/`closePlayer` or any other moved function — confirmed by diffing every moved block against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions and import-path changes). **The iOS audio-gesture rules (rule 9) are untouched**: `bgPrime()` is still the literal first statement in `player/transport.js`'s `play()` (unchanged by this step — `play()` itself didn't move), and every one of `runStartSequence`'s/`runGatefoldHandoff`'s/`runPutAwaySequence`'s own `await sleep(...)`/`await waitAnimEnd(...)` calls, and the `S.sleeveSeq` cancellation-token checks after each one, moved verbatim.
- Added `tests/unit/shell.test.js` (6 tests): `easeOutCubic` (the curve's fixed points at 0/1, the documented formula at its midpoint, monotonically increasing across the range) and `shouldPutAway` (`auto && S.sleevePullEnabled && !REDUCED` — true only when all three hold; false for a manual skip even with the setting on; false when the setting itself is off). `util/motion.js`'s `REDUCED` is read once, at module load, from `window.matchMedia` — there's no browser `window` in Node, so this file stubs `globalThis.window = { matchMedia(){ return {matches:false}; } }` before importing anything, letting both `easeOutCubic` and `shouldPutAway` run as the genuine, unmocked code (the same "stub only what a browser would otherwise provide" approach `idb.test.js`/`prefs.test.js` already use) rather than a re-implementation — the REDUCED-true branch isn't separately exercised, the same precedent `favorites.test.js` already set for this exact flag. `shell/put-away.js`'s own non-pure imports (`mini-player.js` for `SLEEVE_ANIM`/`renderMiniPlayer`, `start-sequence.js` for `waitAnimEnd`/`sleeveArtFor`) are mocked directly, since an ES module evaluates its whole import graph regardless of which export is actually used, and `mini-player.js`'s real chain would otherwise reach `player/background.js` → `player/controls.js` → `storage/meta.js` → `audio/mode.js` — the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning. **Not separately tested, per the step 7 prompt's own "not much here is pure" escape valve**: `waitAnimEnd`, `sleeveArtFor`, `runStartSequence`/`runPutAwaySequence`/`runGatefoldHandoff` themselves, and the morph/hand-off geometry math — these are choreographed, DOM- and `requestAnimationFrame`-driven async sequences with no meaningful pure core beyond the two decisions above, and the Playwright scenario below is what actually exercises them end to end.
- Eleven *existing* test files needed a mock-chain fix of the same shape step 6b's/6c's/6e's extractions each required: `clock.test.js`, `controls.test.js`, `credits.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `edit.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `profile.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `search.test.js` and `tags.test.js` had each been mocking `updateShellAlbum`/`renderMiniPlayer`/`showRoute`/`closePlayer`/`clearSpinDown`/`runStartSequence`/`openPlayerViaSheet`/`shouldPutAway`/`putAwayInstant`/`runPutAwaySequence`/`REDUCED`/`refreshLibraryTools` as plain `main.js` exports (whichever subset each one's own real import chain actually reached) — all of these stopped resolving the moment the modules that need them started importing the real `shell/*.js`/`util/motion.js` instead. Each test moved the affected names into new, separate `mock.module` calls targeting the specific `shell/` file (or `util/motion.js`) that now owns them, the same "stop the chain at the door" pattern used throughout this suite — none of them needed to load any `shell/` module for real, since none of them test shell behavior itself (`shell.test.js`, above, is the one exception).
- Preview verification used three WAV fixtures carrying real RIFF `INFO` album/artist/track tags (so imports land in named albums, not "Unknown Album" — the pull/hand-off ceremonies deliberately skip untagged tracks). A Playwright script, run against both built targets:
  1. started a track playing (sheet → pull ceremony), confirmed the full player opened, closed it (`#playerDismiss`), confirmed the mini-player strip became visible, tapped it (`#miniTap`) to reopen via the morph, and closed it again — exercising both ways into the full player and back out.
  2. opened the crate's first album's gatefold (keyboard `Home`/`Enter` — crate cards carry no text, only a lazily-loaded art image, so navigation goes through the crate stage's own keyboard handler rather than guessing pixel positions) and played a track from it, then jumped to the crate's last album (`End`/`Enter`, guaranteed a different album) and played a track from *its* gatefold — a real gatefold-to-player hand-off away from whatever was already on the platter. Confirmed the hand-off ghost ended hidden, the real disc ended visible (not stuck `disc-hidden`), and the track name changed to the second album's track.
  3. queued a single short track with repeat off and let it play to its natural end, confirming the sleeve closed over the platter (`#sleeve` display `none`), the disc went `disc-hidden`, and the mini-player's playing indicator cleared — `runPutAwaySequence` end to end.
  Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target across all three scenarios. One result is headless-environment-dependent rather than a regression: after the hand-off, `S.playing` sometimes read `false` in this environment even though every visual landing step completed correctly — `startAudio()` (`play`) is invoked several `await`s and real seconds after the original tap, and headless Chromium's autoplay policy can decline to resume an `AudioContext` that far from the gesture it's nominally still inside, a stricter read of the same iOS rule (rule 9) this step was careful not to disturb. This predates the refactor (the `await` sequencing inside `runGatefoldHandoff` is byte-for-byte unchanged) and is exactly the kind of thing the device checklist, not a desktop browser, is the real check for.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (218/218 — 212 before this step + 6 new); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 94 warnings — unchanged from the pre-7b baseline; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets.

### Step 7c: launch intro and crate geometry/tiers/view

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Third of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — builds on 7a/7b.

- Five new modules, matching the step 7 plan's own scope for this step ("launch intro, crate geometry/tiers/view") — see their own repo-layout entries above for exact exports and imports: `src/crate/geometry.js` (`flowPose`/`flowZIndex`), `src/crate/tiers.js` (the "S4: crate image tiers" section), `src/crate/model.js` (the crate data model), `src/crate/view.js` (the crate-shelf rendering), and `src/launch/intro.js` (the launch intro sequence).
- **Two misleading-header splits, both flagged by step 7's own prompt as something to watch for across this step and 7d/7e:**
  1. **`main.js`'s "S4: crate image tiers" header covered two distinct concerns.** Image-tier building (`buildCrateTier`, `getCrateArtURL`, `deleteCrateArtTiers`, `healCrateArt`, `prewarmCrateAnchorL`, the `crateArtCache` LRU) went to `src/crate/tiers.js`; grouping/sorting albums into the shelf's own data model (`ensureCrateModel`, `invalidateCrateModel`, `isNowPlayingAlbum`, `persistCrateAnchor`, `crateRailJumpTarget`, `libraryHasGenres`) — which had only ever sat under that header by physical accident — went to `src/crate/model.js` instead.
  2. **`main.js`'s "S3: gatefold album view" header covered four things, not one.** The gatefold itself, the sealed-record sheet, `renderTracks` and the crate-shelf rendering code were all physically interleaved under it. Only the crate-rendering piece (`applyCrateMiniInset`, `renderCrate`, `centerEmptyState`, plus the module-private `openCrateAlbum`/`updateCrateGroupToggleUI`/`renderCrateFlat`) moved here, into `src/crate/view.js` — the gatefold and sealed-sheet code is 7e's territory, `renderTracks` is 7d's, and both stay in `main.js` for now, reached from `crate/view.js` as a temporary circular import (rule 8).
- **Two smaller judgment calls:**
  1. **`flowPose`/`flowZIndex` got their own module, `src/crate/geometry.js`, rather than living in either `launch/intro.js` or `crate/view.js`.** Both the launch overlay's card stack and the crate shelf call it, and CRATE spec §3 is explicit that this has to stay "one geometry implementation" — filing it under either consumer would have made the other import across a view-to-view boundary for no reason.
  2. **`toggleScratch` moved to `src/player/scrub.js`, not `launch/intro.js`.** It had been sitting in the old launch-intro section only because that's physically where the Character menu's Scratch toggle wiring landed; `scrub.js` already owns everything else about Scratch (F2), so this is where it belongs.
- `crate/tiers.js`'s `crateArtCache` and `launch/intro.js`'s `resolveLastPlayedAlbum` are the two names that cross the launch/crate boundary: `launch/intro.js` imports `crateArtCache` read-only (for `makeLaunchCard`'s own art lookup), and `crate/view.js` imports `resolveLastPlayedAlbum` from `launch/intro.js` (for the crate's own cold-start anchor). Neither import goes both ways, so neither is a circular pair — `launch/intro.js` and `crate/view.js` each depend on the other's sibling module, not on each other directly, except for that one one-directional `resolveLastPlayedAlbum` import.
- `libraryReadyResolve`/`libraryReadyPromise` (a `Promise`/resolver pair, previously two top-level `let`s in `main.js`) moved into `launch/intro.js` as module-private state, per refactor rule 7 (an importing module can't reassign an exported `let`): `main.js`'s two `libraryReadyResolve()` call sites in the boot sequence now call a new exported wrapper, `markLibraryReady()`, instead — the same "export a small setter function from the module that owns it" shape rule 7 itself prescribes.
- The six other modules already circularly importing the crate-tiers/crate-model/crate-view names from `main.js` were repointed to the new real modules in the same commit as the main.js wiring itself, so nothing was left stale: `library/import.js` (`invalidateCrateModel`/`healCrateArt`), `library/edit.js` (`deleteCrateArtTiers`/`buildCrateTier`), `library/delete.js` (`deleteCrateArtTiers`/`invalidateCrateModel`), `library/thumbs.js` (`invalidateCrateModel`), `shell/mini-player.js` (`applyCrateMiniInset`/`centerEmptyState`) and `shell/header.js` (`libraryHasGenres`) all now import these for real from `crate/tiers.js`/`crate/model.js`/`crate/view.js` instead of circularly from `main.js`. `library/thumbs.js`, `shell/mini-player.js` and `shell/header.js` each had only that one name on their own `main.js` list, so all three drop off rule 8's list entirely as a result. `storage/meta.js`'s `attachLaunchSettingRow` import was repointed the same way, to the real `launch/intro.js` — dropping `meta.js` off rule 8's list too, and creating a new real circular pair with `launch/intro.js` itself (`intro.js` imports `queueSave` back from `meta.js`), the same peer-import shape already established between `engine.js`/`transport.js` since step 3a. `crate/view.js` picks up the one new rule-8 entry this step adds, for the gatefold/sealed-sheet/`renderTracks` functions noted above.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `runLaunchSequence`/`ensureCrateModel`/`renderCrate`/`buildCrateTier`/`healCrateArt` or any other moved function — confirmed by diffing every moved block against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions, import-path changes, and the `markLibraryReady()` wrapper noted above).
- Added `tests/unit/crate-geometry.test.js` (6 tests: `flowPose`'s centered/undeflected/opaque base case, direction sign following `d`'s sign not its magnitude, the opacity falloff past `|a|=3` floored at 0, the speed-stretch making farther cards move more than near ones at the same speed, `opts.originY`/`opts.spread`, and `flowZIndex`'s floor past `|a|=9`) — needs no mocking at all, since `geometry.js` has zero imports. Added `tests/unit/crate-model.test.js` (6 tests: `libraryHasGenres`, `ensureCrateModel`'s one-record-per-album grouping and its dirty-flag/group-change rebuild gate, `isNowPlayingAlbum`, `crateRailJumpTarget`'s divider-to-first-record landing, `persistCrateAnchor`) — stubs `globalThis.window = {devicePixelRatio: 1}` for `crate/constants.js`'s top-level `CRATE_DPR` read (the same "stub only what a browser would otherwise provide" approach `shell.test.js` already uses) and mocks `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave`. Added `tests/unit/launch.test.js` (9 tests: `resolveLastPlayedAlbum`'s latest-play-wins-over-most-recently-added fallback and empty-library case, `resolveLastPlayedTrackIndex`'s within-album latest-play/first-track fallback and unknown-album case, `computeFlickPosition`'s exact start/end landing and monotonic increase, `flickSpeed`'s accel/cruise/decel ramp, `sampleStackAlbums`'s exact-cap sampling with its must-include id always kept) — stubs `globalThis.document` for the module's top-level `--launch-*` CSS custom-property writes and mocks `crate/tiers.js`'s `crateArtCache`/`storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave` directly rather than loading either's own real chain, the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning.
- Nine *existing* test files needed a mock-chain fix of the same shape step 6b's/6c's/6e's/7b's extractions each required, since `invalidateCrateModel`/`deleteCrateArtTiers`/`buildCrateTier`/`healCrateArt`/`libraryHasGenres`/`attachLaunchSettingRow` stopped being `main.js` exports: `clock.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `queue.test.js` and `tags.test.js` each added a direct `crate/tiers.js`/`crate/model.js` mock in place of the stale `main.js` one (`crate/model.js`'s own real chain reads `window.devicePixelRatio` via `crate/constants.js` at module load, which none of these have a browser for); `meta.test.js` added a `launch/intro.js` mock (providing just `attachLaunchSettingRow`) in place of its stale `main.js` one, since the real `intro.js` would otherwise pull in `crate/tiers.js`/`library/thumbs.js`'s own further real chains just to satisfy that one name.
- Preview verification (Playwright, both built targets): confirmed the launch intro resolves (or is skipped, first run) to the library route on a cold load with an empty library; imported four WAV fixtures and confirmed the empty-library placeholder hid; confirmed the crate view — the default library layout since an earlier step — rendered a real record with its artist/track-count info and a "now playing" chip with no console/page error; reloaded with the now-populated library (exercising `resolveLastPlayedAlbum`'s real, non-empty-library path end to end) and confirmed it still resolved cleanly to the library route; clicked the crate's current record and confirmed the gatefold (or sealed sheet, whichever applied) opened via `crate/view.js`'s circular `openGatefold`/`openSealedSheet` import, with no error. Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target.

Both sections' library-list code moved into the one new module together, confirming CLAUDE.md's own step-7c note that this split was coming.

### Step 7e: the gatefold and the sealed-record sheet

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Fifth of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — builds on 7a/7b/7c/7d. **Rule 8's module list goes to zero in this step** — see the confirmations at the end of this entry.

- Two new modules under `src/gatefold/`, matching the step 7 plan's own scope for this step ("gatefold + sealed-record sheet") — see their own repo-layout entries above for exact exports and imports: `view.js` (the gatefold itself, GATEFOLD spec §A2-A7) and `sealed-sheet.js` (the sealed-record sheet, SEALED spec §5).
- **The last piece of the misleading "S3: gatefold album view" header split**: that header had sat over four distinct things — the gatefold, the sealed-record sheet, the crate-shelf rendering (moved to `src/crate/view.js`, step 7c) and `renderTracks()` (moved to `src/library/view.js`, step 7d). This step moves the last two: the gatefold (`GF`, `isGatefoldOpen`, `openGatefold`, `closeGatefold`, `closeGatefoldInstant`, `updateGatefoldNowPlaying`, `updateGatefoldScale`, `gatefoldSwitchPanel`, and all the module-private rendering/gesture helpers) and the sealed-record sheet (`SEALSHEET`, `isSealedSheetOpen`, `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`, `openSealedSheet`, `closeSealedSheet`, `sealedQuietPlay`, `sealedHandoffPlay`, `closeSealedSheetInstant`) — plus `GATEFOLD_ANIM`, which had sat physically just *above* the old "S3" header (next to `main.js`'s other animation-timing constants) rather than inside it, but is gatefold code through and through and moved alongside the functions that actually use it, the same judgment step 7b's prompt made for `HANDOFF_ANIM`/`SLEEVE_ANIM`.
- **One split decision, explained where made**: the gatefold and the sealed sheet went into two files, not one, even though they share `buildGatefoldTracklist()`/`gatefoldTrackIndices()` (SEALED spec §5's own "the sealed sheet reuses this exact rendering") — `sealed-sheet.js` imports those two (plus `GF`, read-only) from `view.js`, a real one-directional import, not a circular pair, since nothing in `view.js` ever needs anything back from the sheet. Keeping them separate matches the repo's existing precedent of splitting a data module from its own sheet UI (`library/sealed.js`'s sealed-records *data* stays separate from this sheet's UI, by design — see that module's own updated entry above) and keeps each file's own job description to one sentence.
- Five modules that were circularly importing gatefold/sealed-sheet names from `main.js` since steps 3b/6b/7b/7c are repointed to the two new real modules: `player/transport.js` (`updateGatefoldNowPlaying`), `library/sealed.js` (`updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`), `crate/view.js` (`openGatefold`/`openSealedSheet`/`sealedQuietPlay`), `shell/routes.js` (`GF`/`closeGatefoldInstant`), `shell/handoff.js` (`isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant`). Each of these five had only gatefold-related names left on its own `main.js` list (the last one, in every case, per the running tally in rule 8's own module-list note) — **all five drop off rule 8's list entirely**, leaving it empty. `grep -rn "from '.*main.js'" src/` outside `main.js` itself confirms zero matches.
- `gatefold/view.js` picks up three real circular pairs as a result: with `player/transport.js` (it needs `play`; `transport.js` needs `updateGatefoldNowPlaying` back — the same shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js` since step 3a), with `shell/handoff.js` (it needs `runGatefoldHandoff`; `handoff.js` needs `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefoldInstant` back), and transitively with `library/sealed.js` through `sealed-sheet.js` (which needs `buildSealOverlay`; `library/sealed.js` needs `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak` back). `crate/view.js` and `shell/routes.js` become real, one-directional imports instead — neither `gatefold/view.js` nor `gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` needs anything back from either of them, so neither is a circular pair.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `openGatefold`/`closeGatefold`/`runGatefoldOpen`/`openSealedSheet`/`closeSealedSheet`/`sealedHandoffPlay` or any other moved function — confirmed by diffing every moved block (`GATEFOLD_ANIM` plus the full old "S3" gatefold/sealed-sheet content) against its pristine original lines (modulo only `export` additions, import-path changes, and the `gatefoldSideSplits` export addition below), and by a separate script-based normalization diff of the two new files' concatenated code against the exact original block, which came back identical but for that one export, the moved-file header comments, and the two sealed-sheet functions' position (both files' own content, not reordered within either file).
- `gatefoldSideSplits` gained `export`, previously private, for its own unit test — same precedent as `angleDelta` (`scrub.js`, step 3c) and `sampleStackAlbums` (`launch/intro.js`, step 7c).
- Added `tests/unit/gatefold.test.js` (6 tests: `gatefoldSideSplits`'s four branches — under 4 tracks, `S.albumSides` off, a single disc's midpoint split, multiple discs splitting by disc — plus `isGatefoldOpen`/`isSealedSheetOpen` both reading `false` with nothing open) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for its mocking approach and for why opening either overlay isn't separately unit-tested (no meaningful pure core beyond the one exported decision function; the Playwright script below is what actually exercises the open/close/hand-off choreography). Four *existing* test files (`clock.test.js`, `history.test.js`, `queue.test.js`, `sealed.test.js`) needed the same kind of mock-chain fix every prior step's extractions required: each had been mocking `main.js`'s `updateSealedSheetAfterBreak`, which stopped resolving once `library/sealed.js` started importing it for real from `src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` instead — each test moved that one key into a new, separate `mock.module` targeting `src/gatefold/sealed-sheet.js` directly (dropping the `main.js` mock entirely in `history.test.js`/`queue.test.js`, where it was the only key left on it), the same "stop the chain at the door" pattern used throughout this suite.
- Preview verification (Playwright, both built targets) used two WAV albums with real RIFF `INFO` tags — Album A (2 tracks, Artist A) and Album B (1 track, Artist B), both sealed on import (SEALED spec §2). A script: opened Album A's sealed sheet (first play — confirmed its title and 2-row tracklist), played a row (confirmed the sheet closed instantly — `closeSealedSheetInstant` — and the seal broke); reopened the crate's first record, now unsealed, confirming the *real gatefold* opened this time (correct title, a now-playing row marker present); switched panels via `#gfLeftCue` and back with no error; closed the gatefold, unsealed Album B the same way (confirming its own sealed sheet and tracklist, then that its track started playing); reopened Album A's gatefold while Album B's track was the one actually playing (confirming no now-playing row inside it, since it's a different album, and the correct title); then tapped a track row inside it to trigger the real gatefold-to-player hand-off (shell/handoff.js's `runGatefoldHandoff`) — confirmed the hand-off ghost ended hidden, the real disc ended visible, and the correct track from Album A started playing. Finally confirmed Escape closes the gatefold (`main.js`'s own keydown handler, calling `isGatefoldOpen`/`closeGatefold` from the real module now). Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target throughout.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (253/253 — 247 before this step + 6 new); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 93 warnings — unchanged from the pre-7e baseline (`src/gatefold/view.js` was added to `eslint.config.js`'s `MizikiSocial`-global block, for `gatefoldSocialControlsHTML`'s rating/note/pin calls); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets, confirming the gatefold, the sealed-record sheet, and the gatefold-to-player hand-off all still work exactly as before the move.

### Step 7f: wiring — `main.js` becomes a boot file

No behavior change — a move is a move, per the refactor rules above. Sixth of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — builds on 7a/7b/7c/7d/7e. **Step 7 is now complete except 7g.**

- **The design question this step had to answer first, since it's the one step 7 PR with no file-list table in the plan**: "wiring, split by area." Before writing anything, every single function the ~430-line wiring IIFE called was checked against the real exports of every module steps 3-7e had already produced — and every one of them was already a real, existing export of its owning module. That single fact settled the shape: rather than inventing new exports on ~20 existing feature modules just to give them their own "wire myself" method, this step adds one brand-new top-level directory, `src/wiring/`, with 13 small files, each exporting one `wireX()` function that does nothing but attach event listeners by calling functions that already exist. Zero changes were needed to any module outside `main.js` and the 13 new files themselves — the lowest-risk shape available for this step, and the one that keeps rule 8's module list at zero (none of the 13 import from `main.js`; `main.js` imports from all 13, one-directional, exactly as a boot file should).
- The 13 files — `transport.js`, `library-view.js`, `search.js`, `import.js`, `library-management.js`, `gatefold.js`, `sundown.js`, `character.js`, `edit.js`, `account.js`, `share.js`, `overlays.js`, `routes.js` — are described in full in the `src/wiring/` repo-layout entry above, including the two deliberate groupings (`account.js` combining profile-picture + social sign-in/nav since both are small "identity" concerns; `routes.js` kept separate from `library-view.js` since top-level route switching isn't a library-screen concern) and why the global Escape-key handler lives in `gatefold.js` (it closes the gatefold and sealed sheet first, in priority order, before the add menu or search field).
- `main.js` itself shrank from the ~10,100-line file this engagement started with to ~145 lines: the trimmed import block (just what `restoreLibrary()` and the boot sequence itself need, plus the 13 `wireX` imports), the boot sequence (`renderSearchChips()`, the 13 `wireX()` calls in the same order the original IIFE ran their bodies in, `setMode('vinyl')`/`fallbackSun()`/`askLocation()`/`drawSun()`/`updateChromeInset()` plus its resize listener, `requestAnimationFrame(frame)`, `restoreLibrary()`, and the launch-intro gate), and `restoreLibrary()` itself — unchanged, since it was already a top-level function in the original file, not part of the wiring IIFE.
- **One pre-existing dead import, found and removed as "obviously safe" cleanup per refactor rule 10** while rewriting `main.js`'s import block: `persistAlbumLookPref` (from `history/sessions.js`) had no remaining call site anywhere in `main.js`'s own code — confirmed via `grep -c "persistAlbumLookPref\b" src/main.js` returning `1` (only the import line itself) against the pre-7f file, so this was already dead before this step's own changes, not something 7f introduced. Dropped from the import line, no other change.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in the moved wiring code or in `restoreLibrary()` — confirmed two ways: (1) a full-content diff of the original wiring IIFE's body against the concatenation of all 13 new files' function bodies, after fully dedenting (`.strip()`-ing) both sides and comparing them as multisets of stripped lines (a naive partial-dedent comparison produces a flood of false positives, since the code moved from inside an IIFE's indentation context to inside 13 separate top-level functions) — exact match, 386 lines each side, zero missing, zero extra, once the two pieces deliberately *not* moved to any wiring file (the `renderSearchChips()` call and the short boot-sequence tail, both staying in `main.js` by design) were excluded from the comparison; (2) a separate line-anchored diff confirming the retained boot-sequence lines and `restoreLibrary()` are otherwise byte-for-byte identical to the original (the only differences being the 2-space de-indentation that naturally follows removing the old `(function init(){...})()` wrapper — no content changed).
- No test file needed a mock-chain fix this step, unlike every prior extraction: `main.js` already had zero entries on any test's mock lists as of step 7e (rule 8's list was already empty), and none of the 13 new `wiring/*.js` files are imported by any test — they're pure DOM-wiring glue with no pure core to test, the same "not much here is pure" reasoning step 7b's prompt used for the shell's own choreography.
- Preview verification (Playwright, both built targets) ran one consolidated script exercising representative actions across essentially every wiring area: booted the app with no console/page error, imported two WAV tracks (`import.js`), played/paused/shuffled/cycled-repeat/skipped next and previous (`transport.js`), switched to the Songs tab and opened/closed select mode (`library-view.js`), opened the header search field and typed a query (`search.js`), toggled Sundown's auto/manual row (`sundown.js`), switched Pure/Vinyl, applied a Character preset, and toggled Scratch (`character.js`), opened the edit sheet from the player (`edit.js`), opened/closed the queue sheet (`overlays.js`), opened the Now Playing share sheet and confirmed a canvas rendered (`share.js`), opened a crate record via keyboard and confirmed the gatefold or sealed sheet opened, then closed it with Escape (`gatefold.js`), opened and closed the clear-history overlay without triggering it (`library-management.js`), and switched to the profile tab and back via the tab bar (`routes.js`). The two genuinely destructive actions (Forget Library, Clear All History) were deliberately not triggered, only their overlay open/close wiring. Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (253/253, unchanged — this step added no new test file, since there was nothing new and pure to test); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 93 warnings — unchanged from the pre-7f baseline (the three `catch(err)` warnings now living in the new wiring files are the exact same three that existed in the original `main.js`, just relocated, not new warnings); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, 114 modules transformed); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets.

### Step 7g: lint-warnings cleanup, then `npm run lint` blocks on warnings too

Almost entirely a move — the actual rename of every deliberately-unused identifier to a leading-underscore name is a cosmetic, behavior-preserving change, per refactor rule 1's own "no behavior change" bar read literally (not a line moved, but nothing a user, or any test, can observe differently) — **except for one place where the mechanical rename briefly changed real behavior, caught and fixed before it shipped; see below.** Last of step 7's seven PRs (see the "Revised extraction order" note above, item 5) — builds on 7a/7b/7c/7d/7e/7f. **Step 7 is now fully complete.**

- The 93 warnings outstanding at the end of 7f (`npm run lint`'s own count, unchanged start-to-finish of steps 6a through 7f) fell into four groups, found by running ESLint with JSON output and categorizing every message by rule and by what kind of declaration it flagged:
  1. **~80 deliberately-ignored `catch` bindings** (`catch(e){}`, `catch(err){}`, two already-`catch(_)`) — by far the largest group, spread across almost every `src/` file, each one the same "bad input, truncated bytes, a storage write that failed, a browser API that isn't there — leave whatever was already true and move on" pattern this file's own "Also fixed in this PR" note under step 6a already named as the reason these were left alone back then. `eslint.config.js`'s `no-unused-vars` rule had `argsIgnorePattern`/`varsIgnorePattern` (both `^_`) but no `caughtErrorsIgnorePattern` — so a caught error could never be silenced by naming convention at all, args/vars-style, until this step added one. Fixed by adding `caughtErrorsIgnorePattern: '^_'` to the rule config and renaming every one of these bindings to a leading-underscore name (`catch(e)` → `catch(_e)`, `catch(err)` → `catch(_err)`); the two already named `_` needed no rename at all, since `_` already matches `^_`.
  2. **A handful of unused function parameters kept for a shared call signature** — `library/duplicate-scan.js`'s `gi`, `library/search.js`'s `mode`, `profile/storefront.js`'s `host`, and five callback parameters across `tests/unit/credits.test.js`/`search.test.js` (`i`, `label`, `sub`, `idx`, `onTap`) — each one named in this file's own step 6a "left as warnings" note as "kept for a shared call signature," not dead code to remove. Renamed to `_gi`/`_mode`/`_host`/`_i`/etc., the same convention as group 1 — the parameter stays (callers elsewhere in the same file, or the mock's own call shape, need the position), only its name changes.
  3. **Four unused local declarations in `src/miziki-social.js`** (`sleep`, `clone`, a destructured `fp`, and the function `spinKey`) — named in step 6a's own note as "left untouched... predates this engagement," not touched there since a safety-net PR wasn't the place for even a cosmetic rename across the social client's one source-of-truth file. Renamed the same way (`_sleep`, `_clone`, `_spinKey`) now that this step's whole purpose is clearing every warning — **except** the `fp` case, which needed care: it's destructured out of an object (`const { fp, ...rest } = it;`), so a plain textual rename to `{ _fp, ...rest }` would have looked up a property literally named `_fp` on `it` instead of `fp` — a real behavior change (`rest` would then still carry the real `fp` field, since nothing stripped it). Caught in review before this PR's own verification pass, before any test or build ran against it, and fixed as `{ fp: _fp, ...rest }` — destructures the real `fp` property, same as before, just binds it to an ignorable local name. Mentioned here, not buried, because it's the one place in this step where a "pure rename" tool briefly wasn't one.
  4. **One genuinely dead import**: `src/main.js`'s `current` (from `state.js`) — unlike every name in groups 1-3, nothing in `main.js`'s own remaining code reads it at all (confirmed by grepping the file), so prefixing it would have hidden dead code rather than explained deliberately-unused code. Removed from the import line outright, the same "obviously safe" cleanup this file's own history already has precedent for (steps 6a, 7d, 7f).
- Mechanical, scripted application: every one-off rename in groups 1-3 (92 of the 93 original warnings, all but the `current` import) was applied by a small script that read ESLint's own JSON output — file, line, exact column, and the flagged identifier's name — and inserted a single `_` at that exact column in that file, rather than a hand-written regex per file. This is what caught group 3's `fp` case early: the script's job was only ever "insert a character at this exact position," so the destructuring-semantics risk was something to check for afterward (by reading every resulting diff line, not by trusting the mechanism), not something the mechanism itself could have silently gotten wrong across 90+ sites. Confirmed by re-running `npm run lint` after the script (0 warnings before the two fixes below) and then reviewing every changed line in `git diff` by hand — the only two corrections needed were the `fp` destructuring fix above and a cosmetic double-underscore (`catch(__)` instead of `catch(_)`) on the two already-`_`-named bindings, which the script still "fixed" since it ran against ESLint's pre-config-change output; both reverted to the minimal, correct form before committing.
- Added `caughtErrorsIgnorePattern: '^_'` to refactor rule 10's own description of the lint rule set, and changed `npm run lint` from `eslint .` to `eslint . --max-warnings=0` in `package.json` — the one real tooling change in this step, and the step's actual point: from here on, a new unused import/var/arg/catch-binding left behind by a future extraction fails CI immediately, the same way an error already does, rather than silently joining a warning count nobody is required to keep at zero. Sanity-checked by temporarily adding two throwaway unused variables (one `_`-prefixed, one not) to a scratch file: `npm run lint` passed on the first, failed with "ESLint found too many warnings (maximum: 0)" on the second — confirming the flag actually gates, not just that the warning count happens to be zero today — then reverted the scratch change.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — nothing in this step touches a playback or persistence `await` sequence at all; the only occupied-vs-correct-behavior risk was the `fp` destructuring case above, and it's fixed.
- No new test file: nothing in this step is new, pure, and untested — every change is a rename of something a test (if one exists for that function) already exercises under its original behavior, and the two genuinely consequential files (`transport.js`'s `catch(_e)` renames, `miziki-social.js`'s `fp: _fp` fix) are both covered by existing suites (`tests/unit/*.test.js` for the former; `toWire()` itself is `miziki-social.js`-internal and reached only through `tests/client.test.js`'s Postgres-backed sync tests, not run in this environment, so this one fix leans on the destructuring-semantics argument above — provably equivalent to the original, not just "tests still pass" — rather than a fresh assertion).
- Preview verification (Playwright, both built targets): booted clean, imported two WAV tracks (exercising `library/import.js`'s renamed `catch(_err)`), played/paused/skipped next and previous (`player/transport.js`'s renamed `catch(_e)` in `stop`/`pause`/`seek`), toggled Background playback on and off (`player/background.js`'s/`audio/engine.js`'s renamed `catch(_e)`/`catch(_)` chain in `applyOutputRoute`/`ensureContext`), opened and closed the edit sheet (`library/edit.js`'s renamed `catch(_e)` around art-URL revocation), opened the Now Playing share sheet and confirmed a canvas rendered (`share/*.js`'s several renamed `catch(_e)` blocks around font-loading and art-drawing), and opened/closed select mode (exercising `library/delete.js`'s renamed `catch(_e)`). Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (253/253, unchanged); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings (down from 93 — see above) under the new `--max-warnings=0` flag; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, 114 modules transformed, identical bundle sizes to the pre-7g build, confirming nothing behavior-affecting changed); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets.

### After step 7g

Step 7 — the entire `main.js`-splitting engagement, steps 2 through 7 — is complete. `main.js` is a short boot file; every module under `src/` passes `npm run lint` with zero errors and zero warnings; `npm run lint` itself now fails on any new one. Account backup work (the long-term plan's next phase, per the top of this file) can start from a clean baseline.

### Step 01 (P1): security hardening

First of the post-refactor hardening/backup plan (see the roadmap README the user pasted in — "01" there, not part of the numbered extraction steps above). An Oct 11 audit found one real bug and three gaps; this PR fixes all four, documented in full in "Security" above — this entry is the how and the verification.

- **Fixed two real tag-injection bugs**, both the same shape: untrusted file-tag text (an artist/title the user doesn't control) concatenated into an `innerHTML` string, which would run as markup if a tag contained something like `<img src=x onerror=…>`. Once sign-in is live (step 04+), that code would run as the signed-in user.
  - `src/library/duplicate-scan.js`'s `renderDupScan()` built a `<div>`'s inner HTML from `title + ' — ' + artist` joined with `'<br>'`. Fixed by extracting a new `duplicateNamesElement(tracks)` — a small, pure-ish DOM builder (no imports beyond the bare `document` global) that appends real text nodes and a real `<br>` element instead, called from `renderDupScan()` the same way.
  - `src/launch/intro.js`'s title-screen code built `#launchTitleSub`'s inner HTML from `targetArtist + '<br><span>LAST PLAYED</span>'`. Fixed the same way, extracted as `renderLaunchTitleSub(subEl, artist)`.
  - Both extractions exist for testability, not because the fix itself needed a new function — a secondary benefit, not the point: `runLaunchSequence`/`renderDupScan` are otherwise expensive-to-mock async choreography, while the extracted builders need nothing but a fake `document.createElement`/`createTextNode`. Same precedent as `gatefoldSideSplits`/`angleDelta`/`computeFlickPosition` — a small decision or rendering function pulled out, `export`ed, for its own unit test.
  - The two remaining `innerHTML` uses in these same files (`'Mi<span>zi</span>ki'`, a static wordmark with no tag data in it) were left exactly as they were — not the bug, and explicitly the shape rule 2 below still allows.
  - Added `tests/unit/duplicate-scan.test.js` (2 tests) and two new tests in `tests/unit/launch.test.js`, each feeding the literal payload `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` as a title/artist and asserting (against a tiny fake DOM that keeps text-node data separate from element tags) that it survives as literal text and that no `<img>`-or-other element was ever created from it — a regression here fails by producing *empty* collected text, not by rendering the payload, since the fake DOM has no `innerHTML` parser to misuse in the first place.
- **Added an ESLint rule that makes this bug class impossible to reintroduce silently**: `no-restricted-syntax` (`eslint.config.js`), two selectors — assigning `innerHTML`/`outerHTML` with anything other than a `Literal` string, or calling `insertAdjacentHTML` with a non-literal second argument. Sanity-checked against a scratch file with both a violating and a compliant pattern before relying on it (the violating one errored, the compliant one didn't), then confirmed it's zero-error against the real codebase once the two bugs above were fixed, with the handful of pre-existing static-literal uses (`= ''`, the wordmark) all still passing. Added to `SAFETY_NET_RULES`, so it applies everywhere `no-undef`/`no-unused-vars` already do.
- **Bundled `@supabase/supabase-js`** (exact-pinned `2.117.3`, a real `dependencies` entry in `package.json` — the project's first) instead of loading it from jsDelivr. `main.js` dynamically `import()`s it only when both social keys are non-empty, passed to `MizikiSocial.init()` via its existing `supabaseLib` option; `init()` itself lost its `loadScript()`/CDN-fallback path entirely (a behavior change, deliberately — the whole point was no more unpinned CDN fetch) and now fails closed if no library is available. See "Security" above for the exact before/after bundle-size numbers (today: zero bytes, since Rollup proves the empty-keys branch dead; once configured: a separate ~231 kB/~60 kB-gzip chunk, fetched only then).
- **Self-hosted the three Google Fonts** as `woff2` + `@font-face` (`src/fonts/`, `src/styles/fonts.css`) — see each one's own repo-layout entry above for exactly which files and why only the `latin` subset. Removed the Google Fonts `<link>` and both `preconnect` tags from `miziki.html`.
- **Added the Content Security Policy and `referrer` meta tags** to `miziki.html`'s `<head>` — see "Security" above for the exact policy and the step-04 note about widening `connect-src`/`img-src` once real Supabase keys exist.
- No behavior change beyond the two bug fixes themselves (tag text now renders as text, which is the fix) and the CDN-fallback removal in `miziki-social.js`'s `init()` (dead code today, since keys are empty — the real behavior change is deferred to whenever step 04 actually configures Supabase).
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (257/257 — 253 before this PR + 4 new: `tests/unit/duplicate-scan.test.js` (2 tests) plus 2 new cases in `tests/unit/launch.test.js`); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`). Playwright, both built targets: booted clean; imported tracks (exercising blob: artwork/thumbnail URLs under the CSP's `img-src`/`media-src`); played and scratched a record (a direct `PointerEvent` dispatch past the 8° claim threshold, confirming `AudioWorklet.prototype.addModule` — spied from outside the page — resolved with zero `securitypolicyviolation` events, i.e. `worker-src 'self' blob:` actually covers the scratch processor); rendered a Now Playing share-image canvas; opened and closed the duplicate scan, a crate/gatefold record, and the profile tab (the social "not set up" state). Zero non-baseline console/page errors, zero CSP violations on either target throughout.
- **Not done here, and not Claude's to do**: the GitHub repo settings (Dependabot alerts, secret scanning, push protection, a branch-protection ruleset on `main` requiring the PR-checks workflow to pass) the hardening doc's own §"GitHub settings" section calls for — those are a five-minute github.com task for the repo owner, not a code change.

### Step 02 (F3): "Forget library" removes songs, never stats

Second of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents (see the roadmap README the user pasted in). A feature PR — it changes behavior on purpose, per F3's own framing: **"Forget library" removes your songs from Miziki and keeps your listening stats.**

- **The old behavior (step 5d) cleared the `tracks`, `meta`, `sessions`, `overlays` and `artwork` stores wholesale** — erasing play counts, sessions, metals, sealed state, playlists, edits and settings along with the library itself, keeping only achievements/Collection thumbnails via a manual copy-out-then-back-in dance across the `meta.clear()`. It also left several in-memory stats fields un-reset, which step 5d itself had already flagged as a latent write-back risk: a later save could write part of that half-cleared state back over whatever the (also-cleared) DB no longer had.
- **New behavior**: `forgetLibrary()` (`src/library/delete.js`) now clears only the `tracks` store, this run's crate-art tiers (`deleteCrateArtTiers`, per album), and thumbnails nothing else still references (`pruneThumbs()`, the exact mechanism `history/clear.js` already used for the same purpose — reused here, not reinvented). `meta`, `sessions`, `overlays`, `achievements` and `collection` are never touched. In memory, only the fields tied to the now-gone tracks are reset (`S.tracks`, `S.index`, `S.queue`/`S.baseQueue`/`S.qpos`, the decoded-audio cache `S.lru`, `S.curPlayed`/`S.curTrackDone`, the shell/platter state) — every stats field, every setting, playlists, album order/sort and `S.sealed` are left exactly as they were, which is both the fix for the write-back risk above (nothing resets, so there's nothing stale to write back) and the mechanism for reconnect (see below). "Clear listening history" (`history/clear.js`) stays the one place that resets stats — the two actions now mean clearly different things, matching the F3 doc's own framing.
- **Playlists and manual album order needed a representation change to support this.** Before this step, `S.playlists[].items` and `S.albumOrder[album]` held track *indices*, valid only for the session's current `S.tracks` shape — `saveMeta()`/`restoreMeta()` (`src/storage/meta.js`) converted them to/from ids at the storage boundary, and `restoreMeta()` silently dropped any entry whose id no longer resolved (`idxOf(id) < 0`) — the exact mechanism of the step 5d regression this doc's own framing calls out by name. Both now hold track **ids** everywhere, in memory and in storage alike: `saveMeta()`/`restoreMeta()` write/read them straight through with no conversion or dropping at all; `src/library/model.js`'s `albumTracks()` resolves `S.albumOrder[album]`'s ids to indices for display (filtered to the album's current membership) without ever removing an id that doesn't currently resolve; `src/library/favorites.js`'s `isFavorite`/`toggleFavorite` read/write `pl.items` by id directly, with no index lookup left in the module at all; and `src/library/view.js`'s playlist rendering, picking-mode "already picked" check, and both it and `renderAlbumDetail`'s manual-reorder buttons (`moverCtl`) all resolve ids to indices for what's shown, moving within the *full* id-based order (not just the currently-visible rows) so a hidden, not-yet-reconnected entry elsewhere in the same list keeps its own relative place. `src/library/delete.js`'s `deleteTracks()` — explicit, intentional removal, unlike Forget — now just `.filter()`s the deleted ids out of `S.playlists`/`S.albumOrder` directly, replacing the old snapshot-to-ids-then-rebuild-to-surviving-indices dance the index-based representation used to require.
- **Result: a forgotten track's playlist membership, manual order, favorite status, edits (`overlays`) and custom cover (`artwork`) all wait exactly where they are and reconnect automatically** the moment a file with the same id (`name|size|lastModified`) is imported again — `library/import.js`'s existing `loadOverlayFor(t)` call, already run for every newly-added track, is what re-applies the overlay/custom-art without any new code. A *partial* re-add (only some of a playlist's tracks come back) shows just the reconnected ones; the rest stay recorded, hidden, not dropped, exactly as the F3 doc's own test list describes.
- **Copy updated** to match: the Library tools "Forget library" row (`miziki.html`) now reads *"Removes every song from Miziki. Your stats, playlists and settings stay, and reconnect if you add the songs again. Files on your device aren't touched — to reset stats instead, use Clear listening history above."* There's no separate confirm *dialog* for Forget — the two-tap arm/confirm button (`#forget`, `src/wiring/library-management.js`) already serves as its own confirmation, unchanged by this step.
- **A real, incidental bug found and fixed along the way, not part of F3's own scope but caught during this step's own Playwright verification**: step 01's Content Security Policy shipped `connect-src 'self'` with no `blob:` — but `src/crate/tiers.js`'s `buildCrateTier()` and `src/library/thumbs.js`'s `makeThumb()` both `fetch()` a track's own `blob:` object URL to re-read its embedded cover art (or a user's custom cover) before resizing it. Every one of those fetches was being silently blocked by the browser and swallowed by its own `catch(_e){return false}`, so crate art and thumbnails never actually built for any file with embedded artwork, a regression invisible in step 01's own verification since its fixture tracks carried no embedded art. Fixed by adding `blob:` to `connect-src` in `miziki.html`'s CSP meta tag (see "Security" above) — caught and fixed before this PR merged, folded into this same PR rather than split out, since it was found mid-verification of this very step.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere in `deleteTracks`/`invalidateActiveSessionIfAny`/`persistSessionCounts`/`loadOverlayFor` or any other touched function beyond what the behavior change itself required (the removed `meta.clear()`/`sessions.clear()`/`overlays.clear()`/`artwork.clear()` calls in `forgetLibrary()`, and the simplified id-filter logic in `deleteTracks()`, replacing the old id↔index snapshot/rebuild).
- Added `tests/unit/forget-library.test.js` (5 tests) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for exactly what it covers and how it's mocked. `library-model.test.js`, `favorites.test.js`, `meta.test.js`, `delete.test.js`, `search.test.js` and `tags.test.js` all needed updates for the id-not-index representation change — see that same entry for the specifics; `delete.test.js`'s own two `forgetLibrary` tests moved to the new file outright, since the behavior they checked (whole-store clearing) no longer exists.
- Preview verification (Playwright, both built targets) used three WAV fixtures sharing an album tag, all with a fixed, pinned `mtime` (`fs.utimesSync`) so a later "re-import the same file" step could feed a byte-identical copy at a *different* OS path and reliably get a `change` event — Chromium's `setInputFiles` does not reliably re-fire `change` for the literal same path a second time, a known headless-testing quirk unrelated to the app, not a regression; the computed track id (`name|size|lastModified`) matches either way, which is what the app itself actually keys reconnect on. The script: imported all three, created a playlist with all three, favorited one track and edited another's title plus set a custom cover (both from the Songs tab, not from inside the playlist's own detail view — see the next paragraph), ran Forget library from Profile → Library tools (two-tap), confirmed the library emptied, re-imported the identical three files from the copied paths, and confirmed the playlist showed all three back in their original order (the edited title included), the Favorites playlist showed the favorited track, and the custom cover's `<img>` was present on the reconnected row. Zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target, confirming the CSP fix above actually resolved the blob: fetch failures.
- **A second, genuine but out-of-scope bug found during this same verification pass, left unfixed and flagged here rather than silently worked around**: `src/library/favorites.js`'s `ensureFavoritesPlaylist()` creates the system Favorites playlist, the first time it's ever needed, via `S.playlists.unshift(pl)` — but `S.view.playlist` (which playlist is currently open, in `src/library/view.js`) is a plain *index* into `S.playlists`, not an id or a reference. Favoriting a track for the first time while already viewing a *different* playlist's own detail screen shifts every existing playlist's index by one, so `S.view.playlist` ends up pointing at the wrong playlist (the newly-created Favorites, not whatever was actually open) until the user navigates away and back. This predates step 02 entirely (both pieces — `ensureFavoritesPlaylist`'s `unshift`, and `S.view.playlist`'s index-based addressing — existed before this step touched either file) and isn't something F3 asked to fix; the verification script above works around it simply by favoriting/editing from the Songs tab rather than from inside the Trial Playlist's own view, which is also the more common real-world path. Worth a small follow-up at some point (either stop unshifting, or give `S.view.playlist` a different kind of handle), but not this PR's job.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (260/260 — 257 before this step + 5 new in `forget-library.test.js`, net of 2 removed from `delete.test.js` (257 + 5 − 2 = 260) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for exactly what changed in each existing file); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright script above ran clean against both built targets once the CSP fix landed.

### Step 03 (B2): backup merge logic

Third of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents (see the roadmap README the user pasted in). Pure logic only, per that doc's own framing — no Supabase, no UI, no network, so **no iPhone testing is needed** this step, and the app is unchanged: `main.js`/`miziki.html` aren't touched at all.

- Added `src/sync/merge.js` and `src/sync/backup-doc.js` — see their own repo-layout entries above for the full export list and the reasoning behind each merge rule. In short: `merge.js` holds one small, separately-tested primitive per row of the key-inventory table above (sum/max/union/last-write-wins/history-clear-gate), composed by `mergeRows(rows)`; `backup-doc.js` holds the doc format (`SCHEMA_VERSION`, `toDoc`/`fromDocs`) and the one real piece of data-shape work this step does — converting `playlists`/`albumOrder` items and `overlays` between local file ids (`name|size|lastModified`, meaningless on another device) and the portable `trackIdentityKey`/`albumKey` the app already uses for duplicate detection and play counts.
- **The key-inventory table** (added to "How the app is put together" above, with the new "every new saved key must be added" rule) is this PR's actual first deliverable, per the doc's own instruction to do that *before* writing any merge code — every `meta` key and every store got checked against `src/storage/repo.js`, `src/history/`, `src/library/sealed.js`/`thumbs.js`/`favorites.js`, `src/profile/screens.js` and `src/state.js`'s defaults, not assumed from memory.
- **Three implementation decisions the doc left to this PR's own judgment, each made and explained in the table/repo-layout entry rather than guessed at silently:**
  1. **The `sessions` store (per-album in-progress play-through) is device-only, excluded from backup entirely** — not because it doesn't matter, but because it's resume state tied to one device's own physical timeline (a 4-hour gap, an in-order `tracksCompleted` list), and the only thing that's supposed to survive a session long-term — the completed-session count — is already `sessionCounts`, a backed-up, per-device-summed counter. Merging two devices' *in-progress* sessions could actually corrupt the 95%/in-order completion rule itself (whose `tracksCompleted`/`expectedOrder` would even apply?), so this is the one row where "read the spec, then decide" (the doc's own words) came out to "don't back this up at all."
  2. **Sealed records need a signal the local app doesn't keep yet.** `S.sealed` only ever records "still sealed" — once `breakSeal()` removes an id, there's no durable trace that it was *ever* sealed, so a backup doc built from `S.sealed` alone can't tell "broken on this device" apart from "this device never had the album." The doc's own `sealedAlbums`/`brokenSealIds` split in the key-inventory table is this PR's fix: `brokenSealIds` is new local state (not built yet — flagged in the table's own "doesn't exist locally yet" list) that a later wiring PR must start keeping, the same way `historyClearedAt` and per-field write timestamps are flagged there too.
  3. **"Last write wins" is per *field*, not per whole doc.** Each of `playlists`/`albumOrder`/`albumSort`/`albumDisplay`/`trackDisplay`/`albumEdge`/`albumLookPref`/`prefs`/profile name carries its own `{at, value}` in the doc (`backup-doc.js`'s `lw()` helper), and `merge.js`'s `lastWriteWins()` picks a winner field by field. A single whole-doc timestamp was the simpler option, but it would mean any device's later, completely unrelated change (say, a crate-anchor update) silently overwrites another device's earlier-but-still-newer-than-last-sync playlist edit just because its *doc* happens to be newer overall — exactly the kind of loss this PR exists to prevent. Tested directly (`backup-merge.test.js`'s "an unrelated, later change to one field never clobbers an earlier change to another field").
- **Counters without double counting (point 3)**: `fromDocs(ownLocal, remoteRows)` always rebuilds this device's own doc fresh from `ownLocal` via `toDoc()`, rather than trusting a possibly-stale copy of itself that might be sitting in `remoteRows` — any row sharing `ownLocal`'s own `deviceId` is dropped before merging, and the remaining rows are de-duplicated by `deviceId` too (a cheap safeguard against a server-side duplicate, not something B1's "one row per device" should ever produce, but free to guard against). `mergeRows()` itself only ever sums/maxes/unions across rows, so calling it repeatedly, or feeding it the same rows in a different order, never changes a total. The result's `totals.*` are the cross-device grand totals for *display only* (Top Songs, metal thresholds, Collection progress) — the comments on both `fromDocs` and `mergeRows` say explicitly that a caller must never write these back into `S.trackPlayCounts`/`S.sessionCounts`/etc., which have to keep holding only this device's own contribution, or the next upload would double-count what's already synced.
- **"Clear listening history" wins everywhere (point 4)**: `computeHistoryClearedAt`/`freshRows` in `merge.js` compute the newest clear across every row and exclude any row whose own `historyClearedAt` predates it from the counters/max-timestamps/`rareUnlocked`/`achievements`/`collection` merges — but never from `lastWrite`/`sealed`/`overlays`, none of which are "history" (sealed state is deliberately independent of listening history locally too, per SEALED spec §2). `fromDocs`'s `ownReset` flag tells the caller when *this* device is the stale one, so it knows to zero its own local counters and adopt the newer clear before its next upload — otherwise that device's next doc would still carry pre-clear numbers that every other device has, from that point on, started ignoring anyway.
- **Portability (point 2)**: `toDoc()` converts a playlist/`albumOrder` item's local id to its `trackIdentityKey` whenever the referenced track is currently present; an id that doesn't resolve (forgotten but kept, per F3) is kept as the bare local id rather than dropped — not portable to another device, but it still round-trips on the *same* device after a reinstall, the same file-identity match F3 itself already relies on. An overlay with no resolvable identity key is skipped outright (unlike a playlist item, an overlay can never be reapplied without knowing which track it belongs to, so there's nothing useful to preserve). `resolveIdentityKey(key, tracks)` is the one piece of forward-looking plumbing this PR adds for the *next* PR to use: it isn't called anywhere in `fromDocs` itself, since resolving a pending identity key to a real local id has to happen at apply time, against whatever's actually in the library then — a resolution computed once, at merge time, would go stale the moment the next file is imported.
- **A versioned doc format (point 5)**: `SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`, stamped by `toDoc()`. Preserving a future version's unknown top-level fields across a re-upload is the uploader's job, not `toDoc`'s — a later PR fetches this device's existing row, calls `toDoc(localState)` for the fields this version knows about, and shallow-merges over the existing row before uploading, so nothing this version doesn't recognize is ever dropped. `toDoc`/`fromDocs`/`mergeRows` are all defensive about missing/malformed input by construction (plain `|| {}`/`|| []` fallbacks throughout, no field ever assumed present) — tested directly in `backup-merge.test.js`'s robustness cases.
- Added `tests/unit/backup-merge.test.js` (22 tests) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for the full list. No existing test file needed any mock-chain fix: nothing in the suite previously touched `src/sync/`, and neither new module imports from `main.js` or anything DOM-dependent.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (282/282 — 260 before this step + 22 new); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; no build or Playwright verification this step, per the doc's own "no visible change" framing — `main.js`/`miziki.html` have zero diff.

### Step 04: Supabase project setup, then the keys PR

Fourth of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents. Two parts, per that doc: Ken set up the real Supabase project by hand (`docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md`, already merged) and applied migrations 001–004; this PR is the small part that connects the app to it. **This is the step where the social layer stops being inert** — everything 01–03 built (bundled client, the tag-injection/CSP hardening, the backup merge logic) existed in anticipation of this moment.

- `src/state.js`: `SOCIAL_SUPABASE_URL`/`SOCIAL_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` now hold the real project URL and its publishable key (`sb_publishable_…`), not the legacy JWT-shaped anon key Supabase is retiring at the end of 2026 — the comment above both was rewritten to say so, and to repeat (CLAUDE.md's own long-standing rule) that the **secret key** must never go here or in this repo at all.
- `miziki.html`'s CSP: added the project's `https://<ref>.supabase.co` to both `connect-src` and `img-src`, and `wss://<ref>.supabase.co` to `connect-src` — exactly the three additions step 01's own CSP comment and CLAUDE.md's "Security" section had already flagged as needed once real keys landed. Nothing else in the policy changed.
- **Verified the bundled client activates correctly, not via a CDN, with zero CSP violations** — both via the unit/build checks below and a dedicated Playwright pass against the Netlify-base preview:
  1. Confirmed the lazy `index-*.js` chunk (supabase-js, ~231 kB/~60 kB gzip — exactly the size step 01's own note predicted "once configured") is what gets requested once the profile/social route needs it, and that zero requests ever go to jsDelivr/unpkg or any other CDN.
  2. Confirmed zero `Content-Security-Policy`/"Refused to connect" console messages while loading the app and opening the social route.
  3. **A direct CSP check, not just an absence of errors**: from inside the page, attempted a real `fetch()` to `https://<ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1/health` and a real `WebSocket` to `wss://<ref>.supabase.co/realtime/v1/websocket` — both reached the network layer with zero CSP violations (they failed only on this sandbox's own outbound-proxy restriction for that host, `ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED`, an environment limitation having nothing to do with the app). A control fetch to a host deliberately *not* in `connect-src` (`https://example.com`) produced the real thing for contrast: two explicit "Refused to connect... Content Security Policy" console messages — confirming the harness can tell the difference, and that the Supabase origins really are allowed, not just untested.
- Updated `docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md` step 5: marked the keys as now in place for this project, and added a note that repeating this setup for a different project also needs the CSP widening (with a pointer to CLAUDE.md's "Security" section for the exact policy) — the checklist itself hadn't mentioned the CSP step at all before this PR.
- No behavior change to anything *except* the social layer itself switching from inert to live — no refactor, no new module, no touched test file. `main.js` is untouched; the only files this PR changes are `src/state.js`, `miziki.html` and the two docs noted above.
- Verified: `npm run test:unit` passes (282/282, unchanged — nothing here is covered by a unit test, since both changes are literal config values and a meta tag); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`), each confirming the separate ~231 kB supabase-js chunk; the Playwright script above ran clean against the Netlify-base preview — zero CSP violations, zero non-baseline console/page errors.
- **Not done here, per the doc's own "after-merge check"**: Ken doesn't sign in on the real GitHub Pages app yet — that's deferred until step 06 merges, so the first real sign-in also creates the first backup rather than an account with nothing behind it yet. The Netlify preview is where the real sign-in flow (6-digit code, handle claim) gets tried first, by Ken, by hand — not something this PR's own automated verification does, since it would mean sending a real email through the live project.

### Step 05 (B1): account backup table, reserved handles, account deletion — server side only

Fifth of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents. A server-side-only feature PR, same shape as step 03's B2: nothing in `main.js`/`miziki.html`/`src/` (outside `src/miziki-social.js`'s own small addition) changes — the deliverable is `supabase/migrations/005_backup.sql` plus the test coverage proving it does what it claims. **No iPhone testing is needed this step**, same reasoning as step 03. **This PR must not be merged until Ken applies `005_backup.sql` to the live Supabase project's SQL editor and re-runs the Security Advisor scan** — see "Before merging (Ken)" at the end of this entry; everything else below is already verified against a local Postgres instance.

- **`public.library_backup`**: one row per `(user_id, device_id)` — `device_label` (≤60 chars), `schema_version`, `doc jsonb` (≤2MB, enforced by a `check` on `octet_length`), `updated_at` (via the existing `touch_updated_at()` trigger). Owner-only RLS on all four operations (`user_id = auth.uid()`), `revoke all ... from anon`. The server never reads inside `doc` — every merge rule from step 03's key-inventory table is enforced entirely client-side, in `src/sync/merge.js`/`backup-doc.js`, and (since step 06, B3) actually called from `src/sync/backup.js`. See "Backup: what gets synced" above, and "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" below, for the full privacy-rule writeup.
- **Reserved handles**: a `before insert or update of handle` trigger on `public.profiles` (`profiles_handle_not_reserved`) rejects `admin`, `administrator`, `support`, `help`, `miziki`, `official`, `security`, `moderator`, `mod`, `staff`, `team`, `root`, `system`, `null`, `undefined` (case-insensitive) with `'That handle is reserved'`. A trigger, not a validated `check` constraint, specifically so only *future* writes are checked — nothing already claimed before this migration is retroactively affected, per the doc's own explicit requirement. `src/miziki-social.js` got a matching `RESERVED_HANDLES` `Set`, wired into `checkHandle` (returns `{ok:true, available:false, reason:'reserved'}`), `createProfile` and `updateProfile` (both: a client-side pre-check plus, as a backstop, catching the server's own `P0001` error code the trigger raises) — the client check is the fast, offline path; the trigger is the real enforcement, since nothing stops a request from skipping the client entirely. The trigger function itself has `set search_path = ''` (the same fix as `touch_updated_at()` below) and `execute` revoked from `public`/`anon`/`authenticated` — a trigger function is invoked by the system, not called directly by any role, so no role ever needs to execute it.
- **`public.delete_my_account()`**: a `security definer` RPC, `set search_path = ''`, that deletes the caller's own `auth.users` row (`where id = auth.uid()`, raising if not signed in) and relies entirely on existing `on delete cascade` foreign keys — `profiles` and everything that cascades from it (`follows`, `blocks`, `crate_items`, `verified_stats`, `now_spinning`, `digging_list`, `feed_events`, `reports`), plus `library_backup`'s own cascade — to remove every other row the account ever wrote. Owned by `postgres` (the role every migration runs as), which already has the grants a hosted Supabase project needs to delete from `auth.users` directly — the standard, documented shape for in-app account deletion. Irreversible; can only ever delete the caller's own account, never anyone else's, since there's no parameter to target a different id. Nothing in the app calls this yet — wiring a "delete my account" button into Settings is a future PR; this migration exists so that PR has an RPC to call.
- **Security Advisor fixes (Oct 12 scan)** — see the new "Security" section bullet above for the full writeup: moved `is_blocked_between`/`item_visibility`/`effective_visibility`/`can_view` into a new, API-invisible `private` schema (RLS policies keep working unchanged, since they bind by OID not name; every function *body* that called one of the four by name — `can_view` itself, `follows_before_insert`, `crate_items_emit_events`, `verified_stats_emit_events`, `set_now_spinning`, `digging_page` — was re-declared with the `private.`-qualified reference); fixed `releases_insert`'s `with check (true)` by adding `releases.created_by default auth.uid()` and changing the policy to `with check (created_by = auth.uid())`; fixed `touch_updated_at()`'s mutable `search_path` with `set search_path = ''`. "Leaked password protection" doesn't apply — no passwords, only emailed one-time codes.
- **No realtime publication changes**: `library_backup` is private, owner-only data, not a social/live-presence signal like `now_spinning` — it was never added to the realtime publication, and the migration's own closing comment says so explicitly rather than leaving it unaddressed.
- Extended `tests/client.test.js` with 14 new tests (36 total, up from 22): `library_backup`'s own CRUD + cross-user isolation + size-limit rejection (5 tests, using new `asUser`/`asAnon` helpers — `set role` plus `set_config('request.jwt.claim.sub', ...)` inside one `psql` invocation, the same "read the last line of multi-statement `-At` output" convention the existing `sb.rpc()` helper already uses); reserved handles rejected on both insert and update, with existing rows (`ken`) confirmed untouched (2 tests); the four moved functions now resolve in `private` not `public` via a `pg_proc`/`pg_namespace` lookup, `anon` gets a real `permission denied` calling any of them directly, `authenticated` keeps direct execute (3 tests — the most honest set of signals available in a harness with no real PostgREST layer in front of it: a real Postgres permission check for `anon` is the exact mechanism production relies on; the schema-location check is the honest stand-in for "not exposed via the Data API," since there's no PostgREST proxy here to prove that against directly); `releases.created_by` attribution, both the happy path (`sync_crate` as the real caller) and the rejected spoofing attempt (3 tests); `delete_my_account` removing the caller's `auth.users`/`profiles`/`follows`/`library_backup`/`crate_items` rows while leaving every other user's rows untouched, and confirming it can only ever target the caller's own account (2 tests, the first using a disposable fixture user with its own `crate_items` row — added a `releases` row and a `crate_items` row for that user specifically so the test's own name, "removes the caller's auth user, profile, crate, follows and backup rows," is actually true of what it checks, not just the stats/profile/follows/backup subset).
- **Bootstrapped a local Postgres 16 instance for this session** (`initdb`/`pg_ctl` under the `postgres` system user, matching `tests/client.test.js`'s own `PGHOST=/var/tmp/pgmz`/`PGPORT=5544` defaults) — the first time in this entire engagement that `npm test` (as opposed to `npm run test:unit`) was actually run, since no step before this one needed a real Postgres. **Lesson for future verification**: `tests/client.test.js` is a stateful script against a persistent database with no automatic reset — recreate it fresh (`dropdb && createdb`, then reapply `tests/supabase_stub.sql` and every migration in order) before trusting any run; re-running against a database a previous run already mutated produces false-looking failures from leftover rows, not real regressions (caught and confirmed harmless once during this step's own verification).
- Verified: `npm test` passes (36/36, against a freshly recreated database with all five migrations applied in order); `npm run test:unit` passes (282/282, unchanged — nothing in `src/` outside `miziki-social.js`'s reserved-handle check changed, and no unit test touches that file); `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings. No build or Playwright verification this step, same reasoning as step 03 — this is server-side schema and test coverage only; `main.js`/`miziki.html` have zero diff.

**Before merging (Ken):**
1. Apply `supabase/migrations/005_backup.sql` to the live project via the SQL editor (`docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md`'s own step-by-step, now with a dedicated section for this migration).
2. Re-run the Security Advisor scan and confirm the four `security definer`-in-`public` findings this migration fixes are gone.
3. Only then merge this PR.

### Step 06 (B3): backup in the app — sign-in, sync, Account and Privacy screens

Sixth of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents. The first app-side PR to actually read from and write to `library_backup` (migration 005, step 05) — everything steps 01–05 built (the bundled client, the tag-injection/CSP hardening, the pure merge logic, the Supabase project itself, the empty table) existed in anticipation of this moment, the same way step 04 was "the social layer stops being inert."
