This one changes real app behavior (new screens, a background sync loop) and touches real server data for a signed-in user, so — per the task's own instruction — **this PR must not be merged** until Ken has verified it by hand; the full "Preview focus (Ken)" list is at the end of this entry.

- **The sync engine, `src/sync/backup.js`** (new) — see its own repo-layout entry above for the full export list and the two deliberate "never import this" rules it follows. In short: `scheduleBackupSync()` debounces ~30s (piggybacking `storage/meta.js`'s existing `queueSave` cadence rather than inventing a second one), `flushBackupSync()` pushes immediately (called from `src/wiring/lifecycle.js`'s new `visibilitychange`/`pagehide` handlers on backgrounding), and `pullBackupOnForeground()` pulls-and-merges on launch/foreground — all three are plain synchronous entry points with their own internal `async` work, never awaited by anything, so sync can never block launch, play, or a tap-to-play (point 2 of the spec). Offline-safe with the same backoff shape (`[15000, 30000, 60000, 120000, 300000]`) `src/miziki-social.js`'s own sync engine already uses — a failed push only ever sets `lastError`, never flips the UI's "backed up" state back off for a transient blip.
- **Four local-state prerequisites step 03's own doc flagged as missing now exist** (all added in step 06, three in `src/state.js`, one in `src/library/sealed.js`/`src/library/edit.js`): `S.deviceId`/`S.deviceLabel` (generated once, persisted, the `library_backup` row key), `S.historyClearedAt` (stamped by `src/history/clear.js`'s full-scope clear), `S.sealedBroken` (a `Set` recording every album ever sealed-then-broken on this device, persisted by a new `persistBrokenSeals()` alongside the existing `persistSealedAlbums()`), `S.backupUpdatedAt` (one timestamp per per-field-last-write-wins key, stamped by a new `touchBackupField(name)` at each field's real mutation site), and per-overlay `editedAt`/`revertedAt` timestamps (`src/library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit`/`revertTrack`/`undoLastEdit`, the last two now writing a `{fields:null, reverted:true, revertedAt}` tombstone instead of deleting the overlay row outright — see "Backup: what gets synced" above for why that's safe for local-only use too). `src/library/import.js`'s `addFiles()` calls a new `reconcileImportedTrack(t)` on every newly-added track, resolving any pending cross-device playlist/albumOrder/overlay entry that was waiting on this exact identity key.
- **How it behaves, matching the spec's own point 1**: signed out, nothing is ever uploaded (`scheduleBackupSync`/`flushBackupSync` both resolve a null Supabase client and return before touching `meta` or the network). Signing in and picking a handle (the existing social sign-in flow, `src/profile/settings.js`) now pre-fills the handle field from the local profile's username (`handleFromUsername()`) and runs an immediate debounced availability check, with the status line distinguishing "taken" from "reserved" from "not quite a valid handle" rather than one generic error. First sign-in on a device that already has local data merges via `fromDocs()` — never overwrites — and `src/sync/backup.js`'s `initBackupSync()` builds a restore summary (`buildRestoreSummary()`) the first time a given account's data is actually merged in (guarded by `S.backupRestoredFor` so it only fires once per account per device). Music re-added later reconciles pending entries via `reconcileImportedTrack` above.
- **Never breaks playback (point 2)**: confirmed by what's *absent*, not just what's present — `grep -rn "sync/backup" src/player/ src/audio/` outside this PR's own `library/import.js` touch point returns nothing; `player/transport.js`'s `play()` is untouched by this step, with `bgPrime()` still its literal first statement before any `await`, exactly as every prior step's own iOS-gesture confirmation describes (rule 9).
- **The Account screen** (point 3, in Profile, `src/profile/settings.js`'s rewritten `renderSocialSettingsScreen`, relabeled "Account" from "Settings" in `screens.js`'s `labels` map): @handle + email (`MizikiSocial.getMyProfile()` + `getClient().auth.getUser()`), a "Backed up · X ago" / "Waiting for connection" line (`getBackupStatus()`), a device list (`listBackupDevices()`), Sign out (`{scope:'local'}`, now explicit rather than relying on the library default) and Sign out everywhere (`{scope:'global'}`, a new `signOutEverywhere` on `MizikiSocial`), and Delete account — type-the-handle-to-confirm, calling a new `MizikiSocial.deleteAccount()` (the `delete_my_account()` RPC from migration 005, finally wired to a UI control after sitting unused since step 05), which resets local social state and signs out.
- **The Privacy screen** (point 4, `src/profile/privacy.js`, new — linked from both the profile home and the Account screen): the visibility picker (private/friends/public, default friends), share-now-spinning/share-milestones toggles (default **off** — moved here from the old Settings screen, where point 4 of the spec actually asks for them to live), follow requests, blocked accounts with unblock (a new `listBlocked` on `MizikiSocial`), the backup status line, the Download-my-data button, and the exact required sentence verbatim: *"Your listening history is backed up privately to your account. Your music and your location never leave this device."* Signed out, the social controls are replaced by a calm prompt, matching the existing "not set up" posture the rest of the profile/social screens already use.
  - **One deliberate design choice, made to avoid a real bug**: `wirePrivacyLink(host, renderProfile)` takes `renderProfile` as a parameter rather than importing it from `./screens.js`. `screens.js` already has a real 3-way circular cluster with `profile/storefront.js`/`profile/settings.js`; routing a fourth edge through `privacy.js` as well — on top of `privacy.js`'s own real import of `sync/backup.js` — was, in practice during this step's own test runs, enough additional surface to transitively reach `library/view.js`'s whole seven-circular-pair web through `record-art/looks.js` ↔ `history/sessions.js` ↔ `library/thumbs.js` ↔ `library/edit.js`. Passing the already-imported function in from each of the two call sites (`screens.js`'s own `renderProfileHome`, `settings.js`'s `renderSocialSettingsScreen`) avoids the edge entirely rather than trying to mock around it everywhere it would have been reached.
  - **A second instance of the same class of bug, in `backup.js` itself**: the sync engine's own `applyMerged()` logically wants to re-render the library and refresh the current disc's look after a pull, but importing `library/view.js`'s `renderTracks` or `record-art/looks.js`'s `applyDiscVariant` directly from `backup.js` hit the identical chain-explosion during this step's test runs (traced through `record-art/looks.js` ↔ `history/sessions.js` → `library/thumbs.js` → `library/edit.js` → `library/view.js`). Fixed the same way as every circular-import problem this engagement has hit before, just with an event instead of a direct call this time: `backup.js` exports a tiny `onBackup(evt, fn)` emitter and calls `emit('applied')`; `main.js` — which already safely imports both `renderTracks` and `applyDiscVariant` for its own boot sequence — is the one listener (`onBackup('applied', () => { renderTracks(); const t = current(); if(t) applyDiscVariant(t, true); })`), added right after `MizikiSocial.init()` in `restoreLibrary()`.
- **Download my data** (point 5, Privacy screen): `collectDownloadData()`/`collectLocalDownloadData()` (`src/sync/backup.js`) build a `miziki-data-YYYY-MM-DD.json` export — local stats/settings/playlists/track-edit text always (explicitly no audio, no images, in any case), plus an `account` section (profile, backup status, device list) only when signed in. Works fully signed out, per the spec's own requirement — confirmed by a dedicated test (below) asserting the exported object has no `account` key at all when there's no social client.
- **Docs (point 6)**: added a new top-level "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" section above, right before "Security" — a table mapping every server-side table/column to who can see it, written for the privacy policy and App Store privacy label rather than for a developer, plus the Download-my-data cross-reference. Rewrote "Backup: what gets synced" and its "doesn't exist locally yet" list to say what now exists and where, since this step is the "later PR" both were written to wait for.
- **Tests (point 7)**:
  - `tests/unit/backup-sync.test.js` (new, 7 tests): the debounce collapsing several `scheduleBackupSync()` calls into one push, a no-`MizikiSocial` no-op, `flushBackupSync()`'s immediacy, the backoff retry sequence (confirmed against the real `BACKOFF[0]`/`BACKOFF[1]` values, not hardcoded numbers), two Download-my-data cases (signed-out local-only export — `schemaVersion` present, no `account` key, no `tracks` key, but `overlays` present, confirming the "no audio/images, but text edits are fine" line is actually true of the real output — and the synchronous `collectLocalDownloadData()` variant), and that `deviceId` is generated once and persisted, not regenerated on a later push. Uses `node:test`'s `mock.timers.enable({apis:['setTimeout']})` plus a `flushMicrotasks()` helper (real `setImmediate`, repeated, to let pending `await`s inside the mocked-timer callback actually settle) and a tiny in-memory `meta`/`overlays` stand-in for `storage/repo.js` — the same "stop the chain at the door" reasoning as every other mock-heavy file in this suite, chosen specifically because `backup.js`'s own real imports (`state.js`, `storage/prefs.js`, `record-art/tiers.js`, `library/model.js`, `backup-doc.js`) are all pure, so nothing else needs mocking. Since step 07 (B4), the `storage/repo.js` mock also gained in-memory `artwork`/`profile` stand-ins (needed once `backup.js` itself started importing both for the image download/upload paths), and the file's destructured import gained `orphanedFiles` — confirmed directly, via a plain Node `import()` of `src/sync/backup.js` with no mocking at all, that its whole real import chain (now also `crate/tiers.js` and `sync/images.js`) stays DOM-free, which is why neither of those two needs its own mock here. 5 new tests cover `orphanedFiles(files, referencedHashes, keepNewest)`: an unreferenced file is returned for deletion at `keepNewest=0`; a referenced file never is, even at `keepNewest=0`; `keepNewest=1` spares the single newest *unreferenced* file (one with only a single unreferenced candidate keeps it, matching the real "avatars keep at most one" rule); a referenced file is correctly excluded from consideration even when it would otherwise be the newest; and an empty input is just an empty result.
  - `tests/unit/images.test.js` (step 07, B4, new, 4 tests) — the one pure, DOM-free piece of `src/sync/images.js`: `sha256Hex` produces the identical hex string for identical bytes (twice, and via a Blob-like object's own `arrayBuffer()` method, confirmed to match the plain-`ArrayBuffer` result), a different hash for different bytes, and the well-known SHA-256 hash of an empty input as a literal test-vector check. `prepareCoverUpload`/`prepareAvatarUpload`'s own canvas re-encode isn't tested here, for the same reason `share/styles.js`'s canvas drawing isn't — no DOM in this test suite — exactly as the file's own header comment says.
  - `tests/client.test.js` (extended, 2 new tests, 38 total): a genuine two-simulated-device run against the real Postgres harness — device A signs in and pushes a play (`trackPlayCounts`); device B's first-ever merge (`fromDocs`) picks it up (total: 1); device B pushes its own play; device A's next merge sees both (total: 2); re-merging the same rows again never inflates the total — and a full-scope "Clear listening history" on one device producing `ownReset: true` on the other once merged, correctly excluding the stale pre-clear count from the merged totals. Uses the real `toDoc`/`fromDocs` (`src/sync/backup-doc.js`) and `trackIdentityKey` (`src/record-art/tiers.js`), not a reimplementation, against real `library_backup` rows.
  - **Two real bugs caught by this step's own test-writing, not by a later review pass**: (1) `touchBackupField` was originally placed in `storage/meta.js`, next to `saveMeta` — but every module that calls it (`library/thumbs.js`, `history/sessions.js`, `profile/screens.js`) already reaches wide, DOM-adjacent chains of its own, and importing it from `meta.js` would have added a brand-new edge into `audio/mode.js`/`audio/engine.js`/`sundown/location.js`/`player/controls.js`/`player/background.js`/`launch/intro.js` for a dozen *existing* test files that had nothing to do with backup at all. Moved to `src/state.js` instead, which every one of those six call sites already imports `S` from — zero new edges. (2) a Postgres test fixture wrote `{ SONG_KEY: 1 }` where `SONG_KEY` was meant as a computed key (`trackIdentityKey(...)`'s return value) — plain JavaScript object-literal syntax, so this created a property literally named the four characters `"SONG_KEY"`, not one named by the variable's value, and the merged total silently read back `undefined`. Fixed to `{ [SONG_KEY]: 1 }` (computed-property syntax) in all three occurrences, caught by the test's own assertion failing rather than passing silently — a reminder of why the test was worth writing in the first place.
  - A large number of *existing* `tests/unit/*.test.js` files needed small mock-chasing fixes of the same shape every prior step's extractions have required — adding `touchBackupField(){}` to existing `storage/meta.js` mocks, a new `sync/backup.js` mock (`scheduleBackupSync(){}`/`reconcileImportedTrack(){}`) wherever a real chain now reaches one, and a `getClient(){ return null; }` added to any pre-existing `globalThis.MizikiSocial` test stub that `backup.js`'s defensive `getSocial()`/`getClient()` accessors would otherwise find incomplete. `tests/unit/edit.test.js` got two genuine assertion rewrites (not just mock patches) to match the new overlay-tombstone shape: a reverted/undone-with-no-prior-overlay edit now reads back `{fields: null, reverted: true}` from the `overlays` store instead of the row being gone entirely.
- **Reserved handles' `src/miziki-social.js` note updated**, and three new methods added to its public API for this step: `getClient()` (the small accessor `backup.js` uses instead of constructing a second Supabase client, per the task's own explicit instruction), `signOutEverywhere`, `deleteAccount`, `listBlocked` — see that module's own repo-layout entry above.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (289/289 — 282 before this step + 7 new in `backup-sync.test.js`); `npm test` passes (38/38, against a freshly reseeded Postgres database with all five migrations reapplied in order — see step 05's own "Lesson for future verification" note, re-confirmed necessary again this step). Both builds (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`) and Playwright preview verification are the remaining items before this PR can be marked ready for review — see the PR description itself for their results, since they run after this changelog entry is written.

**Preview focus (Ken) — please verify by hand before merging:**
1. Sign in on a fresh device/browser profile with no local library, then again on one with existing local stats/playlists — confirm the second shows a restore summary and never silently overwrites what was already there.
2. Play a track on two different signed-in sessions (or two tabs) for the same account and confirm play counts sum rather than one overwriting the other (the same invariant `tests/client.test.js`'s new two-device test checks, but against the real client flow, not the harness).
3. Turn off network (airplane mode or devtools offline) mid-session, make a few changes, turn it back on, and confirm sync catches up without an error dialog.
4. Open the Account screen, confirm the device list and "Backed up · X ago" line look right; try Sign out vs. Sign out everywhere from two sessions.
5. Open the Privacy screen, flip visibility and the two share toggles, and confirm Download my data produces a real, readable JSON file both signed in and signed out.
6. **Do not use "Delete account" on a real account you want to keep** — it is irreversible and calls the real RPC against the live Supabase project; verify it only with a disposable test account.
7. Confirm no CSP violations or console errors anywhere in this flow, against both built targets (GitHub Pages base and Netlify's `/` base).

### Step 07 (B4): custom covers and profile pictures

Seventh of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents. Builds on B1–B3 (steps 03/05/06), all merged and working. A feature PR, same posture as step 06: real images now travel to and from a real Supabase project for a signed-in user, so — per the task's own instruction — **this PR must not be merged until Ken applies `006_images.sql` to the live Supabase project**; see "Before merging (Ken)" at the end of this entry.

- **Point 1 — confirmed, by reading the code, exactly which images get backed up and which never do.** The local `artwork` store mixes two genuinely different things: a *custom* cover a user picks in the edit sheet (`src/library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit()`, stored keyed by the track's `albumKey`) and *generated* images — crate-view tiers (`src/crate/tiers.js`) and `thumb:*` thumbnails (`src/library/thumbs.js`) — both rebuilt locally from whatever source art (embedded, or a now-synced custom cover) happens to be on the device, never something a user set directly. Only the first kind is backed up. Embedded artwork isn't uploaded either — it already comes back with the file the moment it's re-imported, so backing it up would just be a second, pointless copy of something the file itself already carries.
- **Point 2 — two Storage buckets, different privacy, via `supabase/migrations/006_images.sql`**: `covers` (private, `{user_id}/{sha256}.jpg`, owner-only read/upload/delete, 1 MB cap) and `avatars` (public, `{user_id}/avatar-{sha256}.jpg`, owner-only upload/delete but readable by anyone including signed out — it doubles as the account's public avatar once signed in, per the Account/Privacy screens' existing framing). Both buckets also cap `allowed_mime_types` to `image/jpeg`/`image/webp` at the Storage-API level, which no RLS policy can express — see the migration's own comments and docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md's manual-check section for why that half needs a human to verify against the real API, not just Postgres RLS. `profiles.avatar_url` (already existing, owner-updatable) gets set to the Storage public URL after a successful avatar upload — no new column needed.
- **Point 3 — `src/sync/images.js` (new, a leaf)**: `prepareCoverUpload`/`prepareAvatarUpload` re-encode through canvas (covers: max 1200px @ ~0.85; avatars: 512px square @ ~0.85) via a module-private `cropSquareStepped`, a miniature, deliberately *duplicated*-not-imported copy of `crate/tiers.js`'s own stepped-downscale crop — kept separate so this file stays import-free, the same reasoning `backup-doc.js`/`merge.js` already follow. Re-encoding strips EXIF (and with it, GPS) by construction — canvas never carries a source image's metadata through to its own output — called out explicitly in the file's own header comment, since it matters most for the avatar, the one publicly-readable image in this whole pipeline. Each output is named by the SHA-256 of its *own re-encoded* bytes, not the source file's — `sha256Hex` (pure, async, Web Crypto) is the one piece of this file unit-tested directly (`tests/unit/images.test.js`), since the canvas/`Image` half needs a DOM this test suite doesn't have (the same accepted gap `share/styles.js`'s canvas drawing already has).
- **Point 4 — schema v2**: `src/sync/backup-doc.js`'s `SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped 1 → 2. A new `convertCovers`/`covers` doc field (per-track-identity-key, `{identityKey, at, hash}` — `hash: null` is the tombstone, written by a whole-overlay revert alongside the existing `fields: null, reverted: true`) and a new `lastWrite.picture` field (the exact `lw()` shape every other last-write-wins field already uses). `src/sync/merge.js` gained `mergeCovers` (the same per-identity-key last-write-wins primitive as `mergeOverlays`, kept separate on purpose — a cover change and a tag change are independent edits with their own timestamps) and folded `picture` into the existing `lastWriteWins` plumbing. A v1 doc — missing both fields entirely — still merges without throwing: every reader already treats a missing field as "no claim" by construction (`row.covers || []`, etc.), confirmed by two dedicated `tests/unit/backup-merge.test.js` schema-v1-compatibility tests rather than just assumed.
- **Point 5 — upload/download/cleanup, all in `src/sync/backup.js`**:
  - **Upload** rides the exact same debounced/offline-safe queue B3 already built (`scheduleBackupSync`/`runPush`'s existing backoff) — nothing new to invent. `queueCoverUpload(trackId, blob)` (called fire-and-forget from `library/edit.js`'s `applyEdit()`) and `queueAvatarUpload(blob)` (from `profile/screens.js`'s `persistProfile()`) each re-encode/hash via `images.js`, stamp the claim locally, and schedule a sync — neither is ever awaited by its caller, matching the existing fire-and-forget style already used elsewhere in this codebase (e.g. `buildCrateTier(...).catch(() => {})`), so a save can never be blocked waiting on a network upload. The real network step, `uploadOne`/`flushPendingImages`, re-derives the canonical re-encoded blob+hash fresh from the locally-stored *source* blob on every sync cycle rather than keeping a second copy of pre-encoded bytes around — deduped only by a small persisted `Set` of confirmed `'bucket:hash'` tags (`loadUploadedHashes`/`persistUploadedHashes`), so an unchanged cover or picture is never re-uploaded just because a sync cycle ran.
  - **Download** happens only when a matching local track already exists (`queueCoverDownload`) — otherwise the claim waits in `B.pending.covers`, resolved later by `reconcileImportedTrack(t)` the same way a pending overlay/playlist/albumOrder entry already does. A downloaded cover is written into the local `artwork` store and triggers `deleteCrateArtTiers`/`buildCrateTier` so the crate view picks it up without any new rendering code; a downloaded avatar updates `S.profile.art`/`artBlob`/`pictureHash` directly. Both fire `emit('applied')` so `main.js`'s existing listener (see its own repo-layout entry, extended this step to also call `renderProfile()` when the profile tab is open) can refresh what's on screen — `backup.js` itself still never imports `library/view.js`/`record-art/looks.js` (see below).
  - **Cleanup**: `orphanedFiles(files, referencedHashes, keepNewest)` is the one pure decision in this whole area, exported and unit-tested directly — covers keep zero unreferenced files (an old cover that's been fully replaced has nothing left pointing at it), avatars keep the single most recently updated unreferenced one (a brief grace window, never more than one spare file per user). `cleanupOrphanedImages`/`listBucketFiles` do the actual Storage-side removal, wrapped in try/catch as best-effort — a cleanup failure must never block the push itself.
- **A second chain-explosion bug, same shape as step 06's two, caught and fixed before it shipped**: a first draft had `queueAvatarUpload` flush `S.backupUpdatedAt` to IndexedDB via the normal `storage/meta.js`'s `queueSave()`/`saveMeta()` path — but `storage/meta.js` real-chains into `player/background.js` → `player/transport.js` → `library/view.js`, exactly the hub this file has twice already been designed around avoiding. Fixed by calling `meta.put('backupUpdatedAt', S.backupUpdatedAt).catch(() => {})` directly against the already-imported, true-leaf `meta` from `storage/repo.js` instead — `backup.js`'s own header comment now lists `storage/meta.js` alongside `library/view.js`/`record-art/looks.js` as a module this file must never import, with the same reasoning for each.
- **Point 2 (account deletion) — `src/miziki-social.js`'s `deleteAccount()`** now lists and removes every file under the caller's own `covers/<uid>/` and `avatars/<uid>/` folders (a new module-private `removeStorageFolder(bucket, uid)`, best-effort, wrapped in its own try/catch) *before* calling the `delete_my_account()` RPC — Storage objects have no foreign key to `auth.users`, so migration 005's existing cascades can't reach either bucket, and this has to happen while the account's own owner-only RLS policies (migration 006) still apply, i.e. before the RPC deletes the account out from under it.
- **Point 6 — tests**: `tests/unit/images.test.js` (new, 4 tests — `sha256Hex` only, per the file's own documented DOM gap); `tests/unit/backup-sync.test.js` extended (5 new tests, all for `orphanedFiles`, plus `artwork`/`profile` added to its existing `storage/repo.js` mock); `tests/unit/backup-merge.test.js` extended (9 new tests — cover last-write-wins/tombstone, picture last-write-wins/field-isolation, schema-v1-compatibility). `tests/supabase_stub.sql` gained a minimal `storage.buckets`/`storage.objects`/`storage.foldername()` stand-in (the same "real Postgres role/`auth.uid()` semantics, not a real Storage API" approach the existing `auth.users`/`auth.uid()` stub already takes) so `tests/client.test.js` could add 4 real-RLS tests against it: owner CRUD on `covers`; a second user's read/write/delete against the first user's folder all correctly failing or matching zero rows; `avatars`' public-read-but-owner-write split (confirmed for both an authenticated second user and a fully signed-out `asAnon()` request); and `deleteAccount()`'s own Storage-cleanup calls, exercised against the file's existing fake `sb` client's new `storage.from(bucket).list()/.remove()` stub. What the Postgres stand-in can't reach — the Storage API's own `file_size_limit`/`allowed_mime_types` enforcement, and actual byte storage/retrieval — is flagged in the stub's own comment and covered instead by a manual-check section added to docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md (point 6's own documented fallback for what isn't testable locally).
- **Point 7 — docs**: CLAUDE.md's "Backup: what gets synced" table gained two new rows (a custom cover's own image bytes; the profile picture's own image bytes) with the opening paragraph's "never leaves the device" claim corrected to carve out the one exception, plus `S.profile.pictureHash` and `backupUpdatedAt.picture`/the cover-tombstone addition folded into the existing "local state the merge logic depends on" list. "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" gained two new table rows (`covers`, `avatars`) and its own opening claim was corrected the same way. docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md's migration list and the step 05 "Applying" pattern both gained a `006_images.sql` entry, including the Storage-dashboard bucket-settings check and the manual cross-user/public-read policy check noted above.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (307/307 — 289 before this step + 4 new in `images.test.js` + 5 new in `backup-sync.test.js` + 9 new in `backup-merge.test.js`); `npm test` passes (42/42, against a freshly reseeded Postgres database with all six migrations reapplied in order, same "Lesson for future verification" procedure every prior step needing Postgres has followed). Two existing unit test files needed a mock-chain fix of the exact shape every prior step's extractions have required, once `library/edit.js` started importing real names from `sync/backup.js`: `tests/unit/forget-library.test.js`'s existing `crate/tiers.js` mock gained `buildCrateTier` (needed once `backup.js`'s own real import of it, reached transitively through `library/edit.js`, joined the chain this test already mocks `deleteCrateArtTiers` to stop); `tests/unit/history.test.js`'s existing `sync/backup.js` mock gained `queueCoverUpload` (reached the same way, via the real `cropSquareImage` → `library/edit.js` chain its own header comment already documents).

**Before merging (Ken):**
1. Apply `supabase/migrations/006_images.sql` to the live Supabase project via the SQL editor (`docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md`'s own step-by-step, now with a dedicated section for this migration).
2. Verify the two Storage buckets exist with the right settings (private/public, size caps, allowed types) in the dashboard's Storage tab.
3. Run through docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md's manual cross-user/public-read policy check for both buckets — the one thing a real Supabase project can confirm that the local Postgres harness's `storage.objects` stand-in can't (its own `file_size_limit`/`allowed_mime_types` enforcement included).
4. Only then merge this PR.

**Preview focus (Ken) — please verify by hand before merging:**
1. Set a custom cover on a track from the edit sheet, confirm it still shows after a reload, and confirm (via the dashboard's Storage tab, or a second signed-in device) that a file appears under `covers/<your user id>/`.
2. Set or change a profile picture, confirm the Account/Privacy screens still work as expected, and confirm a file appears under `avatars/<your user id>/` — and that it's visible from a signed-out tab or a different account (the one bucket meant to be public).
3. Revert a custom cover back to the embedded/default art and confirm the old cover file is eventually removed from Storage (cleanup runs on the next sync cycle, not instantly).
4. Change the profile picture a second time and confirm at most one old, unreferenced avatar file lingers in Storage afterward, never more.
5. On a second device signed into the same account, confirm a cover/avatar set on the first device eventually appears there too, without having to re-pick either image.
6. **Do not use "Delete account" on a real account you want to keep.** On a disposable test account instead, confirm that after deletion, its `covers/`/`avatars/` folders are actually empty in the Storage dashboard (not just the database rows).
7. Confirm no CSP violations or console errors anywhere in this flow, against both built targets, and that with Supabase not configured the app behaves exactly as it did before this PR.

### Step 08 (P3, part A): Privacy Policy and Terms of use screens

Eighth of the post-refactor roadmap's numbered documents, part A only (see the task doc's own framing: "Part A is one Claude Code PR; parts B–D are mostly you, in dashboards" — B is `docs/MODERATION.md`, covered separately below; C/D/E are Ken's own setup/domain/App Store work, out of scope for Claude Code). A content PR, not a refactor — new screens, new docs, zero behavior change to anything that existed before this step. **This PR must not be merged until Ken has the final text reviewed** (the task doc's own words: "I'm not a lawyer. Before the App Store, have the final text reviewed"). The contact address in all four files (`docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`, `docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md`, and their two in-app mirrors inside `src/profile/legal.js`) is filled in as `sckhitokiri@gmail.com`, noted inline as temporary — to be swapped for an admin@ address on Miziki's own domain once one is purchased — see "Before merging (Ken)" at the end of this entry. The minimum age was 13 throughout at first, per the task doc's own instruction — raising it was flagged as Ken's own later call to make, not something this PR decided on its own, and Ken has since made that call: the minimum age is now **16** in all four files, to avoid GDPR's under-16 parental-consent rules for EU users.

- **The five required content areas**, all written from facts already established elsewhere in this file rather than generic boilerplate — every sentence in `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md` traces back to the "Backup: what gets synced" and "What's stored server-side, and who can see it" tables above, not invented: what's collected (email for sign-in only; handle/display name/bio/avatar; social activity, now-spinning/milestones off by default; the private listening backup — play/session counts, achievements/Collection, sealed state, track-edit text, playlists, settings; uploaded custom covers and the profile picture, both re-encoded and EXIF/GPS-stripped per step 07); what's never collected (music files, embedded art, location — on-device only for Sundown/Car Mode — and "no requests to any third party at all, other than your own Supabase project"); who can see what (backup/covers private; handle/name/avatar visible to signed-in users except anyone blocked; shared activity follows the visibility setting, friends by default); where it lives and how long (Supabase US East as processor; kept until deleted; Download my data / Delete account, both already-shipped features from steps 06/05 this document just explains); and who can use it (16+, must own/have the right to play the music) plus community rules (no impersonation/harassment/illegal content; reports reviewed, consequences up to account deletion) — all five appear in both `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md` and, condensed, in `src/profile/legal.js`'s `renderPrivacyPolicyScreen`.
- **`docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md`** (not separately asked for by name, but the task doc's own five bullets split naturally into privacy-facing and usage-facing halves, and the Account/Privacy screens already distinguish the two concepts) covers what Miziki is, who can use it, how an account works (no password, single-use emailed codes, sign out one/all devices, delete anytime), content ownership (the user owns their music/photos/text; Miziki claims none of it), the same community rules as the bulleted list above, "provided as is" with no uptime guarantee (pointing at Download my data as the mitigation), and a changes clause.
- **Content-addressed canonical source, mirrored by hand**: `docs/PRIVACY_POLICY.md`/`docs/TERMS_OF_USE.md` are the reviewable markdown (so wording changes go through a normal PR diff); `src/profile/legal.js`'s `renderPrivacyPolicyScreen`/`renderTermsScreen` render the same words in-app, since there's no markdown renderer anywhere in this codebase and adding one for two static pages would be more moving parts than keeping the two in sync by discipline — the same two-sources-reviewed-together pattern this very file (CLAUDE.md) already uses for its own prose vs. the code it describes. Each of the four files opens with a comment/note pointing at its sibling.
- **Three link points, exactly as asked** ("linked from the sign-in screen, the Privacy screen and the account screen"): a `legalLinksRow(renderProfile, before)` helper in `legal.js`, reused at all three. The Account screen (`src/profile/settings.js`'s `renderSocialSettingsScreen`) and the Privacy screen (`src/profile/privacy.js`'s `renderPrivacyScreen`) each get the row appended from `src/profile/screens.js`'s own dispatch in `renderProfile()` — right after calling each screen's render function — rather than either module importing `legal.js` directly, the same `wirePrivacyLink(host, renderProfile)`-style parameter-passing shape `privacy.js` already established in step 06 specifically to avoid adding a new edge to `screens.js`'s already-dense circular-import cluster. The sign-in wizard's first step (`renderSocialAuthEmail`, in `settings.js`) gets a one-line "By continuing, you agree to our Privacy Policy and Terms of use" note plus the same row, but with `before` set to `closeSocialAuth` — the one call site that needs it, since tapping either link there has to close the sign-in modal first or it would stay open on top of the profile screen underneath it once `renderProfile()` runs. `renderProfile()`'s `labels` map and dispatch gained two new leaf screens, `privacyPolicy`/`termsOfUse` (`'Privacy Policy'`/`'Terms of use'`), reached the same way every other profile sub-screen already is: `S.profileView.screen = '…'; renderProfile();`.
- **Styling**: a new, correctly-numbered `src/styles/21-legal.css`, appended after `20-friend-store.css` in both the cascade and `miziki.html`'s `<link>` order — scoped `#profileBody h2` styling (no generic element reset exists anywhere else in this app's CSS, confirmed by audit before adding one), `.legal-list` for the bullet lists, and `.legal-links`/`.link-btn` for the inline footer row.
- **Why `el()`/`textContent`, not `innerHTML`, for static text that could never carry a tag-injection payload**: consistency with every other screen in the app, not a security requirement unique to this content — called out explicitly in `legal.js`'s own header comment, since a reader familiar with the step 01 `no-restricted-syntax` rule (which exists for untrusted *file-tag* text, not developer-authored copy) might otherwise wonder why this screen bothers.
- No `await` touched — this step adds two pure-DOM render functions and one small helper function; nothing here is on the playback or sync path.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (307/307, unchanged — per the same precedent step 04's config-only PR set, nothing in this step is pure/stateful logic worth a unit test: two render functions over static strings and a link-row helper, exercised instead by the Playwright pass below); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`, 166 modules transformed each, bundle sizes unchanged beyond the new CSS file); a Playwright script against both built targets opened the Privacy screen and followed its Privacy Policy/Terms of use links (confirming the crumb label, the "Last updated" line, and the contact line are all present and that navigating back lands on the right prior screen), confirmed the same two links appear and work from the Account screen, and opened the sign-in modal's first step to confirm its new legal footer renders, that tapping either link closes the modal first, and that the app then shows the corresponding screen — zero non-baseline console/page errors on either built target.

**Before merging (Ken):**

Replace it with an admin@ address on Miziki's own domain once one is purchased (Part D).

3. ~~Decide whether to raise the minimum age from 13 to 16 for GDPR~~ **Done** — Ken asked for 16, applied in all four files (both docs plus both `src/profile/legal.js` renderers). ### Step 08 (P3, part B): `docs/MODERATION.md`

Part B of the same roadmap document as part A (Privacy Policy/Terms of use, above — already merged). Per the task doc's own framing — "Docs only, so no preview needed" — this is the lowest-ceremony step in the whole engagement so far: one new file, no app code, no migration, nothing to build, lint, or test.

- Three sections: how to review reports (the dashboard SQL query, run weekly, ordered oldest-first, plus the convention this doc picks for `reports.status` — `open`/`actioned`/`dismissed`, since the column itself is a plain `text` with no enum); how to find a user's id from a handle when a report doesn't already carry it; and the three actions, each with the exact SQL and the equivalent Table Editor click-path.
- **Clear an avatar or bio**: null the `profiles` columns; separately flagged that this doesn't delete the underlying Storage object, with the dashboard steps to remove the `avatars` bucket file too when the image itself (not just the bio) is the problem.
- **Remove a sleeve note**: find the `(user_id, release_id)` pair via `crate_items`/`releases`, null `sleeve_note`.
- **Delete an account**: the one action that can't go through the app's own path — `delete_my_account()` (migration `005_backup.sql`) is written `where id = auth.uid()`, so it only ever works for someone acting on their own account, including from the SQL editor (which isn't signed in as anyone). A moderator instead deletes the `auth.users` row directly, relying on the same `on delete cascade` chain `delete_my_account()` itself uses — confirmed against the real schema in `001_schema.sql`/`005_backup.sql`, not assumed. Two things this surfaced, both called out explicitly in the doc rather than left for Ken to discover the hard way: (1) deleting the account cascades away any `reports` row naming it as the target (`reports.target_user_id references profiles(id) on delete cascade`), so the doc tells the reviewer to copy the report's `reason`/`created_at` *before* running the delete, since there's no separate moderation log anywhere in this schema; (2) the cascade never reaches Storage — `covers`/`avatars` objects have no foreign key to `auth.users`, the exact gap `006_images.sql`'s own closing comment already flags for the user-initiated deletion path (`src/miziki-social.js`'s `deleteAccount()`) — so a moderator-initiated delete needs its own manual Storage cleanup in both buckets, spelled out as explicit dashboard steps.
- Every SQL snippet and column/table name was checked directly against `supabase/migrations/001_schema.sql`/`005_backup.sql`/`006_images.sql` (`reports`, `profiles`, `crate_items`, the RLS policies revoking `update`/`delete` on `reports` from `authenticated` — confirming review genuinely has to happen outside the app, matching the doc's own opening line), not written from memory.
- No build, lint, or test run — a markdown-only change with zero code surface, the same posture step 03's "no visible change" framing used for its own pure-logic PR, just one step further (that step at least touched `src/`; this one touches nothing runnable at all).

### Step D1: dark-mode color tokens (desktop layout prerequisite)

First of a new, separate seven-PR plan (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`, added this step — the full handoff doc, verbatim) to add a desktop layout and an Auto/Light/Dark appearance setting. **No behavior change, and — unlike every other refactor in this engagement — no visible change either**: D1's only job is moving every hard-coded dark color onto a CSS custom-property token, with the token's value set to exactly what the literal already was. Light values, the appearance setting itself, and the desktop layout are D2 onward — explicitly not this PR, and explicitly not started: **per the handoff doc's own instruction, this step stops after D1 and waits for Ken before D2 begins.**

- **Survey first, per the doc's own instruction**: grepped every `src/styles/*.css` file and `miziki.html` for literal hex/`rgba()` colors before changing anything, and separately grepped for every `background:var(--paper)` use (`--paper` already existed; the doc's token table splits it from a new `--cream`, so every existing use needed checking against which one it actually means). Found three categories: three files sharing the exact same `linear-gradient(170deg,var(--sky1) 0%,var(--sky2) 62%,#07080C 100%)` "ground" gradient (`00-base.css`'s `body`, `13-player-overlay.css`'s `.player-overlay`, and `09-launch-intro.css`'s `.launch-overlay` — the third one not named in the doc's own prose, found only by the grep); two chrome surfaces (`08-shell.css`'s `.mini-player` at `rgba(10,14,18,.9)` and `.tabbar` at `rgba(10,14,18,.94)`) both meant by the doc's single `--chrome` token; and five `background:var(--paper)` uses that were never actually the page's own paper tone — a cream sticker/note swatch, both in `18-storefront.css` (the awning stripe, the count sticker, the pick note) and `20-friend-store.css` (the record-sheet note).
- **Nine new tokens added to `00-base.css`'s `:root`, dark values only** (D1's whole scope, per the doc): `--sky3` (`#07080C`, the ground gradient's third stop), `--cream` (`#EFE9DF` — same literal `--paper` already held, split into its own name since the doc's table treats "paper" and "cream sticker" as two different future light-mode colors even though they're identical today), `--panel`/`--chrome`/`--raised`/`--field`/`--sel` (the five `rgba()` surface tones the doc's table names), `--cool` (`#9FD8E8`), and `--disc-a`/`--disc-b`/`--disc-c` (the vinyl-disc gradient's three stops). Every value is a direct copy of the literal it replaces — confirmed by diff, not retyped from the doc's table, so a transcription slip in either direction can't silently change anything.
- **Three ground-gradient call sites** (`00-base.css`'s `body`, `13-player-overlay.css`'s `.player-overlay`, `09-launch-intro.css`'s `.launch-overlay`) swapped their trailing `#07080C` for `var(--sky3)` — the literal itself untouched, just named.
- **Chrome consolidation, a deliberate judgment call**: `08-shell.css`'s `.mini-player` (`rgba(10,14,18,.9)`) and `.tabbar` (`rgba(10,14,18,.94)`) both became `background:var(--chrome)` at the doc table's own `.9` dark value — an exact match for the mini-player, but a real ~4% alpha reduction for the tab bar, since the doc's single `--chrome` token covers both surfaces and the two had drifted slightly apart before this PR. Flagged for empirical verification rather than assumed safe — see below.
- **`--paper`→`--cream` on five sticker/note backgrounds** (`18-storefront.css`'s `.store-awning > div:nth-child(even)`, `.store-card-count`, `.store-pick-note`; `20-friend-store.css`'s `.record-note`) — zero value change (both tokens hold the identical literal today), just the correct name for what each one actually is, so a future D2 giving `--paper` and `--cream` different light-mode values doesn't accidentally recolor a sticker note to the page's own background tone.
- **`<meta name="theme-color">` left as a literal, with an explanatory comment, not "made a variable"**: an HTML attribute can't read a CSS custom property, so there's no way to literally satisfy "make it a variable" at the HTML level in this PR. The doc's own PR table assigns the actual JS-driven variable-ization to D2's `src/ui/theme.js` (which will set this attribute from script, per the active theme) — added a comment above the tag in `miziki.html` explaining the deferral and naming `--sky2` as the token this value tracks, rather than guessing at a different interpretation.
- **Verified with Playwright at 390×844 in dark, before vs. after, exactly as instructed**: built a clean `origin/main` baseline in a separate `git worktree` (`MIZIKI_BASE=/`, served on its own port) alongside this branch's own `/` build, then screenshotted five routes (library, home, sundown, character, profile) in both. The first pass, with default animation timing, showed large (2-50%) pixel diffs — traced to the launch-intro card stack and crate-flow animations landing at different, inherently nondeterministic rAF-driven frames between two independent page loads, not a color regression (visual inspection of the diffed frames confirmed the ground-gradient tones were identical; only the animated foreground card positions/colors differed). Re-ran with Playwright's `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` context option (which the app's own CSS already honors — `.launch-overlay{display:none}` under `prefers-reduced-motion:reduce`, eliminating the nondeterministic animation entirely) and got **zero pixel difference on all five routes**, confirmed with `pixelmatch` (threshold 0.1) — including the `.tabbar`'s `.94→.9` alpha consolidation, which a direct pixel comparison shows is visually imperceptible on this near-black, heavily-blurred chrome surface. Zero console/page errors on either build throughout.
- No `await` touched, no JS changed at all — this PR is CSS/HTML only.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (307/307, unchanged — nothing in this PR is JS, so nothing here is newly testable); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright before/after screenshot comparison above shows exactly zero pixel difference across all five routes tested, with reduced motion isolating the comparison from the app's own pre-existing animation nondeterminism rather than masking a real change.

### Step D2: light theme and the Auto/Light/Dark preference

Second of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D1's tokens. Per the doc's own instruction ("Decide these before D2... Each has a default, so Claude Code can proceed if you do not answer"), proceeded on the two open questions that actually bear on this step using the doc's own stated defaults, since no override had been given by the time this PR started: **Auto is the default for new installs, and a saved prefs object without `theme` also loads as Auto**; **the theme choice is device-only**, not synced to the account. (The other four open questions — the light palette's own exact values, record-detail-on-desktop, the 768–1023px tablet range, and the sidebar extras — belong to D3 and later, not this PR.) This PR adds real, visible UI (the Appearance row) — unlike D1, "no visible change" was never D2's own bar; the bar is "renders correctly in both themes, nothing else regresses," verified below.

- **The light token set** (`src/styles/22-theme.css`, new, appended after `21-legal.css`): three selector blocks, exactly as the doc's own "Light and dark theming" section specifies — `:root[data-theme="light"]` (a pinned Light choice), `@media (prefers-color-scheme: light) { :root:not([data-theme]) }` (Auto, the one block that needs zero JavaScript to apply correctly at first paint), and `:root[data-theme="dark"]` re-asserting the dark values (a pinned Dark choice, beating the device). Only the thirteen tokens that actually differ between themes are redeclared in each block (`--sky1`/`--sky2`/`--sky3`, `--paper`, `--cream`, `--muted`, `--line`, `--panel`, `--chrome`, `--raised`, `--field`, `--sel`, `--accent-text`, `--shadow`, plus `color-scheme`) — `--accent`, `--on-accent`, `--cool`, the disc gradient (`--disc-a/b/c`) and the font stacks are left to inherit from bare `:root` on purpose, per the doc's own "the disc, sleeve art, metal tiers and the share-image styles keep their own colors in both themes — only chrome changes" rule. Every light value is copied straight from the doc's own token table, not approximated. `00-base.css`'s bare `:root` also gained `color-scheme:dark` (one line), so native controls (scrollbars, form fields) follow the theme too, matching the doc's "Selectors" section.
- **`S.theme`** (`'auto'`/`'light'`/`'dark'`, default `'auto'`, `src/state.js`) — added to `serializePrefs`/`parsePrefs` (`src/storage/prefs.js`) the same way every other saved setting already is, per the refactor-rules' own "new saved setting" checklist: a default in `S`, both prefs functions, and a `prefs.test.js` round-trip plus a "missing field loads as the default" test. **Device-only**: `src/sync/backup.js`'s `buildLocalState()` deletes `theme` from the `serializePrefs(S)` copy it uploads, so a pinned choice on one device never follows the account to another — added to the backup key-inventory table in CLAUDE.md as the rule itself requires for every new saved key.
- **`src/ui/theme.js`** (new): `applyTheme()` — sets/removes `document.documentElement.dataset.theme`, mirrors a pinned choice to `localStorage` (for the anti-flash script below), updates `<meta name="theme-color">` (resolved against the *effective* theme — the device's own scheme while Auto — since a meta attribute can't read a CSS custom property), installs/tears down a `matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: light)')` listener so Auto keeps following the device live (confirmed working end to end below, not just unit-tested), and syncs `aria-pressed` on every `[data-theme-choice]` button in the DOM, phone or desktop, present or not. `setTheme(choice)` — validates, sets `S.theme`, persists via `queueSave()`, applies; the one function both the phone row and the desktop sidebar's own control (D7) will call, per the doc's own "both call the same function" instruction — forward-compatible with zero changes needed here once D7 adds its own `[data-theme-choice]` markup, since this file was written to the attribute convention, not a fixed element id.
- **`applyPrefs()`** (`src/storage/meta.js`) sets `S.theme` from a valid saved value and calls `applyTheme()` unconditionally at the end (not just inside the `if`) — so a restore with no saved `theme` at all (every device before D2) still re-applies the default correctly, the same reasoning every other `applyPrefs()` field follows. **`main.js`'s boot sequence** also calls `applyTheme()` once, unconditionally, right after `setMode('vinyl')` — the same shape, since Auto's own live-scheme listener and the `theme-color` meta tag both need setting up even on a device's very first-ever boot, before any saved `prefs` row exists for `restoreMeta()`'s own `if(pr)` branch to ever run.
- **Avoiding a flash for a pinned choice** (`src/theme-boot.js`, new): a plain classic script — no import/export, the same shape as `src/miziki-social.js` — loaded in `<head>` *before* the stylesheet `<link>` tags, so it blocks HTML parsing and sets `data-theme` from the `localStorage` mirror before any CSS is even applied. Needs its own `copy-theme-boot` Vite plugin (`vite.config.js`), the exact same reasoning as `copy-social-client` — Vite leaves a plain (non-`type="module"`) `<script src>` completely untouched, so without an explicit copy step it 404s in the built app. Auto needs none of this, by design — confirmed in the verification below that `data-theme` stays unset the entire time Auto is active, letting the CSS `@media` block alone carry it at first paint.
- **The Appearance row** (`miziki.html`, Character route) — a three-button `.seg` (`data-theme-choice="auto"/"light"/"dark"`), the same row shape as Scratch/Sleeve Pull/Launch intro just below it. `src/wiring/character.js` wires it with `document.querySelectorAll('[data-theme-choice]')` rather than fixed ids, so D7's own desktop control needs no wiring change here, only its own markup.
- **A real, pre-existing design interaction found during verification, not a D2 bug**: `src/player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` (Sundown's animated sky gradient, untouched by D1 or D2) unconditionally overwrites `--sky1`/`--sky2` as an **inline style** on `:root` every frame, blending through its own fixed, always-dark-leaning RGB stops regardless of app theme — an inline style always wins over any stylesheet rule, including the new `22-theme.css` blocks, so `--sky1`/`--sky2` never visibly reflect the Light theme while Sundown is actively progressing. This is exactly today's existing behavior (the same three RGB arrays, confirmed to already encode the original dark palette's own hex values) continuing unchanged — fixing or theming it isn't in D2's scope, and the doc doesn't ask for it. Flagged here, not silently worked around: it's also why the Playwright verification below tests `--chrome` for "does the theme follow the device scheme live," not `--sky1`/`--sky2`, which are the wrong tokens to probe for that question on this particular app.
- **Tests** (`npm run test:unit`, 320/320 — 307 before this step + 13 new): `prefs.test.js` gained `theme` to its round-trip fixture plus three tests matching the doc's own required list almost verbatim — round-trips as Light and Dark, not just the Auto default; a saved object without `theme` (every device before D2) loads as if it had been Auto; an unlisted value such as `"sepia"` is ignored. New `theme.test.js` (10 tests): `applyTheme()` sets/removes `data-theme` for a pinned choice vs. Auto; resolves `<meta name="theme-color">` against the effective theme, including from the device's own scheme while Auto; installs a live `matchMedia` listener only while Auto is active and tears it down the moment a choice is pinned (asserted directly via spies on `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener`, not inferred from a side effect that could look the same either way); a simulated `change` event updates the meta tag; mirrors a pinned choice to `localStorage` and clears the mirror for Auto; syncs `aria-pressed` on every `[data-theme-choice]` button; `setTheme()` persists via `queueSave()` and ignores an unlisted choice. `meta.test.js`'s existing `applyPrefs()` tests needed a direct `src/ui/theme.js` mock (`applyTheme(){}`) added, the usual "stop the chain at the door" reasoning every mock-heavy test in this suite already uses, since the real `applyTheme()` needs `document.documentElement`/`localStorage`/`matchMedia`, none of which that file's existing fake `document` provides and none of which its own tests care about.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, 390×844 unless noted): dark and light screenshots of all five routes, zero console/page errors and zero CSP violations in every scenario below. **Auto follows the device scheme live, without a reload**: loaded with `colorScheme:'dark'`, confirmed `data-theme` stays unset; called `page.emulateMedia({colorScheme:'light'})` on the already-open page (Playwright's own live-scheme-change emulation) and confirmed `--chrome` and the `theme-color` meta tag both updated immediately, no navigation. **No flash for a pinned choice**: tapped Light via the real Appearance row, waited out the 600ms `queueSave()` debounce, reloaded, and read `data-theme` at the earliest possible moment (`DOMContentLoaded`, before `main.js`'s own module graph has even finished loading) — already `"light"`, confirming `theme-boot.js` actually got there first; confirmed it was still `"light"` after the full page settled, and that `S.theme` round-tripped through real IndexedDB (`meta.prefs.theme`), not just the `localStorage` mirror. **1440×900 in light**: loads cleanly with zero errors (D3 is the actual desktop shell; this only confirms D2 doesn't break anything at a wide viewport ahead of that work). **Phone regression** (the doc's own requirement, "a before and after screenshot of each route at 390px should match in dark" for D1→D2): compared this step's dark screenshots against D1's own saved baseline via `pixelmatch` — library/home/sundown/profile are **pixel-identical, 0 difference**; character shows the expected, intentional diff (the new Appearance row itself — confirmed by inspecting the screenshot directly, not just the diff count), exactly the one route D2 was always going to change.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (320/320 — 307 before this step + 13 new); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout, confirming Auto's live follow, the anti-flash mechanism, and the phone-regression requirement all hold.

### Step D3: desktop shell

Third of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D1's tokens and D2's light theme/preference (both merged, PRs #80/#81). Per the doc's own PR-order list: "Breakpoint, sidebar from the tab bar, main-area width, and the playlists and Sundown cards. CSS plus a small render function for the sidebar." Per the doc's own constraint, "below the desktop breakpoint, the phone layout must render identically" — verified below, including a real false alarm this step's own verification caught and resolved before trusting the result.

- **The breakpoint** (`src/styles/23-desktop.css`, new, appended after `22-theme.css`): `@media (min-width:1024px)` turns on the desktop shell; a second `@media (min-width:1280px)` is where the doc's own two-width sidebar table actually splits — 76px icon rail from 1024 to 1279px, 232px with labels and the sidebar's own content from 1280px up. Everything in the file sits behind one of those two queries except two unconditional rules, `.sidebar{display:contents}` and `.sidebar-extra{display:none}` — see the sidebar markup entry below for why those two specifically have to apply at every width.
- **Sidebar from the tab bar**: the existing `.tabbar` markup (`data-route="library"/"home"/"sundown"/"character"/"profile"`, `wireRoutes()`) is untouched — not one button, class or id changed. It's wrapped in a new `<div class="sidebar" id="sidebar">` (`miziki.html`), physically in the exact same spot it's always occupied (right after the mini-player), so routing needed zero JavaScript change, exactly as the doc's own "keep the same buttons and `wireRoutes()`" instruction asks. Below 1024px, `.sidebar{display:contents}` drops the wrapper out of the box model entirely — `.tabbar` lays out as if the wrapper didn't exist, which is what keeps the phone layout byte-for-byte unaffected (see the phone-regression investigation below for how thoroughly this was actually checked, not just assumed). At 1024px+, the sidebar becomes a normal-flow flex item sharing a row with `.wrap` (not `position:fixed` — flex alone keeps the two from ever overlapping), `position:sticky` so it tracks scrolling, `overflow-y:auto` for a long playlists list.
  - **A real bug found and fixed during this step's own verification, not a pre-existing one**: `.wrap` physically comes *before* `.sidebar`/`.tabbar` in the DOM (unchanged from the phone markup, where the tab bar has always sat at the end of `<body>`) — so a plain `body{display:flex}` lays `.wrap` out first, left-to-right, with the sidebar pushed to the *right* of it, overlapping and visually broken. Fixed with `.sidebar{order:-1}` (a standard, legitimate flexbox technique for exactly this — visual reordering without reparenting any markup) rather than moving the sidebar's DOM position, which would have meant relocating `<nav id="tabbar">` itself and re-auditing every selector that assumes its current position. Caught by a Playwright assertion (`wrap.left < sidebar.right`, i.e. "do the two boxes overlap"), not by eye — confirmed every other direct child of `<body>` (the launch/player/edit/share overlays, the gatefold, the sealed-record sheet — about twenty `.edit-overlay`/`.share-overlay`/similar divs) is `position:fixed` in its own CSS rule before relying on "nothing else in `<body>` participates in this flex row," rather than assuming it.
  - **The mini-player strip** (`.mini-player{display:none !important}` at 1024px+): D4's own job per the doc's PR table ("the bottom bar on desktop, wired to the existing playback functions") — hiding it here, rather than leaving the phone's narrow, centered strip floating unstyled over the sidebar/main-area boundary, is a deliberate, documented placeholder for that PR, not an oversight. `renderMiniPlayer()` still runs unchanged and still flips this element's own `style.display` to `flex` every time a track loads — `!important` is the only way a stylesheet rule can out-rank that inline style until D4 replaces the strip with its own desktop-aware markup.
  - **A second, smaller knock-on bug, same root cause as the overlap one**: `src/shell/mini-player.js`'s `updateChromeInset()` and `src/crate/view.js`'s `applyCrateMiniInset()` both measure the tab bar's own rendered height to compute how much bottom padding/stage-height budget the phone layout needs to clear the fixed tab bar — a measurement that stopped making sense the moment the tab bar became a full-viewport-height vertical sidebar on desktop (it would have inflated `--chrome-inset` to roughly the sidebar's own height, and driven the crate stage's own height budget deeply negative). Fixed in both places with the same guard: `tabbar.offsetHeight` → `0` when `isDesktopShell()` (new `src/util/viewport.js`, a `matchMedia('(min-width:1024px)')` check — the one place JS needs to ask the same question CSS's own media query already answers, since CSS can't be queried from script directly). Full desktop sizing for the crate itself is D5's own job; this is strictly "don't let the sidebar's new height break pre-existing phone-layout math," caught the same way as the overlap bug — a direct `getBoundingClientRect()` assertion, not a visual check.
- **New sidebar content** (`src/shell/sidebar.js`, new; the Sundown card lives in `player/clock.js` instead — see below): the playlists list, `renderSidebarPlaylists()`, built from `S.playlists` with `el()`/`textContent` per the doc's own instruction, called as the first line of `renderTracks()` (`library/view.js`) — the single existing "a playlist changed, redraw" hub every other playlist-touching call site already lands on (favoriting, creating, deleting), rather than a second call site added at each of `S.playlists`'s own mutation points. Pins a system playlist (Favorites) first, the same order the phone's own Playlists tab already uses. The DOM for this (and the Sundown card) is built unconditionally, on every viewport, hidden below 1024px purely by CSS (`.sidebar-extra{display:none}`) — a deliberate choice over gating the render call itself on `isDesktopShell()`, since the one-line CSS rule is simpler than teaching every call site about the breakpoint, and building a few invisible DOM nodes on a phone costs nothing a user would ever notice.
- **The Sundown summary card**: per the doc's own instruction — "Reuse the Sundown readout that `drawSun()` already writes rather than computing it twice" — this isn't a new render function at all. `src/player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` (unchanged in every other respect) gained a few guarded lines at its own end, mirroring its already-computed `#rateWhy`/`#legStart`/`#legEnd` text straight into the sidebar's own `#sideRateWhy`/`#sideLegStart`/`#sideLegEnd` ids — null-guarded (`if(sideWhy) ...`) since those ids exist only in the sidebar's own DOM, not because of any breakpoint check. Verified directly: the sidebar's `#sideRateWhy` and the real Sundown route's `#rateWhy` read back byte-identical.
- **The header's search field and `+` menu — no new markup needed at all**: the doc's own "main area" bullet asks for these to "move into the main header row." `#libNav`'s existing row (`src/styles/01-header.css`) already lays its Songs/Crate/Artists/Playlists tab group and `#hdrActions` (the search/add icons) out with `justify-content:space-between`, specifically so the icon group stays pinned to the row's right edge regardless of width — a judgment call: dropping `.wrap`'s 520px cap is, by itself, exactly what turns that existing row into the wide top header-row bar the mockup shows, with the icons landing right where "the main header row" implies, with zero DOM change and zero risk to `wireSearch()`/`wireImport()`'s existing element lookups. Reparenting `#hdrActions` into `#appHeader`'s own DOM node was considered and rejected — CSS can't reparent across containers, and a JS-side move was unnecessary surface for what dropping one `max-width` already achieves visually.
- **Main area**: `.wrap`'s 520px cap drops entirely at 1024px+ (`max-width:none;flex:1 1 auto`), filling the row beside the sidebar; its own padding is rewritten outright for that width rather than relying on `--chrome-inset` (now a moot, harmless `16px` fallback at desktop widths per the `updateChromeInset()` fix above, since nothing reads the custom property there once the padding override wins).
- **Pointer and keyboard**: added `:hover`/`:focus-visible` color states to the sidebar's own nav buttons (`.tabbar-btn`), the one new interactive surface this step introduces; the existing Escape handler, spin-to-scrub's pointer events, and every other interaction are untouched — none of this step's own scope.
- **A real, pre-existing false alarm, investigated and resolved before trusting the regression result**: the first phone-regression pass (below) showed a nonzero pixel diff on *every* route, including ones (home, sundown) this step's own code never touches. Chased down methodically rather than assumed either way: confirmed the computed layout geometry (`#empty`'s/`#tabbar`'s/`#appHeader`'s exact `getBoundingClientRect()` values, `--chrome-inset`'s exact resolved value) is byte-for-byte identical between D2's own code and this step's code at phone width, via a `git stash`/rebuild/compare round trip — then confirmed, by diffing a *freshly captured* screenshot of D2's own unchanged code against D2's own *previously saved* baseline screenshot (zero lines of code different, two different capture sessions), that the exact same magnitude of diff reproduces with no code change at all. This is cross-session font-rasterization drift (a different container/font-cache state between the two sessions the two screenshots were captured in), the same class of issue D1's own entry already flagged for animation nondeterminism, not a D3 regression — confirmed by reproducing it with zero D3 code in the picture, not merely asserted.
- **Tests** (`npm run test:unit`, 324/324 — 320 before this step + 4 new): `tests/unit/sidebar.test.js` (new) — see the `tests/unit/` entry above for exactly what it covers, including the doc's own required `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` playlist-name test case. No existing test file needed a mock-chain fix: nothing in the suite previously touched `src/shell/sidebar.js` or `src/util/viewport.js`, and neither new module reaches into any wide chain the suite already stops at the door elsewhere.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets): **1440×900** — no horizontal scroll; sidebar (232px) and `.wrap` never overlap; the mini-player is confirmed hidden; the sidebar's own playlists list and Sundown card are visible; creating a real playlist (via the existing Playlists-tab UI, reused unchanged on desktop) shows up in the sidebar list with its exact typed name. **1100×900** (icon-rail width) — sidebar measures exactly 76px, the sidebar's own extra content is hidden, still no horizontal scroll. **390×844, dark, reduced motion** — `#sidebarExtra` confirmed invisible (`getComputedStyle(...).display === 'none'`), `#tabbar`'s own rect confirmed unchanged from its pre-D3 bottom-bar position. Zero console/page errors and zero CSP violations in every scenario above, on both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: per the doc's own requirement ("the phone layout must be pixel-identical" from D3 onward, since the new rules sit behind the breakpoint) — computed layout geometry is confirmed byte-for-byte identical to D2's own code at phone width (see the false-alarm investigation above for the exact metrics compared); the residual `pixelmatch` diff is confirmed, by direct reproduction with zero code change, to be cross-session rendering drift rather than anything this PR changed.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (324/324 — 320 before this step + 4 new); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout.

### Step D4: player bar

Fourth of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D3's shell (merged, PR #82). Per the doc's own PR-order list: "The bottom bar on desktop, wired to the existing playback functions. No change to `play()`." Per the doc's own constraint on this exact PR: "Duplicate element ids are not allowed. The full player owns `#play`, `#prev`, `#next`, `#scrub` and the rest. A desktop player bar must reuse or restyle those, not clone them."

- **What "reuse, don't clone" means here, read literally**: the full player's own `#play`/`#prev`/`#next`/`#scrub`/`#modePure`/`#modeVinyl`/`#queueBtn` stay exactly where they are, inside `#playerOverlay` — a fixed full-screen sheet that's hidden except when actually open (D6's own job, not this one). They can't be *physically relocated* into a persistent bottom bar without either duplicating their ids (which the doc explicitly forbids) or moving the full player's own markup out of its overlay (which isn't this PR's scope and isn't what the doc describes — D6 handles the overlay's own desktop layout separately). So "reuse" means: drive the exact same functions (`prevTrack`/`nextTrack`/`seek`/`setMode`/`openQueueSheet`) from *new*, bar-only ids, the same shape the app already uses between `#play` and `#miniPlay` — two different buttons, two different click handlers, one shared `play()`/`pause()` call underneath. Nothing here is a "second set of controls" in the sense the doc warns against: no new business logic was written, only new buttons wired to logic that already existed before this PR.
- **The player bar *is* the mini-player strip, restyled, not a new element.** `#miniPlayer` already toggles `style.display` between `'flex'` and `'none'` exactly when a track is loaded or not (`renderMiniPlayer()`, `src/shell/mini-player.js`, untouched by this step) — the D3 placeholder (`.mini-player{display:none !important}`) simply forced it hidden at desktop widths until this PR existed to give it a real desktop layout. Removing that `!important` override and adding the desktop-only layout rules below means the *existing* show/hide logic takes over again, unchanged: no JS decides when the bar appears, exactly as before.
- **New markup, all hidden below 1024px** (`miziki.html`, inside `#miniPlayer`, alongside the existing `#miniTap`/`#miniFavBtn`/`#miniQueueBtn`/`#miniPlay`): `#barPrev`/`#barNext` (new buttons, new ids), `#miniBarSeek` (a wrapper around `#barElapsed`/`#barScrub`/`#barTotal`, mirroring `#tElapsed`/`#scrub`/`#tTotal`), and `#miniBarModes` (a wrapper around `#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl`, mirroring `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl`). Each new element (and the two wrapper divs) is `display:none` by default (`src/styles/08-shell.css`) — on a phone none of them occupy a flex slot in the strip at all, so `#miniTap`/`#miniFavBtn`/`#miniQueueBtn`/`#miniPlay` keep their exact same relative visual order, unaffected by where in the DOM the new, invisible siblings happen to sit.
- **Desktop layout** (`src/styles/23-desktop.css`, replacing the D3 placeholder): `.mini-player` becomes a real `position:fixed;bottom:0` strip spanning the full viewport width (not capped to `.wrap`'s own column) at a fixed `height:88px`, matching the doc's own number. `order` on each child (`#miniTap`→1, `#miniFavBtn`→2, `#barPrev`→3, `#miniPlay`→4, `#barNext`→5, `.mini-bar-seek`→6, `.mini-bar-modes`→7, `#miniQueueBtn`→8) gives the three zones the doc describes — now playing, transport+seek, mode+queue — without moving any element's actual DOM position (which would have risked disturbing the phone's own flex order, since `order` only ever applies inside the `@media (min-width:1024px)` block and phone elements keep the default `order:0` they've always implicitly had). `.wrap`'s own bottom padding grows from `28px` to `calc(116px + env(safe-area-inset-bottom))` (the existing 28px gutter plus the bar's 88px) and `.sidebar`'s height shrinks from `100vh` to `calc(100vh - 88px)`, so neither the main content nor the sidebar's own last row ever sits underneath the now-real bar. Both reservations are flat, not tied to whether a track is actually loaded (the bar itself collapses to 0 rendered height then) — the same "a little unused space beats content hidden under fixed chrome" trade-off `--chrome-inset`'s own phone-side fallback already makes, and the simplest option that can't under-reserve.
- **Volume is the one listed zone this PR leaves out, on purpose, not by oversight.** The doc's own text says "mode, queue and volume on the right" — but grepping the entire codebase for anything that exposes master gain to the user turns up nothing: CLAUDE.md's own existing line in "How the app is put together" is explicit that "output is unity gain, so loudness tracks the device's own volume" (`src/sundown/location.js`'s own comment, unchanged since long before this step), and Car Mode's volume scaling is automatic, GPS-driven, never a user-facing slider. Adding a working volume control here would mean writing new audio-affecting logic — a feature addition, not "wired to the existing playback functions," and squarely against this PR's own "no change to `play()`" framing (nothing changes `play()` directly, but a new gain control would be a new piece of the audio graph's *user-facing surface*, the same category of change the doc's framing is clearly trying to keep out of this one PR). Left for a dedicated feature PR instead of invented here to fill a mockup slot; noted in the CSS's own comment at the point where it would have gone.
- **Wiring** (`src/wiring/transport.js`, six new lines, no new imports — every function the bar calls was already imported for the full player's own identical buttons): `#barPrev`/`#barNext` call `prevTrack`/`nextTrack` directly; `#barScrub`'s `input`/`change` listeners are the same two-line shape as `#scrub`'s own (set `S.scrubbing`, then `seek()` on release); `#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl` call `setMode('pure'|'vinyl')` + `queueSave()`, byte-identical to `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl`'s own handlers just above. `#miniQueueBtn` needed no new wiring at all — it was already wired to `openQueueSheet` in `src/wiring/overlays.js` since before this step; only its CSS position moved.
- **Two existing render functions each gained a small, guarded mirror**, the same "reuse the readout, don't compute it twice" shape `drawSun()`'s own sidebar mirror already established in D3: `src/player/clock.js`'s `drawTime()` mirrors `fmt(S.pos)`/`fmt(dur)`/the scrub `value` into `#barElapsed`/`#barTotal`/`#barScrub` (null-guarded, not because they might ever be missing from the real DOM, but for the same defensive-style reason `drawSun()`'s own sidebar mirror is guarded); `src/audio/mode.js`'s `setMode()` mirrors the same `aria-pressed` state it already sets on `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl` onto `#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl`.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — `play()`/`pause()`/`seek()` themselves are completely untouched by this PR, exactly as the doc's own "no change to `play()`" framing asks; every change here is either new markup, new CSS, or a new, additional call site into functions that already existed with their own, already-correct `await` sequencing.
- **No duplicate element ids anywhere in the document** — confirmed directly (not just by construction): every `[id]` in the built page was collected into a frequency map and checked for any id appearing more than once, on both built targets, with a track loaded and the bar actually visible (see Playwright verification below).
- No new unit tests this step: nothing new here is pure, standalone logic worth a `node:test` file of its own — six wiring lines that each call an already-tested function, and two render-function mirrors that are themselves pure side effects onto the DOM (the same "not much here is pure" shape `shell.test.js`'s own entry already gives for `shell/put-away.js`'s choreography). `npm run test:unit` stays at 324/324, unchanged, since no existing test's mock chain reaches `wireTransport()`, `drawTime()` or `setMode()` in a way these additions could disturb — each of the three files already had real, unmocked coverage of the lines directly above/below the ones this step added, and none of those tests assert on the DOM elements this step touches.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, 1440×900 unless noted): with nothing loaded, the bar is confirmed hidden (`display:none`, inherited from the untouched `renderMiniPlayer()` logic); after adding the test tone (`addTestTone()`, which calls `setQueue()` itself the moment nothing was already playing — no row click needed to make it "current"), the bar becomes visible, measures exactly 88px tall, and spans the full viewport width (left/right flush with the window, not capped to `.wrap`); the sidebar and `.wrap` still never overlap; `.wrap`'s own bottom padding (116px) clears the bar's 88px with room to spare.
