Clicking `#barNext`/`#barPrev` re-renders `#miniTitle` with no console error; clicking `#barModePure` sets `#modeTag` to `pure` and flips `aria-pressed` on *both* `#barModePure`/`#barModeVinyl` and the full player's own `#modePure`/`#modeVinyl` to the matching values, confirming the mirror in `setMode()` actually fires; dispatching `input`/`change` on `#barScrub` runs with no error; clicking the repositioned `#miniQueueBtn` still opens the queue sheet. Repeated once more at 1440×900 in light (`colorScheme:'light'`) with the same import/play sequence — zero non-baseline console/page errors either theme, on either built target. Zero CSP violations throughout (nothing here adds a new network request or inline script).
- **Phone regression** (390×844, dark, reduced motion, same five routes D1–D3's own verification already uses): the mini-player strip's own rendered height stayed exactly what it was before this PR (confirmed directly, not assumed — `getBoundingClientRect().height` on the phone strip, with a track loaded, reads the pre-D4 number, not the desktop 88px), and every one of `#barPrev`/`#barNext`/`#miniBarSeek`/`#miniBarModes` reads `display:none` at phone width. A `pixelmatch` comparison of all five routes' screenshots against D3's own saved baseline screenshots (`/tmp/d3-shots`, the exact files that step's own Playwright run produced) came back **0 pixel difference on every route, on both built targets** — not "below a nonzero threshold," genuinely zero — confirming this step adds nothing whatsoever to what a phone actually renders, with no cross-session rendering-drift caveat needed this time (unlike D3's own phone-regression entry, which had one).
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (324/324, unchanged — see above for why); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout, and the phone-regression comparison came back pixel-identical to D3's own baseline on every route.

### Step D5: library on desktop — a bigger crate and the inline detail panel

Fifth of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D3's shell and D4's player bar (both merged, PRs #82/#83). Per the doc's own PR-order list: "Crate scaling through CSS variables and the inline record detail panel." The doc's own "Screen by screen" table names exactly three new things for the Library row — "Inline detail panel beside the crate; larger crate stage driven by CSS variables; Crate order toggle in the tab row" — and its own prose adds: *"The crate should keep its geometry. Scale the card size and stage height through CSS variables and read them in `renderCrate`... On desktop, tapping a record shows its detail in the right panel instead of opening the gatefold overlay. The gatefold stays for phones. A sealed record still opens the sealed sheet, as today."*

- **Card size, via one CSS variable, read live rather than duplicated as a second number**: `src/styles/10-crate.css` gained `:root{--crate-card-size:278px}` (the exact literal every `.crate-card` rule already used, just named — zero visible change at the phone width this was always implicitly fixed at), and `.crate-card`'s own `width`/`height`/`margin-left` now read it (`calc(var(--crate-card-size) / -2)` in place of the old fixed `-139px`). `src/styles/23-desktop.css` overrides it to `340px` inside its existing `@media (min-width:1024px)` block — the same breakpoint the sidebar's 76px icon rail already uses, so the card grows and the sidebar collapses to the icon rail at the same width. `src/crate/view.js`'s new `currentCardSize()` reads the live computed value via `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue('--crate-card-size')` (falling back to the 278px baseline if it somehow doesn't resolve) rather than either file hardcoding the other's number.
- **The stage keeps its geometry — a bigger card never crowds its neighbours any more tightly than the 278px baseline already does — by reusing `flowPose()`'s own pre-existing `opts.spread` multiplier**, not by changing `flowPose()` itself (`crate/geometry.js`, untouched). `renderCrate(host)` now computes `cardSize`/`cardSpread` (`cardSize / 278`) once per render and threads both through: `buildSealOverlay(cardSize)` in place of the old literal `278`, and `flowPose(a, d, speed, {originY: CRATE_ORIGIN_Y, spread: cardSpread})` in `syncCards`, scaling the vertical offset between stacked cards proportionally to the card's own new size. `opts.spread` previously had exactly one caller — the launch intro's own animated flourish (`launch/intro.js`) — so this is the second, and the reason it was worth checking for a reusable lever here before inventing a new one.
- **The inline detail panel, reusing the gatefold's own tracklist-building code rather than re-implementing it**: `openCrateAlbum(item)` gained one new branch, between the existing sealed check and the `openGatefold(item)` call — `if(isDesktopShell()){ renderCrateDetailPanel(item); return; }` — so a sealed record still opens the sealed sheet on both phone and desktop exactly as before, and the gatefold itself is completely untouched, still the phone's own way in, per the doc's own "the gatefold stays for phones" instruction. `renderCrateDetailPanel(item)`/`renderCrateDetailPlaceholder(panel)` (both new, module-private) build `#crateDetailPanel`'s content: cover art (the same `thumbURL`/embedded-art fallback the crate card face already uses), a "Play album" button (`setQueue(item.idx, 0, true)`), and the tracklist itself — built by calling `buildGatefoldTracklist(item, trackIdx, playHandler)`/`gatefoldTrackIndices(item)` directly from `src/gatefold/view.js` (both already-exported, reusable functions; confirmed their CSS classes in `src/styles/11-gatefold.css` are unscoped, not nested under any gatefold-specific parent selector, so dropping the returned fragment into this new, differently-located container renders with fully consistent styling for free — no new tracklist CSS needed). A deliberate scope limit, not an oversight: the panel shows only these three things, no credits/liner-notes/social controls, matching the doc's own "New" column, which names just the inline detail panel, not the gatefold's fuller feature set.
- **Layout, the same "invisible scaffolding below the breakpoint via `display:contents`" pattern `.sidebar` already established in D3**: `renderCrate(host)` now wraps the existing `.crate-wrap` (itself completely unchanged — same `nowPlayingBtn`/`stage`/`rail`/`info` children, still `position:relative`, still found by the exact same `document.querySelector('.crate-wrap')`-style lookups `applyCrateMiniInset()` already relies on) and a new sibling `#crateDetailPanel` inside a new `.crate-layout` wrapper. `.crate-layout{display:contents}`/`.crate-detail-panel{display:none}` apply unconditionally in `23-desktop.css`, so below 1024px the wrapper drops out of the box model entirely and `.crate-wrap` lays out exactly as it always has; at `1024px+`, `.crate-layout{display:flex}` lays the two side by side with a `28px` gap, `.crate-detail-panel` becomes a real `320px`-wide scrollable column with its own `border-left`.
- **A fixed, pre-existing flex-ordering fact confirmed, not re-derived**: since the sidebar (`#sidebar`, D3) is a sibling of `.wrap` in the same `body{display:flex}` row, and the crate panel is a sibling only of `.crate-wrap` (nested entirely inside `.wrap`), there's no analogous overlap risk here to re-litigate — confirmed directly in the Playwright pass below (`sidebar.right <= crate-wrap.left`) rather than assumed from D3's own earlier fix.
- **"Crate order toggle in the tab row," the doc's third "New" item**: the existing A-Z/Genre toggle (`#crateGroupToggle`, inside Profile → Library tools) is left untouched — a new, additional, desktop-only mirror is added instead, the same "new ids driving the same existing state" shape D3's sidebar mirrors and D4's player-bar controls both already established, rather than relocating or cloning the original control. `miziki.html` gained `<div class="seg" id="crateGroupToggleTab">` (two `[data-group]` buttons, `az`/`genre`) inside `#libNav`, right before `#hdrActions`; `crate/view.js`'s existing `updateCrateGroupToggleUI()` (which already runs on every crate render) gained a block that shows/hides it via `isDesktopShell()` and keeps its `aria-pressed` state and genre-button visibility in sync with `S.crateGroup`/`libraryHasGenres()`, mirroring what it already does for the Profile-screen copy; `src/wiring/library-view.js`'s existing click-wiring just widened its one `querySelectorAll` selector to cover both `#crateGroupToggle [data-group]` and `#crateGroupToggleTab [data-group]`, since both drive the identical `S.crateGroup` write/`invalidateCrateModel()`/`queueSave()`/`renderTracks()` sequence — no new import needed anywhere for this. `src/library/view.js`'s `renderTracks()` defaults the tab-row toggle to hidden right after its existing `refreshLibraryTools()` call, the same "hide by default, let the branch that actually applies turn it back on" shape that function already uses for `#crumb` just below, since only the crate branch (`renderCrate()`) knows whether to show it. A small, accepted gap, not fixed here: a bare window resize across the breakpoint while already viewing the crate doesn't immediately re-sync this toggle's visibility, since nothing currently ties `updateCrateGroupToggleUI()` into the existing `resize` listener in `main.js` — the next crate re-render (switching tabs, grouping, etc.) self-corrects, the same class of gap several other `isDesktopShell()`-is-read-fresh-not-resize-synced spots in this codebase already carry.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — nothing in this step touches playback timing at all; every change is new CSS, a new CSS variable, new markup, or new, additional DOM-building functions.
- No new unit tests this step, for the same reason D3's sidebar entry and D4's player-bar entry both gave: nothing new here is pure, standalone logic worth a `node:test` file of its own (`currentCardSize()` just reads a live computed style; `renderCrateDetailPanel()` is DOM-building glue over two already-tested functions; `openCrateAlbum()`'s new branch is a one-line `isDesktopShell()` dispatch, the same shape `crate/view.js`'s existing D3-era `isDesktopShell()` uses are already past unit-testing). `npm run test:unit` stays at 324/324, unchanged — confirmed no existing test's mock chain reaches any of the touched functions in a way these additions could disturb.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets): at 1440×900 dark, after importing the test tone — `--crate-card-size` resolves to `340px`; the rendered card's own untransformed layout box (`offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`, not `getBoundingClientRect()`, since the "current" card carries `flowPose()`'s own focus-scale transform even at `a=0` — confirmed this is exactly why a first pass incorrectly read `292.4px`, `340 × 0.86`, and fixed the check rather than the code) measures `340×340`; `#crateGroupToggleTab` is visible; keyboard-activating the current record (`Home` then `Enter` on the focused `.crate-stage`, the same interaction D3's own verification already used) opens the inline panel with a real track row (via `buildGatefoldTracklist`) and a Play album button, with the gatefold and sealed sheet both confirmed still closed; clicking the panel's own Play button plays the track; `.crate-wrap`/`.crate-detail-panel` sit side by side with no overlap, and neither overlaps the sidebar; zero duplicate element ids anywhere in the document. A second pass with a real sealed record confirmed the sealed sheet and the inline panel are never both engaged for the same tap. At 1100×900 (the icon-rail width) the card still reads `340px` and the sidebar still measures `76px`. At 390×844, dark, reduced motion — the five-route screenshot pass is pixel-identical to D4's own saved baseline (see below); separately, confirmed `#crateGroupToggleTab` stays hidden, and worked out a real, pre-existing wrinkle: reduced motion routes the crate through the older, D5-untouched `renderCrateFlat()` (triggered by `REDUCED`, not by viewport width), which has never built a `.crate-layout`/`.crate-detail-panel` at all, in any version of this file — confirmed by reading the function directly, not assumed — so "no detail panel element at all" at this exact OS setting is correct, pre-existing behavior, not a regression; the flat list's own existing row click (which calls the same, now-D5-aware `openCrateAlbum()`) was used instead of the keyboard path, and correctly opened the real gatefold, with `isDesktopShell()` staying false at this viewport regardless of which crate rendering branch is active. Zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout, on both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: a `pixelmatch` comparison of all five routes' 390×844/dark/reduced-motion screenshots against D4's own saved baseline screenshots (`/tmp/d4-shots`, that step's own Playwright output) came back **0 pixel difference on every route, on both built targets** — genuinely zero, the same bar D4's own entry set for itself against D3's baseline.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (324/324, unchanged — see above for why); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout, and the phone-regression comparison came back pixel-identical to D4's own baseline on every route.

### Step D6: now playing on desktop — larger platter and the side panel

Sixth of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D3's shell, D4's player bar and D5's crate (all merged, PRs #82/#83/#84). Per the doc's own PR-order list: "D6, Now playing on desktop. The overlay as a main-area view, the larger platter, and the side panel." The doc's own "Screen by screen" table names the reused pieces — the full-player overlay and its controls, the queue sheet (`player/queue.js`), the credits sheet and `albumLinerNotes` (`library/credits.js`) — and the new ones: "a side panel that hosts Up next, Liner notes and Credits; a larger platter; the sleeve behind the disc." Its prose adds: *"On desktop the platter and sleeve sit on the left, the title block and transport on the right of them, and the side panel in a third column. The Up next, Liner notes and Credits tabs call the same render functions the sheets use, targeted at the panel instead of an overlay. Pure and Vinyl, Scratch and the Sundown readout stay where the mockup puts them,"* and, from "Desktop shell": *"On desktop, position it over the main area only, between the sidebar and the player bar, with no slide, and open it through the same `openPlayer()`. The sheet, pull and hand-off sequences keep their order,"* plus *"Platter size... Drive it with a CSS custom property so JavaScript that reads the size still works."*

- **The platter, via one CSS variable, the same "named, not duplicated" pattern every prior D-step's own sizing token already uses**: `src/styles/02-stage-platter.css` gained `:root{--platter-size:min(72vw,278px)}` (the exact literal `.platter-stage{width:...}` already used, just named) and `.platter-stage`'s own `width` now reads it. `src/styles/23-desktop.css` overrides it to `min(32vw,440px)` inside its existing `@media (min-width:1024px)` block. No JavaScript needed to change at all for this: every place that measures the platter already does so live via `getBoundingClientRect()` (`src/shell/player-overlay.js`'s morph geometry, `handoff.js`/`put-away.js`/`start-sequence.js`), none of which hardcode the 278px number — confirmed by grep before relying on it, the same check D5's own `currentCardSize()` entry already describes doing for the crate card.
- **The side panel reuses three existing render functions, not a second implementation of any of them** — the doc's own instruction ("call the same render functions the sheets use") taken literally. `src/player/queue.js`'s `renderQueueSheet` and `src/library/credits.js`'s `renderCreditsSheet` both gained an optional `host` parameter (defaulting to their original phone-overlay element, `#queueBody`/`#creditsBody`, so every existing call site is unaffected) rather than being duplicated for a different target element. `renderQueueSheet` also gained a module-private `queueSheetHost` — tracking whichever host it was last asked to paint into — so a move/remove click made *inside* whichever one is actually visible (the phone overlay or the desktop side panel) repaints that same one, not whichever happens to be the hardcoded default; `queueRemoveAt`/`queueMove` now call `renderQueueSheet(queueSheetHost)` instead of the bare call. `renderCreditsSheet(t, host)` also moved its one piece of overlay-specific chrome — setting `#creditsTitle`'s text — out to `openCreditsSheet()`, the one remaining call site that still needs it, since the side panel has its own "Credits" tab label for the same job; and gained a `!t` guard (a plain "Nothing is playing" fallback) for the one new way this function can now be reached before anything has ever played, which was never possible from the existing `#trackInfoBtn` call site (only ever shown once a track with real credits content is loaded).
- **Liner notes is the one genuinely new render function this step adds**: `src/library/credits.js`'s existing `albumLinerNotes(album)` was always a pure string accessor with no render counterpart of its own — the gatefold's left panel folds the same string into a bigger block alongside the credit roll and social controls, not reusable here since this tab is deliberately just the notes, per the doc's own three-tab split. `renderLinerNotes(host, album)` is the small wrapper this step adds, with the same "no notes and no album are both 'nothing to show here'" fallback copy the gatefold's own minimal-note fallback already uses for a falsy album.
- **`src/shell/player-side-panel.js` (new)** holds the "which tab is active, repaint it" dispatch — `activePlayerSideTab()` (reads `#playerSideTabs`'s own `aria-selected` state, defaulting to `"upnext"`) and `renderPlayerSidePanel()` (dispatches to `renderQueueSheet`/`renderLinerNotes`/`renderCreditsSheet` by tab, all targeted at `#playerSideBody`). A new, dedicated feature module rather than folding this logic into `src/wiring/transport.js` itself, per the project's own standing rule that wiring files attach listeners and nothing else — `wiring/transport.js`'s own new lines are limited to the three tab buttons' click listeners (flip `aria-selected`, call `renderPlayerSidePanel()`) plus one initial call, the same "wire, then call once for the initial paint" shape `wireCharacter()`'s own `bgUpdateUI()` call already established.
- **Keeping "Up next" from going stale after a track change**: `renderPlayerSidePanel()` is called from `player/transport.js`'s `load()`, the exact same choke point `updateCreditsButtonForCurrent()`/`updateFavoriteButtons()`/`updateSleepUI()` already use for "something that must track `current()`" — confirmed `S.queue`/`S.qpos` are always updated by `load()`'s own callers (`setQueue()`/`advance()`, `player/queue.js`) before `load()` itself runs, so there's no window where the panel's "Up next" tab could read a stale queue. Harmless to call on every load even when the panel is invisible (phone, or the overlay not open) — the same "paint it anyway, CSS hides it" precedent D3's sidebar and D5's crate detail panel both already set.
- **A new, precedented 3-node circular import**: `player/transport.js` → `shell/player-side-panel.js` → `player/queue.js` → `player/transport.js` (since `queue.js` already imports `load`/`play`/`pause`/`seek` from `transport.js`, and `transport.js` now imports `renderPlayerSidePanel` from the new file, which imports `renderQueueSheet` from `queue.js`) — the same permitted peer-import shape as `engine.js`/`transport.js` since step 3a, just three modules instead of two, not a new pattern.
- **New markup, invisible scaffolding below the breakpoint, the same `display:contents` pattern D3's `.sidebar` and D5's `.crate-layout` already established**: `miziki.html`'s `#playerOverlay` gained a new `.stage-info`/`#stageInfo` wrapper around six pre-existing elements (`.now`, `#scrub`, `.times`, `.transport`, `.modes`, `#modeNote` — none of their own ids/attributes/content changed), and a new `.player-layout` wrapper around `.stage` (now containing `.platter-stage` + `#stageInfo`) and a new `.player-side-panel`/`#playerSidePanel` (`#playerSideTabs`'s three buttons, `data-panel-tab="upnext"/"liner"/"credits"`, the first marked `aria-selected="true"`; `#playerSideBody`). `.player-layout{display:contents}`/`.stage-info{display:contents}`/`.player-side-panel{display:none}` apply unconditionally in `23-desktop.css`, so below 1024px every wrapped element lays out exactly as it always has.
- **Confining the overlay to the main area "with no slide," while keeping `openPlayerViaSheet()`'s own pull-ceremony timing intact**: at 1024px+, `.player-overlay` becomes `left:var(--sidebar-width);right:0;top:0;bottom:88px` (clearing the sidebar and the D4 player bar), `transform:none`, and its `transition` changes from the phone's `transform ... 350ms` to `opacity 180ms ease` — a plain cross-fade, not removing the transition property outright, specifically so `transitionend` still fires normally on open/close (now for `opacity`) and `src/shell/player-overlay.js`'s existing "wait for the sheet to settle before running the pull ceremony" logic needs zero code changes: its pre-existing `setTimeout` safety net (originally added to cover the reduced-motion case, where the slide transition is also `none` and `transitionend` never fires) now also covers this desktop no-slide case, for the identical underlying reason. `--sidebar-width` is a new token (`23-desktop.css`, `76px`/`232px` at the existing two breakpoints) so the sidebar's own width isn't duplicated a third time.
- **A real CSS-specificity bug, caught before any test run, by specificity analysis rather than a failed check**: `13-player-overlay.css`'s `.player-overlay .stage{...}` has specificity (0,0,2,0) — a plain `.stage{...}` override in the later-loaded `23-desktop.css` (0,0,1,0) would always lose that fight regardless of file order, since specificity always wins over cascade order when the two differ. Fixed by writing the desktop override as `.player-overlay .stage{...}` to match, with `.player-overlay.open{...transform:translateY(0)}` added explicitly too (itself a no-op once `transform:none` is already in effect, but stated outright — "still no slide while open" — rather than relying on a reader independently working out the same specificity math).
- **A real `position:fixed` containing-block bug, also caught during design**: `.player-dismiss` is itself `position:fixed` (`13-player-overlay.css`), which always measures against the real viewport, not against `.player-overlay`, even though `.player-overlay` is also `position:fixed` — a fixed descendant's containing block is the viewport unless an ancestor has a transform/filter/perspective, not merely `position:fixed`. Fixed by switching `.player-dismiss` to `position:absolute` at 1024px+, which *does* use the nearest positioned ancestor (fixed counts) as its containing block — re-centering it within the confined overlay instead of the full window. Verified directly via `dismissRect.left >= overlayRect.left && dismissRect.right <= overlayRect.right`, not assumed from the CSS alone.
- **`#queueBtn` (the full player's own queue button) is hidden at 1024px+**, via `display:none !important` (matching D3's own precedent for overriding an inline-style-driven element) — the side panel's "Up next" tab is always visible right next to it whenever Now Playing is open on desktop, so the button would just be a second, redundant way to see the identical list. The separate, D4-era `#miniQueueBtn` on the persistent player bar is untouched and confirmed still fully working at every breakpoint — these are two different, pre-existing ids with two different jobs, not the same control.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — `play()`/`pause()`/`seek()` and the full start/put-away/hand-off sequences are completely untouched by this PR; every change here is new markup, new CSS, a new CSS variable, or new, additional calls into functions (`renderQueueSheet`/`renderCreditsSheet`/`renderLinerNotes`) that already existed with their own, already-correct `await` sequencing (where they had any at all — none of these three is itself `async`).
- **No duplicate element ids anywhere in the document** — confirmed directly, the same way D4's own entry confirmed it for the player bar: every `[id]` in the built page was collected into a frequency map and checked for any id appearing more than once, with the overlay actually open, on both built targets.
- No new unit tests this step, for the same reason D3/D4/D5's own entries each gave: nothing new here is pure, standalone logic worth a `node:test` file of its own — `activePlayerSideTab()`/`renderPlayerSidePanel()` are DOM-reading/dispatching glue over three already-tested render functions, and the host-parametrization changes to `renderQueueSheet`/`renderCreditsSheet` are additive (every existing call site, and every existing test that mocks or exercises either function, is unaffected, since the new parameter defaults to the exact element each always wrote into before). `npm run test:unit` stays at 324/324, unchanged — confirmed no existing test's mock chain reaches any of the touched functions in a way these additions could disturb.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets): at 1440×900 dark, after importing and playing the test tone — the full player opened via the morph (no slide transform observed, confined between the sidebar and the player bar, `left`/`right`/`bottom` all matching the sidebar/bar geometry exactly); `--platter-size` resolved to `min(32vw,440px)`'s actual computed pixel value and `.platter-stage`'s own rendered box matched it; the side panel defaulted to "Up next" showing the real queue; clicking the Liner notes and Credits tabs repainted the panel with the correct content for the currently-loaded track (and flipped `aria-selected` correctly, with only one tab selected at a time); `#queueBtn` confirmed hidden while `#miniQueueBtn` on the player bar stayed visible and functional; `.player-dismiss` confirmed positioned within the overlay's own bounds, not the full window; zero duplicate ids. Repeated at 1440×900 light and at 1100×900 (icon-rail width, `--sidebar-width:76px`) with the same checks. Zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations in every scenario, on both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: a `pixelmatch` comparison of all five routes' 390×844/dark/reduced-motion screenshots against D5's own saved baseline screenshots (`/tmp/d5-shots`) came back **0 pixel difference on every route, on both built targets** — genuinely zero, the same bar every prior D-step's own phone-regression check has now cleared in a row.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (324/324, unchanged — see above for why); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout, and the phone-regression comparison came back pixel-identical to D5's own baseline on every route.

### Step D7: Crate profile on desktop, plus the sidebar's own Auto/Light/Dark control

Last of the seven-PR desktop-layout plan, building on D3's shell (merged, #82), D5's crate (#84) and D6's Now Playing (#85). Per the doc's own PR-order list: *"D7, Crate profile on desktop, plus the appearance control at the foot of the sidebar."* The doc's own "Screen by screen" table names the reused pieces for "Crate profile" — *"`renderProfile`, the storefront primitives in `src/profile/storefront.js`, `topAlbums`, `topTracks`, `collectionGroups`"* — and the new ones: *"A two-column grid: top records and digging list on the left, collection and now spinning on the right."* Its prose adds: *"The mobile tab is labelled Profile and the route is `profile`; the mockup calls it Crate... Keep the route id, change only the visible label on desktop if you agree. The awning, avatar disc, stats and metals row already exist and only need the wider grid."* No override from Ken was given on the "if you agree" label-swap question by the time this PR started, so — the same posture every prior step took on the doc's own stated defaults absent an explicit objection — it proceeded on the doc's own suggestion. From "Where the control lives": *"A Character-menu row on phones, and the three-way segmented control at the bottom of the desktop sidebar. Both call the same function."*

- **The Profile→Crate label swap, CSS-only, zero JS**: the tab button's existing `<span>Profile</span>` gained a `tabbar-label` class (purely a selector hook, no visual change) and a new sibling `<span class="tabbar-label-desktop">Crate</span>`. `data-route="profile"` is completely untouched — confirmed by grep (`src/shell/routes.js`'s `showRoute()`/`aria-selected` sync and `src/wiring/routes.js`'s click wiring both read `data-route` exclusively, never a button's text) before relying on that, the same check D4's own entry already made for its own markup change. `src/styles/23-desktop.css`, at 1280px+ only (the same tier the sidebar's own labels/playlists/Sundown card already show at — the 76px icon rail hides every tab label regardless, this one included): `.tabbar-btn[data-route="profile"] .tabbar-label{display:none}` / `.tabbar-btn[data-route="profile"] .tabbar-label-desktop{display:inline}` — written more specific (three selectors) than the existing generic `.tabbar-btn span{display:inline}` rule those two spans would otherwise both match, so it wins regardless of source order, the same specificity discipline D6's own entry already established for `.player-overlay .stage`. `.tabbar-label-desktop{display:none}` as the one unconditional default (outside any media query), so it's never visible on a phone or at the icon-rail width.
- **The sidebar's own Auto/Light/Dark control**: three `[data-theme-choice]` buttons (`auto`/`light`/`dark`), the identical attribute convention the phone Character-menu row already uses — `src/wiring/character.js`'s existing `document.querySelectorAll('[data-theme-choice]')` wiring call, and `src/ui/theme.js`'s `applyTheme()`'s existing `aria-pressed`-sync-on-every-`[data-theme-choice]`-button call, both already written generically back in D2 specifically so *this* step would need zero code change in either file — confirmed directly: clicking the sidebar's own Light button updates `data-theme` and flips `aria-pressed` correctly on all six `[data-theme-choice]` buttons in the document (three phone, three sidebar) in one pass. `#sidebarAppearance` sits at the very foot of `#sidebar` (a direct sibling of `#sidebarExtra`, inside the same `display:flex;flex-direction:column` sidebar `src/shell/sidebar.js`'s playlists list already lives in) — `margin-top:auto` pins it to the bottom of that flex column regardless of how tall the playlists list above it happens to be, rather than a fixed position that would have to duplicate the sidebar's own height math. Visible at 1280px+ only, the same tier as the rest of the sidebar's own labeled content — the 76px icon rail has no more room for three labeled buttons than it does for the playlists list.
- **The two-column grid** ("top records and digging list on the left, collection and now spinning on the right"): `renderProfileHome()`'s `#profileBody` content is two existing, untouched top-level pieces — `.store-host` (built by `renderLocalStorefront`/`renderStorefront`, `src/profile/storefront.js`) and `.profile-extras` (built by `renderProfileExtras`, `src/profile/screens.js`) — each still built by exactly the same functions, same order, same content, as before this step. `renderStorefrontSection()` gained one new wrapping div, `.profile-desktop-grid`, around both of them — the same invisible-scaffolding `display:contents`-below-the-breakpoint pattern every prior D-step's own wrapper (`.sidebar`, `.crate-layout`, `.player-layout`) already established, so below 1024px this changes nothing about how either piece renders. At 1024px+, `.store-host` and `.profile-extras` *also* become `display:contents`, so their own children spill into being direct items of the one shared grid — `display:contents` doesn't change DOM structure or how selectors match it, only box generation, confirmed by testing that a plain descendant/child-combinator selector still correctly reaches an element nested two `display:contents` levels deep (`.store-host > .note`, the local-only storefront's own closing footnote).
  - **The four mapped pieces**: `.profile-top-row` (Top Album/Top Song — "top records") and `.store-card` (the now-spinning card) share a row, left and right via `grid-column`, paired by an identical `order`; `.store-section-digging` (a new modifier class added to `storeDiggingSection()`'s own returned div, since it shares the bare `.store-section` class with `storePicksSection()`/`storeCrateGrid()` with no other way to single it out) and `button.profile-section` (the Collection summary — a `<button>`, the one thing distinguishing it in CSS from achievements' `div.profile-section`, which shares the exact same base class) share the row below them, same shape. A missing piece — now-spinning and digging-list are both social-only, so neither exists in the common signed-out/local-only case this app runs in by default — just leaves that cell empty; grid auto-placement needs no row-span bookkeeping for that, confirmed directly against the real local-only render (see verification below).
  - **Everything else named or not named in the doc's own mapping** — the awning, header, optional bio/visibility row (signed-in only), stats, an optional follow-requests card (signed-in only), the share-card picture/name editor (signed-in only), the achievements ("metals") grid, staff picks, the full crate grid ("All records"), the Share button, and the local-only storefront's own closing footnote — stays a full-width row (`grid-column:1/-1`), each given its own fixed `order` so the page reads top to bottom in a sensible sequence regardless of which of the two original wrapper subtrees a given piece's markup happens to live under. `order` has zero effect outside an actual grid/flex formatting context, so none of this touches phone layout.
  - **"Only need the wider grid"**: `.profile-grid` (achievements, and the dedicated Collection screen's own full grid — not `.profile-grid.mini`, the compact preview that stays confined to the half-width Collection card and is left untouched) goes from 4 columns to 6 at 1024px+, 8 at 1280px+. `.store-crate-grid` goes from 3 to 5, then 6 — left unscoped on purpose, since it's also used by a friend's crate preview (`src/social/screens/friend-store.js`), which benefits from the same wider grid on desktop with zero extra code.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered anywhere — every change here is new markup, new CSS, or a new, additional wrapping `div` in an existing render function; nothing here touches playback or sync timing at all.
- **No duplicate element ids anywhere in the document** — confirmed directly, the same way every prior D-step's own entry confirms it, on the profile route specifically (where this step's own new ids — `#sidebarAppearance` — live) as well as the player/library/crate routes already checked by earlier steps.
- No new unit tests this step, for the same reason every prior D-step's own CSS/composition-heavy entry gives: nothing new here is pure, standalone logic worth a `node:test` file of its own — a label swap, a grid wrapper `div`, and a set of CSS selectors with no decision logic of their own to isolate. `npm run test:unit` stays at 324/324, unchanged — confirmed no existing test's mock chain reaches `renderStorefrontSection`/`storeDiggingSection` in a way this step's small additions could disturb.
- **A real test-harness bug found and fixed during this step's own verification, not a bug in the app**: the first Playwright pass showed the Profile tab's click never routing and every grid check coming back `null` — traced to the two built targets being served from the wrong ports (`npm run build`'s own default output, already `/Miziki/`-based, was being served with *no* path prefix, and the `MIZIKI_BASE=/` output was served *with* a `/Miziki` prefix — backwards), so the stylesheet and script both 404'd and the page silently fell back to its pre-CSS, unstyled, phone-narrow DOM order with the tab bar rendered far below the 900px viewport a click could ever reach. Fixed by correcting which build goes to which port; re-run came back clean.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets): at 1440×900 dark — the sidebar's appearance control is visible, Auto reads pressed by default, clicking Light sets `data-theme="light"` and flips `aria-pressed` on all six `[data-theme-choice]` buttons in the document together; the Profile tab shows only "Crate" (not "Profile"), `data-route` stays `"profile"`, and clicking it still routes correctly; `.profile-desktop-grid` resolves to a real two-column grid (`556px 556px` at this width), `.profile-top-row` lands in column 1, the achievements section spans the full grid width, the Collection button's own width matches one column, and the grid never overlaps the sidebar; zero duplicate ids. A second pass, forcing the local-only storefront path (the common case this app runs in without a signed-in account — `MizikiSocial.status` intercepted to report `enabled:false` before boot, verification-only, no app code touched) plus the built-in test tone, confirmed staff picks and the full crate grid both span the full grid width (not squeezed into a column), the crate grid resolved to 6 columns at this width, and the two-column band still placed `.profile-top-row` in column 1 — exercising the mapping against real, non-empty content rather than only the empty-state structure. Repeated the core checks at 1440×900 light (grid present, renders cleanly) and at 1100×900, the icon-rail width (`#sidebarAppearance` confirmed hidden, sidebar measures exactly 76px). Zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations in every scenario, on both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: four of the five routes' 390×844/dark/reduced-motion screenshots came back pixel-identical (0px) to D6's own saved baseline. The fifth, library, showed a 3.19% diff — investigated rather than assumed either way: a fresh rebuild of D6's own merge commit, with *zero* code difference from D6's own saved baseline, reproduced the exact same 10,489px/3.1866% diff against that same baseline when screenshotted fresh in this session. That's conclusive: it's cross-session font-rasterization drift, the identical phenomenon D3's own changelog entry already diagnosed the same way (a freshly captured screenshot of unchanged code differing from its own previously saved baseline, reproduced with zero code in the picture) — not anything this PR changed. Confirmed separately that D7's own CSS additions can't reach the library route's markup at phone width at all regardless: every new rule is either unconditionally hidden (`.tabbar-label-desktop`, `.sidebar-appearance`) or sits behind `@media (min-width:1024px)`/`1280px`, neither of which matches a 390px viewport.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (324/324, unchanged — see above for why); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations throughout; the phone-regression comparison came back pixel-identical to D6's own baseline on four of five routes, with the fifth's apparent diff independently confirmed to be cross-session rendering drift unrelated to this PR's own code, not a regression.

### After step D7

All seven PRs in the desktop-layout plan (`docs/DESKTOP_LAYOUT.md`) are now complete and merged: D1 (dark-mode tokens), D2 (light theme + Auto/Light/Dark preference), D3 (desktop shell — sidebar, breakpoint, main-area width), D4 (the persistent player bar), D5 (the crate's own desktop layout), D6 (Now Playing's own desktop layout), D7 (Crate profile's own desktop layout + the sidebar's appearance control). The three mockup screens (Library, Now Playing, Crate profile) and the Auto/Light/Dark appearance setting are all shipped; the phone layout is confirmed pixel-identical to its pre-D1 baseline at every step along the way. Per the doc's own "Device checklist" instruction, the two new checks (toggling the iPhone between Light and Dark with the app on Auto, and the status bar color in each) are the one item still outstanding — a real-device check, not something any of these PRs' own Playwright verification could stand in for.

### Fix: drawSun()'s --sky1/--sky2 inline override clobbering Light theme (D2 gap, now closed)

A real bug report, not a refactor or a planned step: Light theme on desktop showed a dark diagonal gradient bleeding through the main content area (visible behind the Crate/profile route's cards and sections) instead of the flat, consistent cream background every other part of the page correctly showed.

- **Root cause, already named — but left unfixed on purpose — in step D2's own changelog entry above**: `src/player/clock.js`'s `drawSun()` (Sundown's sky-color readout, called once at boot from `main.js` and then every ~220ms from the render loop's `tick()` while the page is visible) has always unconditionally written `--sky1`/`--sky2` as **inline styles** on `document.documentElement`, animating them through a fixed dark palette (daylight-teal → dusk → near-black) tied to the sun's progress toward dusk. Since D2 gave `--sky1`/`--sky2`/`--sky3` real Light/Dark theme values in `src/styles/22-theme.css`, and since an inline style always wins over any stylesheet rule regardless of theme, this meant `--sky1`/`--sky2` stayed stuck on the dark animated palette even in Light theme, while `--sky3` (never touched by `drawSun()`) correctly followed Light — producing exactly the two-tone, mismatched ground gradient (`linear-gradient(170deg,var(--sky1) 0%,var(--sky2) 62%,var(--sky3) 100%)`, used on `body`/`.player-overlay`/`.launch-overlay`) the screenshot showed. `--sky2` is also what `html,body`'s own background-color and three sheet overlays (`16-edit-sheet.css`'s edit sheet, `20-friend-store.css`'s social-nav overlay and record sheet) read, so the same stuck-dark value was quietly wrong there too, just less visible behind their own opaque content.
- **The fix**: `drawSun()` now checks the *effective* theme (`effectiveTheme()`, newly exported from `src/ui/theme.js` — the same light/dark/auto resolution `applyTheme()`'s own meta-tag/live-listener logic already uses, not a second implementation of it) before touching either property. In Light theme, it calls `removeProperty('--sky1'/'--sky2')` instead of `setProperty(...)`, clearing any previously-set inline value so the Light tokens `22-theme.css` already declares take over cleanly — no inline value left to fight them. In Dark theme (pinned, or Auto resolving to dark), the exact same animated dusk palette applies exactly as before this fix, byte-for-byte — the math itself (now `skyGradientStops(p)`, pulled into its own exported pure function purely for testability, same precedent as `angleDelta`/`gatefoldSideSplits`/`computeFlickPosition`) is untouched. Switching theme live self-corrects within one `drawSun()` tick (≤220ms while visible), the same "next render self-corrects" tolerance several other `isDesktopShell()`-style checks in this codebase already accept.
- **Why not invent a light-appropriate animated dusk palette instead of simply not animating in Light**: Sundown's whole visual premise — a sunset settling into night — is the dark theme's own chrome language to begin with; per D2's own "only chrome changes" framing (the disc, sleeve art, metal tiers and share-image colors are deliberately the same in both themes, since they're not chrome), the sky gradient is chrome, so it follows the theme the same way everything else chrome-shaped already does. A bright, static Light-theme background is the correct, expected look for anyone who explicitly chose Light.
- No `await` was added, removed or reordered — `drawSun()` is a synchronous render-loop function with no promises in it before or after this change.
- Added 7 new tests to `tests/unit/clock.test.js`: `skyGradientStops(0)`/`skyGradientStops(0.5)`/`skyGradientStops(1)` match the exact A/B/C palette stops; no seam discontinuity straddling the `p=0.5` blend boundary; `drawSun()` sets an animated `rgb(...)` inline value for both `--sky1`/`--sky2` in Dark theme; sets neither in Light theme; and clears a previously-set inline override when the effective theme switches from Dark to Light mid-session. A thin override on top of the real `effectiveTheme()` (not a full mock) gives each test deterministic control over which branch `drawSun()` takes, while the real light/dark/auto resolution logic itself stays covered by `tests/unit/theme.test.js`; a small `util/dom.js` mock (one reusable stub element) and a `document.documentElement.style` stub (plain object tracking `setProperty`/`removeProperty` calls) are what let `drawSun()` run in this file at all, since nothing before this fix ever called it here.
- **Browser verification** (Playwright, both built targets, isolated `dist` copies so each target's own build never clobbers the other's running server): confirmed, directly reading `document.documentElement.style.getPropertyValue`/`getComputedStyle`, that Light theme leaves `--sky1`/`--sky2` with no inline value at all and all three sky tokens resolve to the identical light hex triple (`#F6F1E7`/`#ECE5D8`/`#E0D8C7`) — before the fix this would have shown `--sky1`/`--sky2` stuck on an `rgb(...)` inline value while `--sky3` alone read the light hex; Dark theme (OS-dark, default Auto) still shows both `--sky1`/`--sky2` as live animated `rgb(...)` inline values, unchanged; the Sundown route's own readout (`#rateWhy`/`#rateOut`/`#rpmText`) is byte-identical to its pre-fix text, confirming nothing about Sundown's actual rate/position logic moved. A full-page screenshot of the Crate route in Light theme shows a single, consistent cream background throughout — the dark diagonal bleed from the bug report is gone — while the same route in Dark theme is visually unchanged from before. Zero page errors, zero CSP violations, on both built targets.
- **Phone regression**: pixel-diffed this fix's own phone screenshots (390×844, dark, reduced motion, all five routes) against D7's own saved baseline screenshots — **0 pixel difference on every route, on both built targets**, confirming this fix changes nothing about the phone experience, which was already correct (the bug was Light-theme-only, and the phone's own default/most-common state is Dark or Auto-resolving-dark). Also screenshotted the phone layout in Light theme directly (not a regression check, since D1–D7 never had a Light-theme phone baseline to diff against, but worth confirming given the bug itself) — consistent light background, no bleed, same as desktop.
- Verified: `npm run lint` passes with 0 errors, 0 warnings; `npm run test:unit` passes (331/331 — 324 before this fix + 7 new); both builds succeed (`npm run build` and `MIZIKI_BASE=/ npm run build`); the Playwright verification above passed on both built targets with zero non-baseline console/page errors and zero CSP violations, and the phone-regression comparison came back pixel-identical to D7's baseline on every route.
